**Malformed records.** A record that passes every stop check and the chain check, but whose body does not decode under §7.1, §12.2 and §12.3 (an unknown kind, an op that its kind does not allow, an op out of §12.3's order or running past the record's end, bytes left after its last op, or a field that those sections fix set otherwise, such as a `SegmentEnd`'s `n` or a `Chunk`'s `commit_ts`), is detected damage, never an end of the WAL, as a splice is. A correct writer writes only records that decode (card A-6a's encoder refuses any other), so such a record was written by a faulty writer or is a CRC collision, and the WAL may go on past it with acked commits. Pass 1 decodes every record it accepts, kind and ops, so the check is made before pass 2. A read-write open fails with **XX001** (detail: malformed record, with its LSN and what did not decode) before pass 2, and writes nothing; a read-only open serves salvage cut at the record's start (§14 step 3a, §14.1). Mutant **WAL-MALFORMED-AS-END**: such a record ends the walk cleanly, as a stop, and recovery truncates there.
- Test `t_wal_malformed` (A-6b, A-7d; §15.1). At A-6b, on crafted streams: after accepted records, a record whose magic, `len`, CRC, `lsn`, boot id and back-pointer all pass, but whose kind is 0, whose op tag is unknown, whose op runs past its end, whose last op leaves a byte over, which is a `SegmentEnd` with `n` 1, or which is a `Chunk` with a nonzero `commit_ts`: each gives damage at its start, never a stop, and the walk's visitor never sees it. At A-7d, the test writes such a record, of the same length, over one synced record between acked commits (forged through `Env`), past `wal_ckpt_end`, and in a second run below it: the read-write open fails with XX001 before pass 2 and leaves every file byte-identical, and the read-only open serves salvage cut at the record, with every acked commit below it. WAL-MALFORMED-AS-END fails the first run: the read-write open returns `Ok`, and its step 5 truncates the acked commits behind the record.

**Splices (I-WAL-CHAIN).** A record that passes every stop check above (magic and CRC, `lsn`, `len`, and the ordered boot id) must also have a `prev_lsn` and a `prev_crc` (§12.2) equal to the start and the `crc` field of the record the walk accepted just before it. Across a hop, that is the `SegmentEnd`, or the record that ended exactly at its segment's end. For the walk's first record, they must equal the opened meta's `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc` (§2.2). Both are compared, and the chain is checked last, after every stop check. A record that passes the stop checks but fails this one, in either field, is a **splice**, never an end of the WAL. The walk ends there, the position is not a valid end, and recovery treats it as detected damage (§14 step 3a). Every stop check keeps its meaning: a record that fails one of them ends the walk cleanly at a valid end, as before, whatever its `prev_lsn`.
- *Why a mismatch is damage, not a crash.* Without post-sync damage, every record the walk accepts is one its writer appended at that position, right after the record the walk accepted before it:
  - an ordinary crash loses or tears only bytes that no completed sync covered, and the walk stops at the first of them (above), so it never compares a record behind a loss;
  - a boot's first record names the record its own recovery accepted last, or the opened meta's `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc` if it accepted none (§12.2). A later walk that reaches that record has accepted the same records before it, byte for byte, whether it runs from the same meta or from an older one (above): it passes no lost or torn byte (previous bullet), and any record a later boot wrote over them is stopped by the boot-id order (next bullet). At every meta, `wal_start_lsn` is a record start of that meta's own boot or its append position (§12.6), and the record there was written, or will be, with `prev_lsn = wal_start_prev` and `prev_crc = wal_start_prev_crc`;
  - a stale record of an older boot is stopped by the boot-id order before this check. That needs boot ids that increase across the boots one walk can reach (*Why the boot id*, above; §14 step 8). It holds outside the Residual, where a mismatch is a true detection.

  So a record that passes every stop check but names another predecessor (another start, or another `crc`) shows that the bytes before it are not the ones its writer appended after: a synced write was lost, reverted or misdirected, or a region came back from another history, and the WAL went on past the damage.
- *Unlike PostgreSQL,* which ends redo at a record whose `xl_prev` is wrong, the store fails closed. Here the stop checks alone define the end, so a record behind a splice is positive evidence that the WAL went on past it, and detected damage is never silent (PLAN-v3 §10 items 8 and 9).
- *What it does not catch:*
  - a lost tail: nothing follows to disagree;
  - damage the walk stops at: the records behind a stop are never read, so a stop stays a clean end. A forward scan past it would need a durable-end witness per batch, because after an ordinary crash, unsynced later batches can persist past a torn earlier one (§19 T4);
  - a spliced-in record whose own predecessor is byte-identical, at the same position, to the record the walk accepted before it: for example, byte-identical records from two boots that share an id and both appended from the same valid end (the Residual, above). Then the two histories agree up to the spliced-in record. A `crc` collision between differing predecessors at the same position also escapes, with probability about 2^-32 per splice. A position alone would also miss records of equal length that differ in content, which `prev_crc` catches (`t_wal_splice`, variant *same-start*). The chain is transitive: a record's `crc` covers its `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, so two records with equal `crc` at the same position name, up to a collision, the same predecessor, and so on back. A walk the chain accepts is therefore, up to collisions, one history as its writers appended it, and a splice that escapes the check agrees byte for byte with the walk up to the spliced-in record.

  It adds no recovery.
- *Cost.* 12 bytes per record, 12 per meta, and two comparisons per record; and record CRCs are finished in LSN order (§12.2).
- *Status.* The E4 prototype (`spikes/e4-recovery`) omits back-pointers, so E4's oracle and its ruling X5-R33 stand as written. A prototype that adds them must widen X5-R33's explained-`Err` rule to accept a splice `Err` whose fault lies anywhere in the walked span.
- Mutants:
  - **PREV-CHECK-SKIP**: the check is removed;
  - **PREV-CRC-UNCHECKED**: the check compares `prev_lsn` only, a position-only chain;
  - **PREV-MISMATCH-AS-END**: a splice ends the walk cleanly, as a stop, and recovery truncates there;
  - **PREV-FIRST-WRONG**: a boot's first record carries its own LSN or all-ones instead of its recovery's last accepted record, or a `prev_crc` other than that record's `crc`;
  - **PREV-HOP-WRONG**: a segment's first record names something other than the `SegmentEnd` or exact-end record before it;
  - **WAL-START-PREV-WRONG**: `wal_start_prev` is the append position's predecessor even when an open chunked transaction holds `wal_start_lsn` back (§12.6);
  - **WAL-START-PREV-CRC-WRONG**: `wal_start_prev_crc` is not the `crc` of the record `wal_start_prev` names (for example, the `crc` of the record at `wal_start_lsn` itself, or of the append position's predecessor when `wal_start_lsn` is held back);
  - **WAL-START-PAIR-SPLIT-READ**: step 0 reads the chain head in a later entry to the append critical section than the append position and the open-chunk set (§13 step 0), so a new chunked transaction's first `Chunk` reserved between them, at the append position, which is then `wal_start_lsn`, makes the pair name that chunk itself. Or step 0 reads the chain head in an earlier entry than the append position and the open-chunk set. Then a `Commit`, `CommitMarker` or `Chunk` reserved between the two reads holds `wal_start_lsn` at or below its own LSN (a first `Chunk` through the set read with the position), so the pair, the chain head read first or the own `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc` of the record at `wal_start_lsn`, is correct, except on a build that reserves a hop's `SegmentEnd` in an entry of its own (§12.4): there a new chunked transaction's first `Chunk` that hops, with its `SegmentEnd`'s entry between the two reads and its own entry after them, can leave `wal_start_lsn` at the next segment's first LSN while the pair names the record before that `SegmentEnd`, which the record there does not name. On a build that reserves each hop in one entry, this order is equivalent;
  - **BOOT-PAIR-UNSET**: a boot checkpoint records all-ones and 0 as its append position's pair, because its boot has appended nothing, instead of the recovery's chain head (§12.2);
  - **SEAL-STALE-HEAD**: the writer seals a batch's first record with a chain head read before the previous batch's last record was sealed (§12.2).
- Tests (A-6b, A-7d; §15.1 states which card runs which form):
  - `t_wal_splice`: a synced region in the middle of the replay span is overwritten through `Env` with records that pass magic, CRC, `lsn` and boot-id order, and that end at the start of an intact later record whose `prev_lsn` names a different predecessor. The open must fail with XX001 and leave every file byte-identical. There are two placements: the splice below `wal_ckpt_end`, and at or above it. PREV-CHECK-SKIP replays the spliced records. PREV-MISMATCH-AS-END opens and drops the intact acked commits behind the splice. Variant *read-only*: the open serves salvage cut before the splice (§14.1), and SPLICE-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT fails it. In each read-only form the overwritten region begins at `m` (§14 step 3a), so that the cut keeps no overwriting record and the records below it are the original history's: an overwriting record that ends at or before `m` would be kept, since no record tells it from an original one. It also runs crossed with the *same-start* variant below, with one record overwritten, the walk's predecessor, a `Commit` with data that the cut must exclude, since the cut is that predecessor itself (§14 step 3a); SPLICE-CUT-SAME-START-KEEP replays it. And it runs with a cut between a chunked transaction's chunks and its marker, and a `Commit` of a lower ts between the cut and the marker: at every admitted `S` the salvage state is the model's at `S` over the records below the cut, without the chunked transaction; SPLICE-MARKER-PAST-CUT applies its chunks and passes the lower-ts commit. Variant *same-id residual* (classes MW and MP): the fixture of the Residual, with the stale record's predecessor starting at a different LSN from the walk's. It must give XX001; PREV-CHECK-SKIP accepts it. Variant *same-start* (the case a position-only chain misses): the overwriting records have the same lengths as the records they replace but differ in content, so the intact record behind them names its predecessor's start correctly but not its `crc`; and the Residual's fixture with the two same-id boots appending records of equal length, differing in content, from the same valid end, so the stale record's predecessor starts where the walk's does. Both must give XX001; PREV-CRC-UNCHECKED accepts both.
  - `t_wal_chain_crash` (false-positive guard): ordinary power cuts, with no media fault, at every step around:
    - a `SegmentEnd` hop and an exact-end hop;
    - a boot's truncation and its first append, both when its walk accepted records and when it accepted none;
    - a flip while an open chunked transaction holds `wal_start_lsn` back;
    - a flip whose step 0 races a `Chunk` reservation: with no `Commit` or `CommitMarker` above `T` and the open-chunk set empty, a hook reserves a new chunked transaction's first `Chunk` after step 0's read of the append position and the open-chunk set and before its read of the chain head, wherever an implementation separates them, and otherwise just before and just after step 0's one entry (§13 step 0); on a build that reserves a hop's `SegmentEnd` in an entry of its own, the hook also makes that `Chunk` hop, with its `SegmentEnd`'s entry between step 0's read of the chain head and its read of the append position and the open-chunk set wherever an implementation reads the chain head first, and otherwise just before step 0's one entry, and with the `Chunk`'s own entry after them; the hook reserves through the store's append from a thread that holds no gate (§12.4);
    - two or more batches in flight, batch 2 written and synced before batch 1 is written, as in `t_ack_prefix` (§12.4), with cuts after both are synced as well; the test orders the writes and syncs, and the writer still seals in LSN order (§12.2);
    - a process crash, with no power cut, then a power cut after the next boot's first ack, the crashed boot's records crossing a `SegmentEnd` hop and an exact-end hop before the crash;
    - the walks of `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot` and of both variants of `t_boot_id_after_rollback`.

    None may give a splice error. PREV-FIRST-WRONG, PREV-HOP-WRONG, WAL-START-PREV-WRONG, WAL-START-PREV-CRC-WRONG, WAL-START-PAIR-SPLIT-READ, BOOT-PAIR-UNSET and SEAL-STALE-HEAD fail it. Its rollback walks rely on step 5's durable truncation (I-BOOT-DURABLE): without it, a cut that tears a boot's first record can bring that boot's later records back under the next boot's id (Residual, above), and the chain reports them, truly, as a splice.

### 12.6 Replay start and recycling
At the flip of checkpoint `k`, `wal_start_lsn` is the minimum of:
- the LSN of the first `Commit` or `CommitMarker` with `commit_ts > T`, or the append position if there is none;
- the first `Chunk` LSN of every chunked transaction whose marker **is not yet below `T`** at the flip. That is, it has written chunks, and either it has no `CommitMarker` yet, or its marker has `commit_ts > T`.

**When the terms are taken** (card S-GAMMA). "At the flip" is step 0's one entry to the append critical section (§13 step 0), with the `T` that step 0 has taken. The first term is taken over the `Commit` and `CommitMarker` records reserved before that entry, or is the append position that entry reads; the second over the **open-chunk set** as that entry reads it. The append critical section keeps the set (§12.4): the entry that reserves a chunked transaction's first `Chunk` adds the transaction with that `Chunk`'s LSN, and the entry that reserves its `CommitMarker` records the marker's commit_ts. A transaction leaves the set only once it has ended with no marker (below), or once a flip's entry has read it there with a marker whose commit_ts is at or below that flip's `T`; that entry drops it. One that ends with no marker leaves in an entry of its own as it ends, and a flip whose entry comes before that one still counts it, which is safe. The set is empty at open: no transaction starts before the boot checkpoint's meta is durable (C-T0 v8 §2.5), so no recovered transaction is ever a member. So every first `Chunk` reserved before the entry is in the set the entry reads, unless its transaction has ended with no marker or has its marker at or below `T`, and every first `Chunk` reserved after it lies at or above the append position the entry reads: `wal_start_lsn` is at or below every `Chunk` of every transaction whose marker is not below `T`.
- *Why one entry* (`verdicts/S-BETA.md` at 5cb70935, implementer item 4). Step 0 holds the epoch gate and the I-WAL-TS-ORDER critical section, and a `Chunk` reservation needs neither: it is not a gated operation (§12.4), and it need not be made inside that section (§13 step 0). A `Chunk` reserved by a caller that holds the gate, or the I-WAL-TS-ORDER section, cannot fall inside step 0's hold of it, but one reserved by a caller that holds neither can, since the store's append takes neither for a `Chunk` (§12.4). If step 0 read the set in an earlier entry than the append position, or a first `Chunk` entered the set in a later entry than its reservation, a first `Chunk` reserved between the two would lie below `wal_start_lsn` and be counted by neither term. Its transaction can still commit above `T` and be acked. A crash before the next checkpoint's meta then replays it from `wal_start_lsn` on, without that chunk, and nothing compares `nchunks`, so an acked commit is replayed in part, silently and not atomically (mutant START-LSN-SPLIT-READ, §14).

**Why the marked case counts.** A chunked transaction `c` can have its chunks at LSN 100–200 and its marker at LSN 400, with a small commit `≤ T` at LSN 300 between them. If `c`'s apply had not finished at the flip, then `T < c`. The first term alone gives 400, so pass 2 (§14) would never see the chunks, and step 6 could recycle them. `c` would be lost although acked. Counting `c`'s first chunk puts the start at 100.

A transaction that aborted before the flip does not count, and neither does one whose marker has `commit_ts ≤ T`, which is wholly in the image. **Nor does a transaction that committed with no ts** before the flip (C-T0 v8 `Released(⊥)`, §3.1 step 0: a large transaction whose `ROLLBACK TO` left it no data). It writes no marker, ever, so recovery ignores its chunks exactly as it ignores an aborted transaction's, and nothing durable depends on them. An aborted transaction, or one that committed with no ts, still counts at a flip whose entry precedes the one in which it leaves the open-chunk set (above), which only retains more. After a crash, an open transaction's chunks are ignored, because no marker follows them. **A segment wholly below the `wal_start_lsn` of the meta before the durable meta may be recycled** (§13 step 6, I-TWO-GEN), lowest first (§12.1). This is PLAN-v3's chunk rule ("segments holding chunks of a txn whose marker isn't yet below T are never recycled"), stated as a replay-start position.

### 12.7 Large transactions
A transaction is chunked **iff** it is large (C-T0 v8 §2.1). Its `Chunk` records are written before commit, as it materialises its writes (C-T0 v8 §3.2), and the commit is the `CommitMarker`, written in ts order (I-WAL-TS-ORDER).
- **Supersede.** Within one transaction's chunks, a later op on the same `(tree_id, key)` **supersedes** the earlier one. That happens on a rewrite of the key and on `ROLLBACK TO`. A `Delete{revert}` cancels the key altogether. Replay applies the last op per key (§14). C-T0 v8 I-PROV-WAL keeps every provisional entry equal to its key's last op.
- **No apply at commit.** A large transaction's data becomes readable at its flip, through its provisional entries, and its finalize converts them into ordinary entries. Until then the apply frontier stays below its commit ts (C-T0 v8 §3.5), so a crash replays it from these chunks.

### 12.8 Bulk loads (the rule PLAN-v3 §3.3 asks S-1 to pick)
**S-1 picks: force a checkpoint before the result is published. Page images are not WAL-logged.**
1. **Unlogged writes.** A bulk load (the sorted bulk load of an online index build, C-T6 §7) may write entries **without WAL records**, only into a tree that is **not yet published**: no reader may use it. The tree itself is created by a logged `CreateTree`. Concurrent logged writes into the tree, such as C-T6's write-only maintenance, stay logged as usual.
2. **The forced checkpoint.** The loaded entries become durable only through a checkpoint whose flip follows the load's last unlogged write.
3. **Publication.** The publishing commit (C-T6 write-only → public) may be issued only after that checkpoint's meta is durable (§13 step 5). It carries `RequireCkpt{id}` (§12.3), where `id` is the `ckpt_id` that the forced checkpoint returns once its step 5 is done: never a WAL position or a ts, because a load writes no WAL, so a checkpoint flipped during the load can share the forced checkpoint's `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi` (I-CKPT-DEP). A chunked publishing transaction carries the op in a `Chunk` written after that meta is durable. Catch-up from the WAL (PLAN-v3 §3.5 step 3) is ordinary logged writing.
4. **Crash before publication.** The recovered tree holds only its logged content plus whatever the last image caught of the load. S-3 must treat such a tree as an unfinished build: restart or discard it.

**Pages (I-LINK-ATOMIC).** §13's Exception exempts a load's *entries* from I-CKPT-AT-T, not its pages from I-LINK-ATOMIC (§5.6). Each load step links what it maps, so a discarded build (item 4) can be walked (§6.8) and leaks nothing. Mutant **LOAD-PAGE-SPLIT**: a page mapped one gated step before its parent's pointer. Test `t_load_every_cut` (B-4d, §15 I-LINK-ATOMIC).

Mutant: PUBLISH-BEFORE-META.

**The dependency (I-CKPT-DEP).** A commit that makes visible what only a checkpoint made durable names that checkpoint; in 2.0 only item 3's publication does. Recovery refuses to replay such a commit over an image older than the checkpoint it names (§14 step 3b): that image lacks the load's unlogged entries, and nothing in the WAL restores them, so the replay would publish a tree that silently misses entries (E4's first silent-loss path, `header-design-verify.md` §1.1–§1.3). Its over-refusals are deliberate and fail closed: an open from an image older than the forced one refuses even when the tree was dropped after its publication, or written to after it. Mutants: **DEP-BY-POSITION**: the publication names, and recovery compares, the forced checkpoint's `wal_ckpt_end` or `ts_ckpt_hi` instead of its `ckpt_id`. **DEP-ID-STALE**: the forced checkpoint returns the `ckpt_id` of the meta that was durable before its own step 5. The other mutants and the test are §14 step 3b's.

**Why not page images.** Under (b), a load writes versions at a registered snapshot ts `S`. Such versions may be `≤ T` of a later checkpoint, so a replay filtered by `commit_ts > T` (§14) could never place a logged copy of them. Page images would also carry LPIDs, which are meaningless to logical consumers (N14's replicas and CDC).

**TRUNCATE is not an unlogged load.** PLAN-v3 §3.3 lists TRUNCATE with bulk loads, but nothing in it bypasses the WAL, so it needs no forced checkpoint. It runs as three logged steps:
1. a `CreateTree` of a new, empty tree;
2. the catalog swap (`Ddl`) to the new tree, in the same commit;
3. a `DropTree` of the old tree, run when `W` passes the DDL (§6.8).

**Consequence, owned by D-6.** Unlogged loads are invisible to the WAL stream. Backup and PITR must therefore treat the forced checkpoint's image as a base-image boundary, and replicas must receive that image or rebuild the index (§19 Q5).

---

## 13. Checkpoint (the step order is binding)

At most one checkpoint runs at a time. Its triggers (WAL bytes, dirty volume, time, or a forced checkpoint for a bulk load) are configuration; correctness does not depend on them.

**Step 0 (flip).**
1. Take the epoch gate exclusively. No gated operation is now in flight (§5.6). This generalises PLAN-v3's "pause commit-apply" to every multi-page change.
2. Take `T` := the apply frontier, record `gc_w := gc_w_used` (§10.5) and the `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6) with its `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc` (§2.2). Step 0 fixes which record the pair names: for a record at the chosen position, the one its own `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc` name; for the append position, the chain head (§12.2), which a boot checkpoint, before its boot's first append, takes from the recovery. The append position, the chain head and the open-chunk set are read in one entry to the append critical section (§12.4), the same read that gives `wal_ckpt_end` below, and both of §12.6's terms are taken at that entry, with the `T` taken above. So no reservation, a `Chunk`'s included, falls between the position and the record the pair names (mutant WAL-START-PAIR-SPLIT-READ, §12.5), and no first `Chunk` falls between the position and the set (mutant START-LSN-SPLIT-READ, §14). The entry drops from the set every transaction whose marker's commit_ts is at or below `T` (§12.6). The pair's `crc` is taken once it is final, at the latest after step 1 has written its record, and before step 4 (§2.2). Inside the commit path's I-WAL-TS-ORDER critical section (§12.4), read `wal_ckpt_end` := the append position and `ts_ckpt_hi` := the commit_ts of the last `Commit` or `CommitMarker` appended, or the frontier this boot started from if none was. The two values are read together, in one entry to that critical section, so they describe the same point of the stream. `Chunk` reservations need not be made inside the critical section, and the store's append does not take it for one (§12.4): a chunk appended concurrently with the read may fall either side of `wal_ckpt_end`, and its provisional entry is dead after recovery (C-T0 v8 §2.4). Every `Commit` and `CommitMarker` reservation is inside it, and that is all the bound below needs. Record the boot id, record the map's root and its level as image `k`'s (§4.1), open image `k`'s image-record list (step 2; card S-MU), and set `e := k+1`.

   Every ordinary entry in image `k` was inserted by an apply that started before the flip and after its record was appended, so its record ends at or below `wal_ckpt_end` and its ts is at most `ts_ckpt_hi`. A record appended after the read has no entry in image `k`: its apply inserts are gated operations, and the gate is held exclusively. The unlogged entries of a §12.8 bulk load are not apply inserts. Their ts is the load ts, which is the fence commit's ts `f` (C-T6-rehost §9.1 step 4.2), and `f`'s `Commit` record was appended before the load began, so before the flip: it too ends at or below `wal_ckpt_end`, and `f ≤ ts_ckpt_hi`.
3. Release the gate.

**Step 1 (WAL).** `sync_data` the WAL through `wal_ckpt_end`: the step ends only when `durable_end ≥ wal_ckpt_end` (I-ACK-PREFIX, §12.4), so every byte below it is written and synced, not just the last batch's. It syncs every segment that holds a byte in `[durable_end, wal_ckpt_end)`, whether or not this boot wrote that byte: at a boot checkpoint `durable_end` starts at the recovery walk's start (§12.4, "After recovery"), so step 1 makes every byte the walk accepted durable before the boot meta is written (I-BOOT-DURABLE). This is stricter than PLAN-v3's "through the last record with commit_ts ≤ T", and the reason is out-of-order apply:
- applies run concurrently and finish out of ts order (`e1-mvcc/src/common/commit.rs:35–63`: apply, then `mark_applied` in ts order);
- so the image may hold some entries of a commit `c > T` that started applying before the flip;
- with `synchronous_commit=off`, `c`'s record may not yet be synced;
- syncing through `wal_ckpt_end` guarantees `c`'s record is durable before the meta, so recovery replays `c` wholly (I-CKPT-AT-T, I-REPLAY-IDEM). Without it, a crash could expose part of `c` with no record to complete it (mutant WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY).

Mutant **CKPT-SYNC-OVER-HOLE**: step 1 ends when the latest batch's sync completes while an earlier batch is unwritten. Test `t_ckpt_sync_prefix` (A-7c; the open's verdict A-7f, §15.1): a flip with batch 1 unwritten and batch 2 synced, then a crash after step 5. The test needs no media fault: under the mutant, a class C open fails I-WAL-COVER with XX001.

**The start segment** (I-WAL-START). Before step 4, the segment that holds `wal_start_lsn` exists durably, with its preallocated length: step 1 has made a record in it durable, whose writer made the segment's creation or rename and its length durable before that record (§12.5's writer rule), or, when `wal_start_lsn` is the append position at a segment's first LSN with nothing written there yet, step 1 first makes that segment's creation, or its recycling rename, and its preallocated length durable, as §12.5's writer rule requires before an append. Mutant **START-SEG-NOT-ENSURED**: step 1 does not; a power cut after step 5 then leaves a healthy store whose open finds its start segment missing, or short at `wal_start_lsn`, and fails with XX001.

**Step 2 (image).** The checkpointer writes image `k`, each page to a fresh slot (§4.4). Only step 2 writes the image map, the epoch-`k` map pages. Every write of epoch-`k` content made after the flip outside the map phase, by step 2's data phase, by a claim (§5.6) or by an eviction (§5.2), appends `(page, slot, crc)` to the **image-record list** before it releases the written frame's latch; a page is an LPID, or a map page's position in image `k`'s radix, counted from the leaf level and keyed apart from LPIDs, so that no record for a map page can collide with one for an LPID and a radix level added in epoch `k+1` (§4.1 "Growth") changes no record's meaning. "The image map" in §5.2 and §5.6 means image `k`'s map as the map phase builds it: each MapLeaf from its epoch-`k` content with the list's records for its entries applied, and each MapInner from its children's image refs (§4.1), never from a MapInner's epoch-`k` content. So it references no MapLeaf's eviction home (§4.4), and the epoch-`k` content of a MapLeaf whose content epoch is `k` is at no slot it references until the map phase has taken that content (card S-MU). The list lives in memory outside the pool, reserved at open (§5.7), under a leaf lock: under it a thread only appends or reads records, with no I/O and no other lock taken. A crash before meta `k` loses the list together with image `k`, which no open uses, and every slot it names was allocated after meta `k−1`, so it is free in image `k−1` (I-FREE-EXACT). Meta `k` is written only after the map phase has applied the list. Step 2 writes, in order:
- every page other than a map page whose epoch-`k` content is not already at a slot the image map references: the dirty frames with content epoch `≤ k`, and the epoch copies (§5.6). Pages that eviction already wrote during epoch `k`, or wrote after the flip as epoch-`k` content (§5.2), are adopted as they are;
- when a written frame was not mutated in epoch `k+1` and its page was not already written for image `k` (the image-record list holds no earlier record for it), its new slot also becomes its current home. The data phase decides both under the frame's latch, and it does not write a page whose epoch-`k` content the list already records: adopting such a write anyway would treat the earlier write's slot as current-only and reusable (§4.4) while image `k`'s map still names it;
- then the **map phase**, once the data phase has ended and no write of epoch-`k` content is in flight, whether a claim (§5.6), a §5.2 previous-epoch victim or a data-phase write: it waits on each such write's latched frame, holding no latch, so the list is complete when it starts, and an append for a page other than a map page after it has started is an engine invariant violation (XX000, §9.2; card S-MU). It writes the epoch-`k` MapLeaf and MapInner pages that changed since image `k−1` or that the list's records name, in post-order (children before their parent): each MapLeaf whose epoch-`k` content has content epoch `k`, or for one of whose entries the list holds a record, and each MapInner a child of which it writes. A MapLeaf is built in a buffer of the checkpointer's own from its epoch-`k` content, with the list's records for its entries applied. Its source is decided under that source's latch and re-checked: its epoch copy, if under the copy's latch it is still that page's epoch copy; else its frame, if under the frame's latch its content epoch is `≤ k`; when it is `k+1`, the map phase checks for the epoch copy again, since one may have been installed meanwhile; else the slot the list records for it, which append-before-release guarantees exists once its copy or frame has been written; else its epoch-`k` home: its current home, unless a write since the flip has moved it, and then its image ref's slot (§4.1). A source is read into the buffer, never loaded into the page table, which names only current frames. A MapInner is built in such a buffer from its children's image refs (§4.1), never from a level-1 MapInner's current refs: those name its MapLeafs' current homes, and a MapLeaf that an earlier map phase wrote, and whose current home no write of its frame has moved since, has a current home with older entries than its image ref's page (card S-MU). The built page is written to a fresh slot, and that slot is recorded in its parent's buffer, up to the root that step 0 recorded (§4.1 "Growth"); a level added in epoch `k+1` is not part of image `k`. Once it has written a page, the map phase sets the page's image ref to the new ref; a page it does not write keeps its image ref, which image `k` names too, the recorded root's being meta `k`'s `map_root` (step 4). The map phase holds at most one latch at a time. A map page whose epoch-`k` content it has taken counts as written for image `k` (§5.6 "When it copies"). It takes a map page's epoch copy under the copy's latch and, still under that latch, detaches it, so that the frame is no longer that page's epoch copy and no claim can evict it; it returns the frame to the `R_EPOCH` reserve only after it has released that latch and before it latches its next source, so it never holds the latch of a frame in the reserve (§5.6 "Reserve") and at most one detached frame is ever out of the reserve, inside step 2's share of §5.6's Bound. A claim that wins the frame's latch afterwards finds it no longer the copy it chose and moves on, so no record for a map page the map phase has built is appended later, and such an append is an engine invariant violation (XX000, §9.2). A slot the list records for a map page it rewrites leaves §4.4 item 2 once the new one is written; it is reusable only if no other reference of §4.4 holds it, since it may be the page's current home. The map phase never makes a slot a map page's current home: only a write of the page's live frame does, as §4.1 says. Image `k`'s slot bitmap is derived from its built map, not from the list (I-FREE-EXACT);
- then the free set **last**: allocate the slots for the FreeSet pages first (growing the file first if needed), then serialise both bitmaps of image `k`, with those slots marked used (I-FREE-EXACT).
  - **Sizing.** The bitmap size depends only on `slot_count` and `next_lpid`. If allocating the FreeSet slots grows the file, `slot_count` changes, so the checkpointer recomputes the number of FreeSet pages after the growth and allocates the difference.
  - **Convergence: the condition.** Say the original allocation needs `n` FreeSet pages but finds only `f < n` free slots.
    - The growth adds `g ≥ 16` slots (§4.5: 1 MiB at the largest page size).
    - The rest of the original allocation, `r = n − f` pages, lands in those new slots.
    - Covering the `g` new slots needs `extra` more pages: at most `⌈g / (8·(page_size − 24))⌉` FreeSetLeaf pages plus one page per radix level. These must land in the new slots too.

    One growth is therefore enough exactly when **`r + extra ≤ g`**. `extra < g` alone is not enough.
  - **Convergence: why it holds.** `r ≤ n`, the whole FreeSet:
    - while the file is at most 16 MiB, `g` is at its 1 MiB floor and the FreeSet is a handful of pages;
    - above that, `g = file_len / 16` slots, while the FreeSet is about `(slot_count + next_lpid) / (8·(page_size − 24))` pages, under 1/30,000 of that sum even at 4 KiB, the smallest page size.

    So `n + extra ≤ g` unless `next_lpid` exceeds `slot_count` by orders of magnitude.
  - **Convergence: the check.** The checkpointer still checks `r + extra ≤ g` after the growth. If the check fails, it grows again and repeats the check. Each further growth places at least `g − extra > 0` of the outstanding pages, so the repetition ends.
  - Growth does not change `next_lpid`, because FreeSet pages are physically addressed.

Step 2's data phase ends only when no epoch-`k` content is outstanding: it waits on a latched frame (§5.6's claim and install) (mutant STEP2-NO-WAIT, §15). It waits holding no latch, map latches included. It records a data write in the image-record list, not in a map page; it latches a current MapLeaf only after the written frame's latch, to set a current home; and in the map phase it holds at most one latch at a time (§5.3) (mutant CKPT-WAIT-HOLDING-MAP, §15).

Pages created or mutated only after the flip are not part of the image.

**Step 3.** `sync_all` the page file.

**Step 4.** Write meta `k` (the file's static fields, `ckpt_id = k`, `T`, `gc_w`, epoch, the roots, `map_level`, `next_lpid`, `slot_count`, `wal_start_lsn`, `wal_start_prev`, `wal_start_prev_crc`, `wal_ckpt_end`, `ts_ckpt_hi`, `boot_id`, CRC) twice: copy 0 to slot `1 + (k mod 2)` and copy 1 to that parity's replica block (§2.2, §2.4), in either order, both before step 5 (I-META-COPIES; mutants REPLICA-SKIP and COPY-INDEX-OFF, §2.2). Checked at meta write: each copy is well-formed (§2.2; mutant WRITE-MALFORMED), with `T ≤ ts_ckpt_hi` and `wal_start_lsn ≤ wal_ckpt_end` among its domains, and `wal_ckpt_end ≤ durable_end`. A violation is an engine invariant violation, fail-stop with XX000 (§9.2), as for `gc_w ≤ T` (§10.5), whose violation at a boot checkpoint is XX001 instead (§10.5).

**Step 5.** `sync_all`. Meta `k` is now the durable image.

**Step 6.** Only now, one generation behind (I-TWO-GEN, §4.4):
- move to free the slots pending since the previous step 6: those that image `k−2` referenced and neither image `k−1` nor image `k` references (after an open, the open's pending list: those the previous image referenced and the opened image does not, §14 step 2);
- make pending the slots that image `k−1` references and image `k` does not;
- recycle the WAL segments wholly below meta `k−1`'s `wal_start_lsn` (after an open, the opened meta's), lowest first, each rename durable before the next (§12.1, I-WAL-START). Mutant **RECYCLE-UNORDERED**: a rename is issued before the previous one is durable, or out of order, so a power cut can leave a higher segment recycled and a lower one present.

**I-META-ATOMIC.** A recovered meta never points at an unwritten or unsynced page: step 3 precedes step 4. Mutant: META-BEFORE-SYNC.

**I-CKPT-AT-T.** Image `k` is a consistent cut: every gated operation is wholly before or wholly after the flip. In image `k`:
- **(a)** every commit with ts `≤ T` is wholly present, up to GC `≤ gc_w`. For every snapshot `S` with `gc_w ≤ S ≤ T`, reads on the image equal reads on the committed state at `T`;
- **(b)** every ordinary entry with ts `> T` belongs to a commit `c` with `T < c ≤ ts_ckpt_hi` whose records end at or below `wal_ckpt_end` and are durable when meta `k` is written (step 1). Recovery replays `c` wholly **if those records still read back**; if they do not, I-WAL-COVER refuses to open the image as it is (§14 step 3a). An unlogged bulk-load entry (the Exception below) with ts `f > T` has its fence commit `f`'s record below `wal_ckpt_end` in the same way, and a recovered frontier below `f` fails the same check. No open therefore holds an entry above its recovered frontier;
- **(c)** every ordinary entry belongs to a committed transaction (N2). An image may hold **provisional entries** (§7.3 tags 6–8) of any transaction, committed or not. After recovery every one of them is dead (C-T0 v8 §2.4: its `txn.boot` is not the new boot id), so none is ever read as data. A committed transaction's data is in the image as ordinary entries if its commit ts is `≤ T` (`T` is the apply frontier, C-T0 v8's `M`), and otherwise it is replayed from its durable chunks (§12.6, §14). Mutant CKPT-T-VISIBLE (`T` taken as `visible_ts`).

**Exception.** The unlogged entries of a §12.8 bulk load in a tree that is **not yet published** are exempt from (a)–(c):
- they come from no commit;
- an image may hold any part of them;
- no reader can use the tree until after a forced checkpoint makes them whole (I-BULK-PUBLISH).

(a)'s read equality is over published trees.

Mutants: FLIP-NO-GATE (a split or pack straddles the flip), WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY.

**I-FREE-SAFE** and **I-TWO-GEN** (§4.4). Mutants: FREE-EARLY (step 6's frees moved before step 5), FREE-ONE-GEN and RECYCLE-ONE-GEN (§4.4).

---

## 14. Recovery

1. Read page 0 and the four meta places, and select the newest meta `k` and the previous meta by §2.2's open rule (I-META-COPIES, I-META-SLOT). An intact copy with the file's `creation_uuid` whose `T > ts_ckpt_hi` or `wal_start_lsn > wal_ckpt_end` is not well-formed (§2.2): in any place, the open fails with XX001, or with 55000 when §2.2's rule meets a record of the file of a later format version first, with no fallback. Mutant **META-RANGE-SKIP**: this open-time check is removed (tests `t_cover_ts_cross_check`, §15 I-WAL-COVER, and `t_meta_copies`' table form, §2.2).

   **The fence** (I-META-COPIES; card S-DELTA, S-ALPHA-F1). A read-write open in which a meta place's read failed (§2.2) zeroes every such place, and runs `sync_all`, before its first write of any kind: a pass-2 eviction (step 4), the retire write (step 4a), step 5's truncation or the boot checkpoint. If a fence write or its sync fails, the open fails with that error and writes nothing more; a place that cannot be written would fail the next checkpoint of its parity in the same way (§9.2). A read-only open, and an open refused before its first write, fence nothing.
   - *Why.* A place whose read failed may hold a meta newer than the one the open selects, or a copy of the `ckpt_id` this open's boot checkpoint will write (§2.2), and the open's writes may reuse slots that such a meta's image references. If the read later succeeds, a later open would select that meta over the reused slots, or, after this open's boot checkpoint rewrote that `ckpt_id` with one copy's write lost, meet the stale copy beside the new one with an equal `boot_id` and fail with XX001 (the tie rule, §2.2). Zeroing the place before any write closes both. It gives up only a copy this open could not read, and the next checkpoint of that parity rewrites both places.
   - *Residual.* A fence write that the device loses after its sync brings the unreadable copy back: the reused slots then fail I-INTEGRITY when read (§3.3), and an equal-`boot_id` tie fails the open with XX001. Neither is silent. A fenced place of the selected meta's own parity leaves that meta one copy until its parity's next checkpoint rewrites both: a second fault on that copy meanwhile, such as the boot checkpoint's first copy misdirected onto it with a crash before its second copy, leaves no copy of it and a misplaced copy two `ckpt_id`s above the meta before it, and the next open fails with XX001 in both modes (two faults; draft 7.13 refused the same state).
   - Mutant **FENCE-SKIP**: the open writes before it zeroes those places, or never zeroes them (`t_meta_copies`' fence variants, §2.2). Mutant **FENCE-BEFORE-REFUSAL**: an open that the open rule refuses has zeroed them (`t_meta_copies`' unlocated variant, §2.2).
2. Load the map root, at `map_level` (§4.1), and the free set of image `k`. Every slot not referenced by image `k` is free, including the slots eviction wrote after meta `k`, except those the previous image references (I-TWO-GEN, §4.4): they stay protected until the first step 6 after the open, and those of them image `k` does not reference are the open's pending list (§13 step 6). Step 2 reads meta `k−1`'s free set even when a read of image `k` has already failed in it, so that step 4a can fall back from a failure in step 2 (§14 step 4a item 1). If meta `k−1` is valid and that read fails, by an integrity failure (§3.3, or its decoder's refusal of a page that passes it, step 4a) or a read error (58030), the open protects every slot below meta `k−1`'s `slot_count` that image `k` does not reference instead, until the first step 6 after the open, and those slots are its pending list; its allocations meanwhile take slots at or above that `slot_count`, and the file grows when none is free (§4.5). It reports the failed read, and it has no previous image to fall back to (step 4a item 1). It is not refused for the failed read alone, and a later open that reads that free set finds image `k−1` as it was (I-TWO-GEN; card S-DELTA, S-ALPHA-F2). Then build the root table from image `k`'s directory (§6.8), before replay.
3. **Pass 1:** if the segment that holds `wal_start_lsn` is missing, the open has detected damage (I-WAL-START, §12.5): a read-write open fails with **XX001** (detail: start segment missing, with `wal_start_lsn`) before the walk, and writes nothing; a read-only open serves salvage with the valid end at `wal_start_lsn` (§14.1). Otherwise, walk the WAL from `wal_start_lsn` to the valid end (§12.5), collecting the set of `(txn, commit_ts)` of every `CommitMarker` with `commit_ts > T`. It checks each record for a splice as well (§12.5, I-WAL-CHAIN), and ends at the first one. It decodes every record it accepts, and ends at the first that does not decode, or where it needs a byte past a segment file's end-of-file anywhere but in the record header at the first LSN of a segment it entered by a hop (§12.5, "Malformed records" and "Short segments"; step 3a). A read error ends it with no valid end (§12.5, "Read errors"): a read-write open fails with **58030**, whatever was asked of it, and writes nothing, or, in a fall-back from an image `k` that failed in pass 2, or a rollback that a read error in pass 2 let proceed, nothing more than the fence and that pass 2's evictions (step 4a); a read-only open serves salvage instead at the **read cut** (§12.5): the start of the first record whose bytes the walk could not read, or, where the open of a segment failed, the position at which the walk was to read from it. Every record pass 1 accepted lies below the cut, so its set of `CommitMarker` records, its largest `commit_ts`, the coverage check and the dependency check (steps 3a and 3b) are taken over the records below it, and the salvage is reported with the read error (§14.1).

3a. **Coverage check (I-WAL-COVER).** Pass 1 also records the largest `commit_ts` of the `Commit` and `CommitMarker` records it reads. The WAL **covers** image `k` iff:
   - the valid end (§12.5: the LSN at which the walk stopped, never an end-of-file) is at or above meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end`; and
   - `max(T, that largest commit_ts) ≥ ts_ckpt_hi`.

   If it does not, there is **WAL loss**: bytes that step 1 of checkpoint `k` synced before meta `k` was written no longer read back, and image `k` may hold entries of the commits they recorded (I-CKPT-AT-T (b)). The open then fails with **XX001** (detail: WAL loss, with the valid end, `wal_ckpt_end`, `T` and `ts_ckpt_hi`) **before pass 2, and writes nothing**: no replay mutation, no eviction write and no truncation (step 5). The files stay exactly as found, including any intact records behind the damage. A read-only open serves the salvage state instead (§14.1).
   - *Why it is sound.* Suppose image `k` held an entry with ts `c` above the recovered frontier.
     - An ordinary entry's commit record lies below `wal_ckpt_end` (§13 step 0) and at or above `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6, I-WAL-TS-ORDER), and it was not replayed. So the walk stopped below it, and the first condition fails.
     - An unlogged bulk-load entry (§12.8) has ts `c = f`, its fence commit's ts. `f`'s `Commit` record was appended before the load, so before the flip, and so it ends at or below `wal_ckpt_end`. Since `f >` the frontier `≥ T`, the record lies at or above `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6), and it was not replayed. The first condition fails the same way.

     So no store opens holding an entry above its recovered frontier, and C-T0 v8's sequencer restart at frontier + 1 (C-T0 v8 §3.7) never reissues the ts of an entry in the trees (I-ENTRY-UNIQUE). The check has no false negatives for that hazard, but it is not exact (below). The second condition checks the same floor directly from the meta. In a correct store it never fails alone; it turns a breach of §12.4's "apply waits for the append" or of I-WAL-TS-ORDER into an XX001 here instead of a reissued ts.
   - *What it also refuses.* It fails on any loss of bytes below `wal_ckpt_end`, whether or not the image depends on them:
     - records of commits that were appended but not yet applied at the flip, which are synced and may be acked;
     - chunks of a chunked transaction that committed after the flip, whose chunks lie below `wal_ckpt_end` and whose marker lies above it;
     - bytes that held **no committed data at all**: chunks of a transaction that never committed, a `SegmentEnd`, or records `≤ T` with nothing above `T` behind them.

     This is deliberate. The check compares positions, so once the bytes are gone it cannot tell what they held, and detected damage below `wal_ckpt_end` is never silent.
   - *Why never open the image as it is.* The image would serve part of a commit whose record is gone, above the frontier, so the state would be neither atomic nor a ts-prefix, and the sequencer would then reissue that commit's ts. E4 found this in 451 class MW rounds (X-5c-F3, MW-ORPHAN).
   - Mutants:
     - **COVER-CHECK-SKIP**: the check is removed;
     - **COVER-END-AT-T**: `wal_ckpt_end` is taken as the end of the last record with commit_ts `≤ T`, WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY's position;
     - **COVER-AFTER-TRUNCATE**: the check runs after step 5 or pass 2;
     - **TS-HI-CHECK-SKIP**: the second condition is removed;
     - **COVER-AT-EOF**: the first condition compares against a short segment's end-of-file (§12.5).

     Tests: `t_wal_loss_below_ckpt_end` and `t_cover_ts_cross_check` (A-7d, the bulk-load variant's load also B-4d; §15 I-WAL-COVER, §15.1).

   **A splice** (§12.5, I-WAL-CHAIN) found by pass 1 fails a read-write open in the same way, whether or not the coverage check passes. The open fails with **XX001** (detail: WAL splice, with the record's LSN, its `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, and the walk's predecessor's start and `crc`, plus the coverage result) before pass 2, and writes nothing. That holds wherever the splice lies, at or above `wal_ckpt_end` included. A read-only open serves salvage instead (§14.1), with the valid end, and every result of pass 1, cut back to the **splice cut**. The cut is the start of the first accepted record that ends after `m = min(the walk's predecessor's start, prev_lsn)`, or the walk's start if no accepted record ends after `m`. When `prev_lsn` equals the walk's predecessor's start and only `prev_crc` differs, `m` is that start, and the cut is the predecessor itself. Every record from that point on is suspect, because the two histories disagree there: if the splice record is genuine, the walk's history differs from the splice record's from the record covering `prev_lsn` on, or from an earlier record; if it is foreign, from the splice on. The cut keeps no record from that point on, but it can keep records of an overwrite that began earlier. When the records written over two or more original records chain to the walk's record before them, the walk accepts them up to the splice: the first is a spliced-in record whose predecessor is byte-identical at the same position (§12.5, "What it does not catch"), and no record shows where the overwrite began. So the records below the cut are one history as its writers appended it, up to a collision (§12.5): the history the two share, or, after such an overwrite, the overwriting history up to the cut, which salvage serves as the walk read it, as any open serves a splice it does not detect (§14.1).

   **A malformed record or a short segment** (§12.5) found by pass 1 fails a read-write open in the same way, whether or not the coverage check passes, and wherever it lies: XX001 (detail: malformed record, with its LSN and what did not decode; or short segment, with the segment, its file's length and the position the walk had reached) before pass 2, and the open writes nothing. A read-only open serves salvage instead (§14.1), with the valid end, and every result of pass 1, cut back to the **damage cut**: the start of the malformed record, or the position at which the walk needed a byte past the end-of-file (the start of the record whose header or body runs past it). The records below the cut are ones the walk accepted, one history as its writers appended it up to a collision (§12.5), so the salvage serves the ts-prefix at its frontier, as for WAL loss.
3b. **Dependencies (I-CKPT-DEP).** After step 3a and before pass 2, recovery reads every `RequireCkpt{id}` op that pass 2 would apply: in a `Commit` with `commit_ts > T`, and in a `Chunk` of a transaction in pass 1's set. Every op is checked, not only the first. If any `id` exceeds the opened meta's `ckpt_id`, the image lacks entries a replayed commit makes visible (§12.8): a read-write open fails with **XX001** (detail: checkpoint dependency, with the record's LSN, the `id` and the opened `ckpt_id`) before pass 2, and writes nothing. A read-only open serves salvage at the **dependency cut**: the start of the commit record (the `Commit`, or a chunked transaction's `CommitMarker`) of the first transaction, in LSN order, whose op fails. Pass 2 then applies only the `Commit` records below the cut and the `Chunk` records of transactions whose `CommitMarker` lies below it (§14.1). While it reads the records pass 2 will apply, step 3b also counts each `CreateTree` op among them whose tree id the root table does not hold, and the open charges an entry to `R_file` for each before it sizes its pool, so that pass 2 installs no root-table entry it has not charged (R-1-memory §5.2; card S-LAMBDA).
   - Mutants: **DEP-CHECK-SKIP**: the check is removed. **DEP-FIRST-ONLY**: the check stops after the first `RequireCkpt` it reads. **DEP-CHECK-LATE**: the check runs after pass 2 or after step 5. **DEP-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT**: a read-only open replays the failing transaction, or a record at or after the cut. DEP-BY-POSITION and DEP-ID-STALE are §12.8's.
   - Test `t_ckpt_dep` (A-6a, A-7d, B-4d; §15.1). At A-6a, `RequireCkpt` encodes and decodes inside `Commit` and `Chunk` records, the fuzz targets reach it, and no other op's bytes decode as it. At A-7d, on S3 for the load and its publication (the test makes the unlogged inserts, forces the checkpoint, and issues the publishing commit with the id the checkpoint returned): a checkpoint, a load, its forced checkpoint `k`, its publication and later acked commits, then a post-sync LOST on both copies of meta `k` and a crash. The read-write open fails with XX001 before pass 2 and leaves every file byte-identical; the read-only open serves the state cut before the publication, with every acked commit below the cut and the tree unpublished. Variants: (a) TORN, and MISDIRECTED to a page slot, instead of LOST; (b) two loads, publication A depending on `k` and publication B on `k+1`, with both copies of meta `k+1` lost: the open refuses on B; (c) a checkpoint flipped during the load, after the fence commit, so that it has the forced checkpoint's `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`, with the forced meta lost: refused; (d) the tree dropped after its publication, and logged writes into it after its publication: each refused; (e) a chunked publication: refused; (f) faultless, a crash at every step from the load to the commits after the publication: every open succeeds, and the tree holds every loaded entry. DEP-CHECK-SKIP fails the main case: the open succeeds, and a scan through the published tree misses the loaded entries. DEP-FIRST-ONLY fails (b). DEP-BY-POSITION fails (c): it opens the image of the checkpoint flipped during the load, which lacks the load's later entries. DEP-CHECK-LATE fails the main case's byte-identity: a pass-2 eviction or step 5's truncation has written. DEP-ID-STALE fails the main case: the publication names meta `k−1`, and the open from it replays the publication. DEP-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT fails the read-only check: the tree is served published and short. At B-4d the main case runs with B-4d's load and C-T6's publication.
4. **Pass 2:** walk the same span in LSN order, and apply:
   - every `Commit` with `commit_ts > T`;
   - every `Chunk` of a transaction in pass 1's set, at that set's `commit_ts`.

   A chunk whose transaction has no marker is ignored. Records with `commit_ts ≤ T` are skipped: they are in the image. A read error in this walk fails the open with **58030**, a read-only open's included, after no more than the fence, pass 2's own evictions and any retire write before it (§12.5, "Read errors").

   **DropTree.** A replayed `DropTree` sets `dropping` and does nothing else (nothing if the id is absent), except that, when it changes an entry from `live` to `dropping`, it raises `gc_w_used` to its op's `w` (§12.3; §6.8 step 1, §10.5 "Drops"; mutants REPLAY-DROP-NO-GCW and REPLAY-DROP-AT-TS), in a read-only open too, since the raise writes nothing. Its walk runs at step 6. Pass 2 runs no walk. Mutant **REPLAY-DROP-WALK**: pass 2 walks, so a read-only open frees a page (§14.1; test `t_ro_open` with a replayed `DropTree`, A-7d). Its state change includes the root-table entry's, and a replayed `CreateTree` installs its entry, as in normal operation (§6.8).

   **Chunk supersede (I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE).** For a chunked transaction, each op inserts or replaces the entry `key@commit_ts`, and a `Delete{revert}` deletes it if present. A replace or delete frees the replaced value's overflow chain in the same gated operation (§6.7). So the last op per key wins, whatever the image already holds for that key at that ts. An op that inserts an entry identical to the present one is a no-op. Ops of a `Commit` record keep I-ENTRY-UNIQUE's XX001 for a different value. Mutant SUPERSEDE-AS-CONFLICT: a superseding op is treated as a conflict (XX001).
4a. **Fall-back (I-FALLBACK).** The newest image **fails at open** when an integrity check (§3.3) fails on a page read through image `k`'s references, in step 2 or in pass 2, or when such a page passes it but a decoder refuses the page as it was read, or an entry key, value or tag that it held as read (§3.1, §7.2, §7.3; card S-LAMBDA, S-ZETA-F1). Either is an **integrity failure** at open, here and in step 2's read of meta `k−1`'s free set: a page that passes §3.3 holds the bytes its writer wrote under the reference's `crc`, so a decoder's refusal of it shows a faulty writer's page, or a CRC collision, in image `k` alone, on which neither image `k−1` nor the WAL span the proof covers depends, while the selected meta, which the open rule checked, chooses the history. A refusal of bytes that this open itself wrote is not one, and a re-insert with a different value (§9.1) is no decoder's refusal: there the WAL's record and the image disagree about one commit, and a fall-back would take one side without a word, so it stays XX001 with no fall-back (§14 step 4). Nothing else falls back on its own: a refusal in step 1, 3, 3a or 3b, a read error (58030), and a failure in steps 5–8 are handled as those steps and §9.2 say. A read error on a page read through image `k`'s references, in step 2 or in pass 2, fails a read-write open with 58030 and writes nothing more than the fence and the evictions before it; an explicit rollback (below) and a read-only open's salvage (§14.1) accept it as they accept an integrity failure, when there is a previous image (card S-ALPHA-F4). Image `k` can fail in pass 2 after pass 2 has evicted: those evictions wrote only slots that image `k` does not reference, and none that image `k−1` references when meta `k−1` is valid (step 2, §4.4, I-TWO-GEN), and an outcome of this step that writes nothing writes nothing more than they and the fence before them did (step 1). The open then:
   1. Falls back only to the previous image (§1): meta `k−1`, and only when step 2 read meta `k−1`'s free set and so protected its slots (I-TWO-GEN). With no previous meta, or with its free set failing a read in step 2, there is nothing to fall back to, wherever image `k` failed, and the open fails with **XX001** (detail: image `k` failed, with no previous image), or with **58030** when that read failed with a read error (58030), which a later open may not meet: it may then read the free set and fall back. An explicit rollback that proceeds on a read error through image `k`'s references (below) and finds no previous image fails with **58030** too, never XX001: a read error is not evidence of damage, and a later open may not meet it. Image `k−1` is as it was when meta `k` is the newest meta that was synced and every open since meta `k` was synced protected image `k−1`'s slots too (step 2, I-TWO-GEN). Otherwise a slot of image `k−1` that a later write reused fails I-INTEGRITY when it is read (§3.3): at open, as item 2's integrity failure, or at a later read (§9.2); never silently (§2.2, "One fall-back, and only one").
   2. Discards everything it built from image `k`, and runs step 1's range check on meta `k−1` and steps 2–3b from meta `k−1`: the load of image `k−1`, pass 1 from its `wal_start_lsn`, and its coverage, splice and dependency checks, each against meta `k−1`. A refusal there is handled as if meta `k−1` were the newest meta, with the read-only salvage §14.1 gives it. An integrity failure in image `k−1` is XX001, and a read error (58030) on one of its pages is 58030, in both opens and for an explicit rollback: no open falls back two generations, not even to serve salvage. A read error in the walk from meta `k−1` is §12.5's: a read-write open and an explicit rollback fail with 58030, and a read-only open serves salvage from meta `k−1` at its read cut, or fails with 58030 if only pass 2 meets it. Pass-2 evictions made from image `k` before it failed wrote only slots that neither image `k` nor image `k−1` references (§4.4), so image `k−1` is as item 1 says. An integrity failure or a read error in image `k−1` in pass 2, after the retire write (below), leaves meta `k` retired: the next open selects meta `k−1` and refuses as this one did, unless the read error does not recur.
   3. **The proof.** The fall-back is **proven** when, beyond item 2, pass 1 from meta `k−1` reaches meta `k`: its valid end is at or above meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end`, and the largest `commit_ts` it read, or meta `k−1`'s `T` if larger, is at or above meta `k`'s `ts_ckpt_hi`. Then every commit whose entries image `k` holds is replayed from the WAL, and every unlogged entry that image `k` holds and image `k−1` lacks belongs to a load whose publication names checkpoint `k` or later, which step 3b refuses, or whose tree is still unpublished (§12.8). So the open recovers the committed state meta `k` would have given; only GC differs, and an older image holds more versions, not fewer.
   4. **Proven:** a read-write open retires meta `k` (below), then runs pass 2 and steps 5–8 from meta `k−1`. It reports the fall-back: image `k`'s failure, and both metas' `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`. A read-only open serves the same state and writes nothing.
   5. **Unproven:** a read-write open fails with **XX001** (detail: image `k` failed and the fall-back is unproven, with the valid end and the largest `commit_ts` against meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`), and writes nothing more (above): no meta place whose read succeeded, no WAL byte and no slot that image `k` or image `k−1` references changes. A read-only open serves meta `k−1`'s recovered state as salvage, at its frontier, and reports the same values (§14.1). Only an explicit rollback opens it read-write.

   **Retiring meta `k`.** Before a read-write fall-back writes anything from meta `k−1` (a pass-2 eviction, step 5's truncation, the boot checkpoint), it retires meta `k` in two writes: first it overwrites with zeros every place of the other parity that holds a copy of meta `k` (a misplaced copy, §2.2) and runs `sync_all`; then it overwrites both places of meta `k`'s own parity with zeros and runs `sync_all`. Only the fence (step 1) and the pass-2 evictions made from image `k` before it failed can precede the retire write (above). No place then holds a copy of meta `k`, so no later open selects it and opens image `k` over slots this open reuses; the next open selects meta `k−1` as the newest, and the boot checkpoint later writes `ckpt_id` `k` to its parity's places, as any checkpoint does. The misplaced copies go first, so that a crash between the two writes leaves meta `k` either in the places of its own parity that held it at the open, to be decided again, or in no place at all. The order is no longer needed for the next open to succeed: a misplaced copy one `ckpt_id` above the newest valid meta fails no open (§2.2). A crash before the second `sync_all` completes leaves either a copy of meta `k` in a place of its own parity, with image `k` as it was, so that the next open decides again, or meta `k` retired. If a retire write fails, the open fails with its error and writes nothing more. A read-only open and a refused open retire nothing.

   **Explicit rollback** (PLAN-v3 §10 item 16). A read-write open may be asked to roll back one generation, naming the `ckpt_id` it abandons. It proceeds only when the newest meta has that `ckpt_id` and its image fails at open, or a read through its references in step 2 or in pass 2 fails with a read error (58030); otherwise it fails with **55000** and writes nothing more than the fence (step 1) and the evictions of a pass 2 that ran from image `k` without a failure, into slots that image `k` does not reference (above). It then falls back as a proven fall-back does, with items 1 and 2 against meta `k−1`, waiving only the proof (item 3). It reports what it abandons: meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`, against the valid end and the frontier it recovered. Commits whose records lie past that valid end are lost; step 5 truncates there. It does not apply to WAL loss, a splice, a malformed record or a short segment under a newest image that opens: there a read-write open stays refused (PLAN-v3 §10 item 8), and the remedy is the read-only salvage and an export (§14.1).

   Mutants:
   - **FALLBACK-OFF**: an image that fails at open is XX001 with no fall-back, as at draft 7.10.
   - **FALLBACK-NO-PROOF**: the fall-back skips the proof (item 3).
   - **FALLBACK-TWO-GEN**: the fall-back takes the other parity's candidate whatever its `ckpt_id`.
   - **RETIRE-SKIP**: a read-write fall-back writes from meta `k−1` before it retires meta `k`, or never retires it.
   - **RETIRE-ONE-COPY**: the retire write zeroes the primary copy only.
   - **ROLLBACK-SKIPS-PREV-CHECKS**: an explicit rollback skips meta `k−1`'s own checks (item 2).
   - **ROLLBACK-REFUSES-READ-ERROR**: an explicit rollback, or a read-only open, refuses a newest image that a read error stops at open, as at draft 7.14, or accepts the read error only in step 2, not in pass 2 (card S-ALPHA-F4).
   - **FALLBACK-CHECKS-ONLY** (card S-LAMBDA): only §3.3's checks count as failing at open, so a page of image `k` that passes them but that its decoder refuses is XX001 with no fall-back, no explicit rollback and no read-only salvage, as at draft 7.20.
   - **ROLLBACK-READ-ERROR-AS-CORRUPT** (card S-LAMBDA): an explicit rollback that proceeds on a read error through image `k`'s references and finds no previous image, because meta `k−1`'s free set failed an integrity check in step 2, fails with XX001 instead of 58030 (item 1).

   Test `t_fallback` (A-7d; §15.1):
   - *Proven:* a post-sync fault (LOST or TORN) on a page of image `k` that the open reads (a MapLeaf, a FreeSet page, a directory page, and a leaf pass 2 replays into, each a case), with acked commits after meta `k`. The read-write open falls back, recovers every acked commit and reports the fall-back, and every later image passes amcheck; a hook asserts that every place that held a copy of meta `k` is zeros, and synced, before the open's first write from meta `k−1`, and that every write before the retire went to a slot that neither image `k` nor image `k−1` references. The read-only open serves the same state and leaves every file byte-identical. FALLBACK-OFF fails it: the open is refused. RO-WRITES fails the read-only form: it retires meta `k`. *Unread variant*, with its fault on a page of image `k` that step 2 reads (a MapLeaf, a FreeSet page or a directory page): a hook corrupts the bytes of one read of meta `k−1`'s free set (an integrity failure, §3.3) in step 2 of the first open only: that open fails with XX001 (no previous image) and writes nothing, and the next open falls back with proof and recovers every acked commit. With a read error (58030) made by the hook in place of the corrupted bytes, that open fails with 58030 instead and writes nothing, and the next open falls back the same way.
   - *Unproven:* as proven, plus a post-sync LOST on a synced WAL record, holding an acked commit, at or above meta `k−1`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and below meta `k`'s `wal_start_lsn` (the fixture acks commits between checkpoints `k−1` and `k`, and no chunked transaction holds meta `k`'s `wal_start_lsn` below meta `k−1`'s `wal_ckpt_end` (§12.6), so that this range holds one), so that only the walk from meta `k−1` reads it (in the leaf case, a loss at or above meta `k`'s start would fail step 3a's coverage check before pass 2, where neither a fall-back nor an explicit rollback applies). The read-write open fails with XX001, and every meta place, every WAL file and every slot that image `k` or image `k−1` references is byte-identical afterwards; where image `k` fails before pass 2, every file is. The read-only open serves the state at its walk's frontier, never a version of the lost commit, and reports the shortfall. An explicit rollback naming meta `k` opens read-write at that frontier, and the next open selects the boot checkpoint's meta. FALLBACK-NO-PROOF fails it: the plain read-write open succeeds without the lost commit. An explicit rollback whose meta `k−1` fails its own coverage check (a second LOST, below meta `k−1`'s `wal_ckpt_end`) fails with XX001; ROLLBACK-SKIPS-PREV-CHECKS fails it, opening with an entry above its frontier that a read at that entry's ts sees. An explicit rollback asked of a store whose newest image opens fails with 55000.
   - *Read error* (card S-ALPHA-F4): as proven, but a hook fails every read of one slot with a read error (58030), a persistent fault until that slot is next written: a slot that image `k` references and image `k−1` does not, holding, in one case, a page that step 2 reads (a MapLeaf, a FreeSet page or a directory page) and, in another, a leaf pass 2 replays into. The read-write open fails with 58030 and writes nothing, or, in the pass-2 case, nothing but the evictions pass 2 made before the read error, each into a slot that neither image `k` nor image `k−1` references; the read-only open serves meta `k−1`'s recovered state as salvage, with every acked commit, reports the read error and the valid end and largest `commit_ts` against meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`, and leaves every file byte-identical; an explicit rollback naming meta `k` opens read-write from meta `k−1` and recovers every acked commit. With both copies of meta `k−1` lost too, so that there is no previous meta, the step-2 case's three opens each fail with 58030 and write nothing, and so they do when, instead, a hook corrupts the bytes of every read of meta `k−1`'s free set (an integrity failure), so that there is no previous image (card S-LAMBDA). ROLLBACK-REFUSES-READ-ERROR fails both cases: the rollback is refused with 55000, and the read-only open fails with 58030; its step-2-only form fails the pass-2 case. ROLLBACK-READ-ERROR-AS-CORRUPT fails the free-set form: the rollback fails with XX001.
   - *Two generations:* the other parity's candidate is meta `k−3`, both copies of meta `k−1` having been lost: the open fails with XX001. FALLBACK-TWO-GEN fails it: it selects meta `k−3`, which a hook sees as a read through meta `k−3`'s references, and its XX001 detail is not "with no previous image".
   - *Retire:* a hook corrupts the bytes of one read of a page of image `k` at the first open only, so that the page reads correctly afterwards. The read-write open falls back with proof and retires meta `k`, and a crash follows a pass-2 eviction into a slot that image `k` references (a hook places it). The next open selects meta `k−1` as the newest, recovers every acked commit, and every image passes amcheck. RETIRE-SKIP fails it: the next open selects meta `k`, whose image now reads, and meets the overwritten slot (XX001, or amcheck fails). RETIRE-ONE-COPY fails it the same way, through meta `k`'s replica. A crash between the two zeroing writes: the next open decides again and recovers the same state. *Misplaced variant:* one copy of meta `k` was misdirected into a place of the other parity before the fault (meta `k−1` keeps its other copy), and a crash follows the retire write: the next open selects meta `k−1` and recovers every acked commit; a retire that zeroes only meta `k`'s own places opens meta `k−1` too, since the surviving misplaced copy is one `ckpt_id` above it (§2.2).
   - *Decoder* (card S-LAMBDA; S-ZETA-F1): as proven, but a hook in A-7c's step 2, before it computes a page's CRC and writes it, alters one field of a page of image `k` that the page's decoder checks whenever the page is read (for a slotted page, its slot count, set so that the slot array runs past the page's end), so that the page passes §3.3 and its decoder refuses it: a directory page that step 2 reads, and a leaf that pass 2 replays into, each a case. The read-write open falls back with proof, recovers every acked commit and reports image `k`'s failure, and the read-only open serves the same state and leaves every file byte-identical; with the unproven case's lost record as well, the read-write open fails with XX001, the read-only open serves meta `k−1`'s recovered state as salvage, and an explicit rollback naming meta `k` opens read-write from meta `k−1`. FALLBACK-CHECKS-ONLY fails it: every open is refused with XX001, and the rollback with 55000.
5. Truncate the WAL at the valid end, **durably** (I-BOOT-DURABLE). Zero the bytes from the valid end to the end of the segment file that holds it, in place (a short segment is not extended, and no segment file is shortened: §12.5, "Short segments"; mutant TRUNC-SHORTENS-SEGMENT, test `t_wal_short_read` (b)), and zero in place or remove every segment file whose first LSN lies above the valid end; then `sync_data` each segment file written and, if a file was removed, sync `wal/` through `Env`. The step ends only when those syncs have completed, so the boot checkpoint and every append of this boot follow a durable truncation. The writer then starts as §12.4 "After recovery" says.
   - *Why durable.* The bytes past the valid end can hold records an earlier life wrote. The boot-id order stops them only while every boot's id is fresh, and a meta rollback (both copies of a meta lost, or a fall-back from it, §14 step 4a) can give the next boot the id of a boot none of whose records a walk reached (§12.5, Residual). If that next boot's truncation were lost to a power cut, the earlier boot's records could come back past its own under the same id: the chain reports them as a splice and fails the read-write open, or, where the two boots' records agree byte for byte up to them, they replay as commits the later boot never made (card S-1-F6; S-1-F5's gate, (7)). Step 1 of the boot checkpoint syncs only below the valid end, and a `sync_data` of the valid end's segment does not reach later segments, so the truncation needs syncs of its own.
   - Mutant **TRUNC-NOT-DURABLE**: the truncation's writes and removals are not made durable before step 6, or it only moves the append position. Test `t_wal_trunc_durable` (A-7d; §15 I-BOOT-DURABLE, §15.1).
6. Resume the walks of every `dropping` directory entry (§6.8), as a B-6 boot job.
7. Optionally run `amcheck` (A-8).
8. Run the **boot checkpoint** (§13). It runs every step 0–6 and **always writes a new meta** (`ckpt_id` one above the recovered meta's), even when no page is dirty. No transaction starts until its step 5 has made that meta durable. C-T0 v8's **boot id** (C-T0 v8 §2.5) comes from it, and is not the checkpoint epoch `e` (one higher after the flip). Its boot id is the newest, in serial-number order (§12.5), of the low 32 bits of its `ckpt_id`, one above the opened meta's `boot_id`, and one above the newest boot id of any record the recovery walk accepted. After a meta rollback (§2.2, X5-R5) the walk can cross into a boot whose meta was lost, and whose `ckpt_id` this boot checkpoint would otherwise repeat. A boot whose id was raised that way can be followed by one whose `ckpt_id` is below that id, which the opened meta's `boot_id` bounds. Meta `k+1`'s `boot_id` records it. Mutant **BOOT-ID-CKPT-ONLY**: the boot id is the low 32 bits of `ckpt_id` regardless. Test `t_boot_id_after_rollback` (A-7d, class MP): two successive rollbacks to meta `k`, across boots `b` and `b+1`, each made by a post-sync LOST on both copies of the boot meta `k+1`; boot `b` leaves an unmarked chunked TxnId `(id, 1)`, and boot `b+1` commits a chunked transaction. Recovery from `k` must never attach `b`'s chunks to `b+1`'s marker; BOOT-ID-CKPT-ONLY does. Mutant **BOOT-ID-NO-META-FLOOR**: the opened meta's `boot_id` term is omitted. Variant of `t_boot_id_after_rollback` (A-7d, class MP), *raised id, then no records*, with `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot`'s fixture constraints (§12.5): boot `b−1` writes meta `k` to its parity's places and crashes; boot `b` recovers from `k` (id `k+1`), writes meta `k+1` to the other parity's places, appends and acks, and both copies' writes are LOST before a crash; boot `b+1` recovers from `k`, its walk crosses `b`'s records (id `k+2`), and both copies of its meta `k+1` are LOST again before a crash; boot `b+2` recovers from `k`, its walk crosses `b`'s and `b+1`'s records (id `k+3`), its meta `k+1` (`boot_id` `k+3`, `wal_start_lsn` the append position `p`) is durable, and it crashes before any record of its own reads back; boot `b+3` recovers from meta `k+1`, its walk from `p` accepts nothing, it writes meta `k+2`, appends and acks `synchronous_commit=on` commits from `p`, and crashes before its next checkpoint; a LOST write on both copies of meta `k+2` leaves its places holding meta `k`. The open selects meta `k+1` and must replay boot `b+3`'s acked commits. BOOT-ID-NO-META-FLOOR gives boot `b+3` the id `k+2`, older than meta `k+1`'s `boot_id`, so the walk stops at `p`, passes I-WAL-COVER and returns `Ok` without them. Chunks of the previous boot that have no marker belong to transactions that ended without one (aborted, cut off by the crash, or committed with no ts), all ended before this flip, so they do not hold back `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6). A **read-only open** (N11) skips this step and has no boot id; every provisional entry is dead to it (§14.1).

Afterwards, the apply frontier is the largest replayed `commit_ts`, or `T` if nothing was replayed. By I-WAL-COVER it is at least `ts_ckpt_hi`, so it is at least the ts of every entry in the trees, bulk-load entries included. C-T0 v8 restarts its sequencer and watermark from that value and from `gc_w_used` as pass 2 leaves it: the opened meta's `gc_w`, raised by every replayed DropTree mark (§10.5; C-T0 v8 §3.7). Provisional entries found in the image are dead and are ignored by every read (§8).

**Post-sync damage.** I-REDO-COMPLETE assumes the device keeps what a completed sync covered. When it does not, recovery's outcome depends on where the damaged bytes lie:
- **Below `wal_start_lsn`:** never read; the image holds what they recorded.
- **In `[wal_start_lsn, wal_ckpt_end)`:** detected by I-WAL-COVER (step 3a).
- **At or above `wal_ckpt_end`:** the valid end stops at the damage. The state is the ts-prefix up to it (I-WAL-TS-ORDER), and no image entry depends on what is lost.
