But synced, possibly acked, commits past it are lost **without detection**, and step 5 truncates any intact records behind it. The exceptions are a splice (below), when the damaged bytes still pass every stop check and an intact record behind them names a different predecessor, and a segment file cut short after its sync, when the walk needs a byte past its end-of-file anywhere but in the record header at the first LSN of a segment it entered by a hop, or a record that does not decode (§12.5): a read-write open fails before pass 2 and a read-only open serves salvage, and step 5 never runs. A single-copy WAL keeps no durable record of how far it was synced past the last meta (§19 T4).
- **Bytes that still pass every stop check but are not what was appended** (a splice), anywhere in the walked span: detected by I-WAL-CHAIN when an intact later record names a different predecessor (§12.5). A read-write open fails with XX001, and a read-only open serves salvage cut before it (step 3a). Not detected when no record follows (a lost tail), or when the predecessor the intact record names is byte-identical, at the same position, to the one the walk accepted, or their CRCs collide (§12.5).
- **A meta copy:** where both copies of a meta were written, one copy lost, torn or misdirected after its sync, or unreadable: the other copy opens the same meta (I-META-COPIES). Where a crash between step 4's two writes left one copy of the newest meta, a fault that leaves no valid copy of it (lost, torn, unreadable, or misdirected to a page slot) opens the meta before it, which recovers the same state; a misdirect within its parity leaves it valid, and it opens; a misdirect into a place of the other parity is a misplaced copy one `ckpt_id` above the newest valid meta, and the open selects the meta before it in the same way (I-META-SLOT, §2.2). **Both copies of the newest meta** (a device that dropped the sync that covered them): the open selects the previous meta, whose image and WAL span are intact as I-TWO-GEN states, and its walk crosses into the newer records (§12.5), so what is lost is only what the dropped sync also lost of the WAL, as above (§19 T4). **Both copies of the two newest metas:** the open selects an older meta; a reused slot of its image fails I-INTEGRITY when read (§3.3), a recycled start segment fails I-WAL-START, and a publication over it fails I-CKPT-DEP. An I-INTEGRITY failure at open there can make step 4a fall back once from the selected meta, to an image also outside the two generations, whose proof holds against the selected meta only: a reused slot of it fails I-INTEGRITY at open, or at a later read (§9.2). None is silent, but such an open can succeed and then fail reads.
- **A page of the newest image:** the open falls back to the previous image when the log proves complete, and refuses otherwise (step 4a, I-FALLBACK). A page that only a later step reads (a resumed drop walk, amcheck, the boot checkpoint) fails as that step and §9.2 say.
- **An older boot's record left at a valid position:** stopped by the ordered boot-id check (§12.5); a stale record that shares the later boot's id (the Residual, §12.5) is detected as a splice unless its predecessor is byte-identical at the same position too.

**I-REPLAY-IDEM.** Replay is idempotent, so recovering twice, including a crash during recovery, gives the same state. Under (b), every op inserts a uniquely keyed entry (I-ENTRY-UNIQUE). Re-inserting an identical entry is a no-op, so partial applies already in the image (I-CKPT-AT-T (b)) are completed rather than duplicated. A chunked transaction's superseded ops replace one another in LSN order, so each pass ends with the last op per key (I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE). CreateTree and DropTree are idempotent (§6.8). The final state is the set union of the entries, independent of insertion order. Pass 2 still runs in LSN order, so tree creation and drop ops come before and after the writes that depend on them. Mutants (one per direction of I-ENTRY-UNIQUE):
- **REPLAY-DUP-CONFLICT:** an identical re-insert is treated as a conflict (XX001);
- **DUP-DIFFERENT-ACCEPTED:** a re-insert with a different value is accepted (overwritten or ignored) instead of XX001.

The double-crash test (A-7d) is the test that runs recovery twice. It is not a mutant.

**I-REDO-COMPLETE.** Every acked `synchronous_commit=on` commit is either `≤ T` of the recovered image, and in it, or has durable records at or after `wal_start_lsn` before the valid end, and is replayed, given I-ACK-PREFIX (§12.4) and a device that keeps what a completed sync covered (above). This holds for a walk from an older meta after a meta fault too, because the walk crosses into newer boots' records (§12.5), and each of those boots made every byte its own recovery walk accepted durable before its boot meta (I-BOOT-DURABLE), when that meta's image and WAL span are intact and no replayed commit depends on a newer checkpoint (§12.8). The previous meta's image and WAL span are intact as I-TWO-GEN states, and I-CKPT-DEP refuses the dependency. An intact image and WAL span are not enough alone: a load's unlogged entries are in no WAL. Otherwise X5-R5's detection (XX001 on the older image's reused slots, §3.3), I-WAL-START (a recycled start segment) or I-CKPT-DEP applies. Mutants:
- **START-LSN-PAST-CHUNK:** the replay start ignores an open transaction's first chunk;
- **START-LSN-MARKED-CHUNK:** the replay start counts only transactions with no marker. Its test is §12.6's case: a chunked transaction whose marker (`commit_ts > T`) lies after a commit `≤ T`, with its apply unfinished at the flip, then a crash after step 6. The transaction must be recovered whole.
- **START-LSN-SPLIT-READ:** step 0 reads the open-chunk set in an earlier entry to the append critical section than the append position, or a transaction's first `Chunk` enters the set in a later entry than the one that reserves it (§12.4, §12.6, §13 step 0). A first `Chunk` reserved between the two is then neither in the set step 0 reads nor at or above the position it reads, and `wal_start_lsn` lies past it. Test `t_flip_chunk_race` (A-7d; §15.1): at a flip with no `Commit` or `CommitMarker` above `T` and the open-chunk set empty, so that `wal_start_lsn` is the append position, a hook reserves a new chunked transaction `X`'s first `Chunk`, through the store's append from a thread that holds no gate (§12.4), at `q`, holding the only op on `k1`, a key that no other transaction writes: between step 0's read of the set and its read of the append position, and between that `Chunk`'s reservation and its entry into the set, wherever a build separates them, and otherwise just before and just after step 0's one entry. `X` then appends further chunks and its `CommitMarker` at `c > T`, and its `synchronous_commit=on` commit is acked; a power cut that loses every unsynced write follows before the next checkpoint's meta is durable. The open recovers from meta `k` and must replay `X` whole: a read at the recovered frontier, which is at least `c` and always admitted, sees `k1`, whose version at `c` is its newest. Under START-LSN-SPLIT-READ, meta `k`'s `wal_start_lsn` lies past `q`, pass 2 applies `X`'s later chunks at `c` without the first, and the open returns `Ok` without `k1`. Variant *ended*: with no `Commit` or `CommitMarker` above `T` and no other chunked transaction open, `X` reserves chunks and then aborts, or, in a second run, rolls back to a savepoint that leaves it no data and commits with no ts (C-T0 v8 §3.1 step 0); a flip after `X` has left the open-chunk set records the append position its entry reads as `wal_start_lsn`, past `X`'s chunks.
- **CHUNK-SET-KEEPS-ENDED:** a transaction that ends with no marker never leaves the open-chunk set (§12.6), so every later flip's `wal_start_lsn` stays at its first `Chunk` and no segment from there on is ever recycled: safe, but one aborted large transaction stops the WAL from being recycled. Test `t_flip_chunk_race`, variant *ended* (A-7d).

**E4's oracle.** The recovered state equals the model at some commit `c`, with max acked sync-on commit `≤ c ≤` last WAL-written commit (PLAN-v3 §4 E4). That follows from I-REDO-COMPLETE, I-CKPT-AT-T, I-REPLAY-IDEM and I-WAL-TS-ORDER: the durable commits form a ts-prefix.

### 14.1 Read-only open (N11)
**Every build offers a read-only open**, in server, embedded and browser modes (PLAN-v3 §10 item 8): it is the only path to the data after WAL loss (below), and the export path of a stopped store (C4 decision 5). N11's single-owner rule is unchanged. A read-only open recovers exactly the state a read-write open recovers, but keeps it in memory and writes nothing, except where a read-write open fails and a read-only open serves salvage instead: WAL loss, a splice, a malformed record or a short segment (§12.5), a read error in pass 1's walk (§12.5), a missing start segment, an unmet checkpoint dependency, an unproven fall-back, and a newest image that a read error (58030) stops at open when there is a previous image (below; §14 steps 3, 3a, 3b and 4a).

**A stopped store opens read-only with zero free bytes (C4 decision 5).** A store that stopped on 53100 (a full volume or an OPFS quota) is, on disk, a crashed store, because poisoning lives only in memory (§9.2). Whatever the write point, a WAL append, a segment's creation or preallocation, a file extension (§4.5), a checkpoint page write or the meta write, the cut-short write lies past the valid end (§12.5), in a slot or extension that image `k` does not reference (§14 step 2), or in a meta place, where a cut-short copy is invalid and the open rule selects the newest meta with a valid copy (§2.2). So a read-only open of it **succeeds on a volume with zero free bytes** and writes nothing, provided its pool meets the evictable minimum for the replay (below). The data stays readable and exportable without freeing any space. Test `t_ro_open_disk_full` (A-7d with the walk scan S6; B-6 with A-4e's cursor; §15 I-RO-OPEN, §15.1).

**No writer.** While a read-only open is open, no read-write open of the file runs: it takes N11's file lock (through `Env`) in a mode that excludes a read-write open, and it fails rather than wait when a writer holds the lock. **The lock creates and writes no file**, in either mode: it is an advisory lock on the page file itself (opened read-only by a read-only open), or, on OPFS, the access-handle mode (`read-only` against a writer's exclusive `readwrite`). A lock file would be a write, and on a full volume its creation fails (mutant **RO-LOCK-FILE**). <!-- For A-1: an O_RDONLY fd cannot take a POSIX fcntl write lock, so the read-only open needs a shared (read) lock mode; PLAN-v3 §3.2's Env has only lock_exclusive (follow-up proposed in verdicts/S-2-F2.md). --> Why exclude a writer: a writer recycles WAL segments and reuses slots after every checkpoint (§13 step 6), so the reader's image and WAL span would change under it. Whether several read-only opens may share the file is the lock mode's choice; nothing here depends on it, because none of them writes.

**What it replays.** Steps 1–4, including the open-time meta check (step 1) and the coverage check (step 3a): the same winning meta (§2.2), the same image, the same valid end (§12.5), or the splice cut (§14 step 3a), or the dependency cut (step 3b), the same fall-back (step 4a), the same two passes, and the same ops, with I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE. It builds the root table as §14 step 2 does, and its replayed directory changes maintain it.
- So its state equals the state a read-write open of the same files has after step 4, before its boot checkpoint.
- The apply frontier is fixed as after step 4 (the largest replayed `commit_ts`, or `T`), and `W` starts at `gc_w_used` as pass 2 leaves it: the meta's `gc_w`, raised by every replayed DropTree mark (§10.5; C-T0 v8 §3.7 steps 2–3). Neither advances, because nothing commits.
- Skipping pass 2 would serve image `k` alone and silently lose every acked commit above `T` (mutant **RO-NO-REPLAY**).

**WAL loss: salvage (I-WAL-COVER).** When the coverage check fails, a read-write open fails with XX001 (§14 step 3a), but a read-only open succeeds in **salvage**:
- It runs pass 2 over the valid span as usual and fixes the apply frontier at the largest replayed `commit_ts`, or `T`. `visible_ts` is that frontier, and no snapshot above it is admitted.
- **It serves the ts-prefix state at that frontier.**
  - Every commit `≤ T` is in the image (I-CKPT-AT-T (a)), and every commit in `(T, frontier]` is replayed from records that read back.
  - Every lost commit has a ts above the frontier (I-WAL-TS-ORDER), including the commits whose entries sit in the image without a record, and so does every bulk-load entry whose fence commit was lost. A read at `S` sees only versions `≤ S` (§8), so no read sees a lost commit.
  - Dead provisional entries stay dead.
- **Dropped trees.** The tree directory is unversioned. Image `k` can hold a `dropping` mark, and page frees, from a drop-cleanup commit `c_d` above `T`, even one whose record is lost: `c_d`'s apply sets `dropping`, and the bounded walk then runs as a background job (C-T6-rehost §7.4), with nothing waiting for `M ≥ c_d`. Salvage still never reads a freed page:
  - the drop job issues `c_d` only once `W > S_job`, where `S_job ≥ S_ddl`, the retiring DDL's commit ts (C-T6-rehost §7.4, C-T0 v8 §9.3);
  - `c_d` was applied before the flip, so it was issued before it;
  - `W ≤ M` at all times and `M` is monotone (C-T0 v8 §3.5), so `S_ddl < W ≤ T ≤` the salvage frontier;
  - a read at a salvage snapshot `S ≥ S_ddl` reaches trees only through the catalog at `S` (C-T5), which no longer names the dropped trees;
  - a `CreateTree` of a lost commit leaves a tree that no catalog at `S ≤` the frontier names;
  - the drop walk does not resume in a read-only open (below).

  A snapshot below `S_ddl` is refused here, as in any open, whenever the recovered state holds the drop: the image that holds the mark or the frees, or pass 2's replay of `c_d`, raised `gc_w_used` above `S_ddl`, and `W` starts there (§10.5 "Drops"; C-T0 v8 §3.7 step 3). Otherwise the tree is `live` and whole, and such a snapshot reads it.
- It reports the loss with the open: the valid end, `wal_ckpt_end`, `T`, `ts_ckpt_hi` and the frontier.
- **The remedy** is to export from it into a new file (D-7). The read-write open stays refused, and nothing in 2.0 repairs the file in place.
- Mutant **SALVAGE-ABOVE-FRONTIER**: the salvage open admits snapshots above the frontier (for example, `visible_ts := ts_ckpt_hi`) and reads part of a lost commit. Test `t_wal_loss_salvage` (A-7d with the directory as the catalog's tree list (S3) and the walk scan S6; B-6 with the catalog and A-4e's cursor; §15.1).

**Splice: salvage (I-WAL-CHAIN).** A splice (§14 step 3a) is served in the same way, with the valid end cut back to the splice cut. Pass 2 replays only records below the cut, and pass 1's results are cut back with it: its set of `CommitMarker` records holds only those below the cut, so pass 2 applies a chunked transaction's chunks only when its marker lies below the cut, and its largest `commit_ts`, the coverage check (§14 step 3a) and the dependency check (§14 step 3b, I-CKPT-DEP) are taken over the records below the cut. A `RequireCkpt` that only a record at or after the cut carries refuses nothing, since pass 2 does not apply that record; where several cuts apply, the lowest one does (below). Pass 1 read up to the splice, so without the cut-back a marker between the cut and the splice would apply chunks that lie below the cut at that marker's ts, and the frontier would pass a `Commit` of a lower ts between the cut and the marker that is not replayed, so the state would not be a ts-prefix (mutant **SPLICE-MARKER-PAST-CUT**). The apply frontier is fixed at the largest `commit_ts` replayed, or `T`, and no snapshot above it is admitted. So, unless the records below the cut hold an overwrite that the chain cannot detect (§14 step 3a), the open serves the ts-prefix at that frontier, by the argument above; such an overwrite is served as the walk read it, as in any open (§14, "Post-sync damage"). The open reports the splice (the record's LSN, its `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, the walk's predecessor's start and `crc`, and the cut) and the coverage result against the cut. The remedy is the same export. Mutant **SPLICE-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT**: salvage replays records at or after the cut. Mutant **SPLICE-CUT-SAME-START-KEEP**: when `prev_lsn` equals the walk's predecessor's start and only `prev_crc` differs, the cut keeps that predecessor instead of cutting at it (§14 step 3a). Test `t_wal_splice`, read-only variant, crossed with the same-start variant and with a marker past the cut (A-7d; §12.5).

**Start segment, dependency and fall-back: salvage.** A missing start segment (§14 step 3, I-WAL-START) is served with the valid end at `wal_start_lsn`: pass 2 replays nothing, and the frontier is `T`. An unmet dependency (§14 step 3b, I-CKPT-DEP) is served with the valid end cut back to the dependency cut. A malformed record, or a short segment that §12.5 counts as damage, is served with the valid end, and every result of pass 1, cut back to the damage cut (§14 step 3a), as a splice is to its cut; a damage cut at `wal_start_lsn` serves image `k` at `T`, as a missing start segment does. A read error in pass 1's walk (§12.5, "Read errors") is served in the same way at the read cut (§14 step 3), from meta `k`, or from meta `k−1` in a fall-back (§14 step 4a item 2), and a read cut at the walk's start serves that meta's image at its `T`; the salvage reports the read error, with its segment and the position the walk had reached, as well as its cut and the coverage result against it, and is never reported as a complete open, even when the coverage check passes against the cut, since the bytes the walk could not read may hold acked commits. An unproven fall-back (§14 step 4a, I-FALLBACK) is served from meta `k−1`, with whatever cut its own checks give, and so is a newest image that a read error (58030) stops at open when there is a previous image (card S-ALPHA-F4). Where several cuts apply, the lowest one does. Each serves the ts-prefix at its frontier, by the argument above, and reports what cut it; a salvage from meta `k−1` also reports, as §14 step 4a item 5 does, the valid end and the largest `commit_ts` against meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`, and, when a read error stopped image `k`, that read error. A proven fall-back is not salvage: it serves the state a read-write open recovers. Mutant DEP-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT (§14 step 3b).

**Where the replay lives: in the pool, unwritten.** Pass 2's mutations, including its splits and the LPIDs they allocate, exist only in memory.
- **Eviction.** A dirty frame is never evicted, because §5.2's dirty-victim write is a write. Clean frames are evicted as usual.
- **Pool budget.** The evictable part of the pool (`F` minus the dirty frames and the MapLeafs that §4.1's pins hold for them, which a read-only open never releases, since it evicts no dirty page, and which §5.4's `R_MAP` does not count; R-1-memory §4.4, §5.3) must meet §5.4's minimum at every instant, during pass 2 and after it, with the height bound `H` and the reserve `R_EPOCH` that R-1-memory §5.3 sets from that evictable part rather than from `F` (`H` before each op that pass 2 replays, `R_EPOCH` whenever the evictable part shrinks, and both afresh after a fall-back): sized from `F`, they would grow with frames the replay holds. A frame acquisition, a dirtying or a §4.1 pin that would take the evictable part below that minimum, and a descent that would pass that `H`, fail the open with 53200 at once, having written nothing; none of them waits for an unpin, since in a read-only pass 2 nothing but the replay's own calls frees a frame, and a check made only between replayed ops would let one op, such as an insert that builds an overflow chain at `ROW_LIMIT` (§6.7), wait for an unpin that never comes (card S-LAMBDA; `t_ro_open`'s budget forms).
- **The remedy for 53200** is either a read-only open with a larger pool budget (`F` is set per open from the N16 budget, §5.1), which still writes nothing, or a read-write open, whose boot checkpoint makes the replay durable, so that a read-only open of the files it leaves replays nothing.
- **On a volume with no free space, the larger read-only open is the only guaranteed remedy.** A read-write open may need space for its boot checkpoint (§14 step 8), can fail there with 53100 before its meta is durable (§13), and leaves a store that recovers the same state as before.
- **Replay is the only mutation.** No GC mutation (§10), page-local prune, LPID free, directory change or checkpoint runs. A replayed `DropTree` only sets its entry's state (§14 step 4), so the tree's pages stay mapped (mutant REPLAY-DROP-WALK).

**What it skips.**
- **Step 5:** the WAL is not truncated. The torn tail stays on disk, every walk stops at the same valid end (§12.5), and the next read-write open truncates it.
- **Step 6:** `dropping` walks do not resume. Each such tree is in the state a read-write open has before its B-6 job runs.
- **Step 7:** amcheck may run, because it only reads.
- **Step 8:** the boot checkpoint does not run, and there is no boot id. Every provisional entry, from any boot, is dead (C-T0 v8 §2.4), and every read ignores it from its bytes, with no lookup (§8). The live-txn registry stays empty.

**What it refuses.**
- **Every write through `Env`** on the page file or `wal/`: no write, sync, rename, truncate, preallocation, segment recycling or creation. A missing file fails the open, since §2.3's creation is a write. Mutant **RO-WRITES**: the open truncates the WAL (step 5), writes an evicted replayed frame, resumes a drop walk, checkpoints, or retires a meta (§14 step 4a).
- **Every write transaction.** Every transaction is READ ONLY, and a write gets 25006 (C-T0 v8 §2.5).
- **A corrupt meta**, a file that a later format version has written to, and a newest image that fails at open, or that a read error stops, with no previous image, fail as they do for a read-write open (XX001; 55000 for the later format version, whose file no read-only open serves salvage from; or 58030 when the previous free set failed with a read error, or a read error stopped image `k`; §2.2, §3.3, §14 step 4a item 1), and so does a read error that only pass 2's walk meets, with 58030 (§12.5, "Read errors"). A newest image that fails, or that a read error stops, with a previous image is served from it (§14 step 4a).

**No trace.** Closing or crashing a read-only open leaves every file byte-identical. A later read-write open recovers the same state it would have recovered without it.

### 14.2 Outcomes at open
A summary of §2.2, §14 and §14.1; where it and they differ, they rule.

| Case | Read-write open | Read-only open |
|---|---|---|
| No valid meta copy; page 0 invalid and copies of two identities; page 0 and both replicas invalid, not all three unreadable (§2.2) | XX001 | XX001 |
| Every meta place unreadable with page 0 intact and well-formed, or page 0 and both replica blocks unreadable (§2.2) | 58030 | 58030 |
| An intact page 0 of a later format version (`format_version` above 1), whatever the meta places hold, or, with page 0 well-formed or not intact, an intact copy of one with the file's `creation_uuid`, in any place: a file that a later format version has written to (§2.2) | 55000, nothing written; an explicit rollback fails with 55000 too | 55000 |
| Otherwise, an intact page 0 that is not well-formed, or, in any place, an intact copy with the file's `creation_uuid` that is not well-formed or that differs from the file's identity in another static field (§2.2) | XX001 | XX001 |
| A misplaced copy two or more `ckpt_id`s above the newest valid meta (I-META-SLOT; one exactly one above fails no open, §2.2) | XX001 | XX001 |
| A tie with equal `boot_id`s (§2.2) | XX001 | XX001 |
| The newest meta fails step 1's range check | XX001, with no fall-back | XX001 |
| The file's identity carries a `wal_seg_size` below 212 (§12.1) | XX001, nothing written | XX001 |
| The start segment missing (step 3, I-WAL-START) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at `T` |
| WAL loss (step 3a, I-WAL-COVER) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at the valid end |
| A splice (step 3a, I-WAL-CHAIN) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at the splice cut |
| A malformed record, or a short segment that §12.5 counts as damage, the start segment's included (step 3a; I-WAL-STALE, I-WAL-START) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at the damage cut |
| A read error in pass 1's walk (step 3), from meta `k`, or from meta `k−1` in a fall-back | 58030, nothing written beyond the fence and the pass-2 evictions of a fall-back from an image `k` that failed in pass 2, or of a rollback that a read error in pass 2 let proceed; an explicit rollback fails with 58030 too | salvage at the read cut, reported with the read error |
| A read error that only pass 2's walk meets (step 4), from meta `k`, or from meta `k−1` in a fall-back | 58030, nothing written beyond the fence, pass-2 evictions and, once meta `k` is retired (step 4a), the retire write; an explicit rollback fails with 58030 too | 58030 |
| An unmet dependency (step 3b, I-CKPT-DEP) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at the dependency cut |
| The newest image fails at open (an integrity check, or a decoder's refusal of a page that passes it), with a previous image (its free set read at step 2), and the proof holds (step 4a) | opens from meta `k−1`, retiring meta `k` first, and reports | the same state, nothing written |
| The same, and the proof fails | XX001, nothing written beyond the fence (step 1) and step 4a's pass-2 evictions; an explicit rollback opens it | salvage from meta `k−1` |
| The same, and a check against meta `k−1` refuses | that check's refusal | that check's salvage |
| The same, with no previous image (no previous meta, or its free set unread at step 2), or image `k−1` failing too | XX001; 58030 when the free set failed with a read error (step 4a item 1) | the same |
| The newest image meets a read error (58030) at open, with a previous image | 58030, nothing written beyond the fence and step 4a's pass-2 evictions; an explicit rollback opens it | salvage from meta `k−1` |
| The same, and a check against meta `k−1` refuses | 58030, nothing written beyond the fence and step 4a's pass-2 evictions; an explicit rollback gets that check's refusal | that check's salvage |
| The same, with no previous image (no previous meta, or its free set unread at step 2), or image `k−1` failing or meeting a read error too | 58030, nothing written beyond the fence and step 4a's pass-2 evictions; an explicit rollback fails with 58030, or XX001 when image `k−1` meets an integrity failure, not a read error (step 4a item 2) | 58030, or XX001 when image `k−1` meets an integrity failure |
| An explicit rollback whose precondition does not hold | 55000, nothing written beyond the fence (step 1) and the evictions of a pass 2 that found no failure | (read-write only) |
| Otherwise | opens meta `k` | opens meta `k` |

## 15. Invariants

Each invariant needs a test and a named mutant that the test catches (PLAN-v3 §3.3, §8 phase A gate). The "Pinned by" column names the card that writes the test. These are P-written tests for the cards PLAN-v3 §7.1 lists (A-3, A-4, A-7, B-1, B-2). Mutant names taken from `verdicts/X-2.md` keep their meaning there.

**Stand-ins.** A test runs on the card §15.1 names, in the form it states. Where a fixture or a pass condition names machinery that lands on a later card, the earlier card runs a stated stand-in form, and §15.1 names the card that runs the full form, or the row says why its stand-in form is the test's last form (S1's and S5's rules; `t_epoch_reserve_two_exhausters` at A-7a). Only these stand-ins are allowed:
- **S1, the stand-in flip.** Before A-7c, "a flip" or "a flip pending" in a fixture is a test-only thread that takes the epoch gate exclusively (§13 step 0, item 1), records the map's root and its level, opens image `k`'s image-record list and sets `e := k+1`, as item 2's last sentence does (S1 need not record the boot id, which only step 4 writes), and releases the gate (item 3), without the rest of item 2. At A-3c only, before step 2 exists (A-3c-C), the stand-in flip also counts as step 2's map phase having taken, for image `k`, the content of every MapLeaf that is not dirty in the pool at the flip, whether its frame is clean or it has no frame, since that content is then at its current home (§5.6 "When it copies"): such a MapLeaf is mutated in epoch `k+1` without an epoch copy, as it would be once the map phase had taken it. A MapLeaf dirty in the pool at the flip still needs the copy at its first mutation in epoch `k+1`, which A-7a builds, and A-3c refuses that mutation. Before A-3c-C the stand-in flip opens no image-record list: none exists, and nothing would append to one, since every write that appends to it (§13 step 2), a previous-epoch victim's (§5.2), a claim's (§5.6) and step 2's data phase, comes with A-3c-C. Nothing this leaves out is read: no image is written or read before A-3c-C, and until then no MapLeaf home whose epoch is below `e` is freed before step 6 (§4.4). A test whose mutants all fail through a hook, the latch tracker, a deadlock, the step budget, or a stale value that a read in the running store returns and the oracle catches, where the read depends on nothing S1 leaves out of item 2 (`t_latch_scope`'s map variant), is judged in full under S1, because none of its mutants touches what S1 leaves out of item 2.
- **S2, the read-back.** Before its card has a meta (A-7c), recovery (A-7d) or amcheck (A-8), a pass condition that names amcheck, an image that "opens", or "a read at `T` after a crash" is judged by reading back directly through the `Env`:
  - image `k`'s map from the slot step 2 records for its root;
  - each page from the slot that map names, with its CRC checked and its content compared with the oracle's epoch-`k` content;
  - for I-LPID-EXACT, the mapped LPIDs against the pages a test walk reaches from the roots;
  - for I-WAL-COVER, meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` against the valid end A-6b's walk finds (§12.5).
- **S3, a test-only caller or input.** Where a store call's real caller, or an input the store reads, lands on a later card, the test makes the call or supplies the input as that caller will. The cases are:
  - `write_guard`'s first touch, copy and install, which call the reserve's draw and claim (§5.6);
  - settlers (a) and (c), `ROLLBACK TO` and replay, which call A-5's replace and delete (§6.7) or A-4e's cursor close (§6.6);
  - the apply frontier `M` and `visible_ts` (C-T0 v8);
  - GC's `gc_w_used` (§10.5);
  - the watermark `W` that C-T0 v8 publishes, which a drop-cleanup commit's `DropTree` op carries as its `w` (§6.8 step 1, §10.5 "Drops", §12.3);
  - a bulk load's fence commit, unlogged inserts and publishing commit, which carries the `ckpt_id` its forced checkpoint returned (§12.8);
  - an allocation restart's call that grows the map (§4.1 "Growth"), made as A-4a's restart will make it;
  - step 6's release of pending slots (§13 step 6), made as A-7c's checkpoint will make it;
  - a drop-cleanup commit's record;
  - a gated operation made to fail with XX001 (§9.2);
  - an owner's rewrite of its own provisional tag-8 entry (B-1d's write path), which calls A-5's replace (§6.7);
  - an `on` ack (A-6c's group commit), taken as the WAL writer's report that the record is synced (§12.4);
  - a meta written by the test with A-2's codec after step 2, where the card's Depends closure lacks A-7c's meta write (A-8's fixtures);
  - the catalog's list of trees at `S` (B-4a), taken from the directory at `S` (A-7b);
  - the end of a chunked transaction that writes no marker, an abort or a commit with no ts (C-T0 v8 §3.1 step 0), on which it leaves the open-chunk set (§12.6; `t_flip_chunk_race`'s variant *ended*);
  - the open's height bound `H`, lowered by a test-only call as an open with a smaller pool sets it (R-1-memory §4.5; `t_height_bound` (b), whose full form at A-7f reopens with that pool).
- **S4, the test-only sweep step.** A step that names its tree's root through §6.8's root names, with the shape of one §10.4 sweep step. A-7b writes it, and A-7d's tests share it.
- **S5, a second tree before A-7b.** A second root in the same pool, made by a test-only call. Directory and root-table behaviour stays A-7b's. A fixture run on S5 is judged in full when every mutant it judges fails through the latch tracker, a hook or a deadlock and the fixture names no root through the root table or the directory: latch ranks and holds do not depend on how the tree was made, and none of those mutants is applied to directory or root-table code. `t_latch_scope`'s two-tree fixtures are judged in full under this rule (§15.1).
- **S6, the walk scan.** Before a card's Depends closure has A-4e's cursor, "a full scan of every published tree" (§14.1), or of every tree the catalog at `S` names, is a test walk through the open store: from each tree's root, named through §6.8's root names, it reads every reachable page through the store's own page reads and decodes every entry of every leaf, and it succeeds when every read and decode does. A-7d's tests write it. S6 replaces a check, not code, as S2 does: a mutant judged under S6 counts at the owner, and the card that §15.1 names for the full form, which scans with A-4e's cursor, judges it again. No cursor mutant is judged under S6.

A mutant counts where it is applied to the real code it names. Applied to a stand-in's own code it proves the fixture only, as ROOT-NAME-KEPT does on A-7d's stand-in. A forged input, a value no caller produces, set to make a check fire (as `t_cover_ts_cross_check` forges metas), is fault injection, not a stand-in: a mutant that removes the check counts on the card whose code the check is. S2 replaces a check, not code, so a mutant of the owner's code that is judged under S2 counts at the owner, and, where §15.1 names a full form, the card it names judges it again. A form that §15.1 names to a Phase B card is pinned by that card (PLAN-v3 §8, Phase A gate).

**The interleaving harness** (card S-THETA; card A-4c0 builds it, and "A-4c's shuttle harness", "shuttle" and "loom/shuttle" below name it). A test that runs under it runs the store's own code under a model checker that chooses which thread runs at every lock, wait, wake-up, atomic and yield of that code. So every lock, wait, wake-up, atomic and yield of the store code that such a test runs goes through one sync layer, which a store build backs with the platform's primitives and a test build with the checker's, and through no other, except a reference count's atomics (`Arc`'s), which never block and order nothing that store code reads (card S-MU): the checker cannot run code that blocks on a primitive it does not schedule (§5.3 "Fair latches"). Each such test's card states the test's **exploration**, as part of its form: **exhaustive**, every schedule of its fixture, which passes only if the search ends within a stated cap of executions; or **sampled**, a stated scheduler (random, or PCT at a stated depth), seed and number of executions, never below the floor that the harness's card states and its self-tests justify. A test whose fixture's search ends within the harness card's cap, a count of executions that card A-4c0 states with its floor, is explored exhaustively, whatever cap the test states (card S-KAPPA). In a test below, **"under every schedule"** and **"every schedule finishes"** mean: in every execution of the test's exploration, the property holds and the execution ends, neither deadlocked nor past its step budget. A sampled exploration is evidence, not proof: its seed makes each execution replay, and the gate of the card that runs it checks that every mutant the test judges fails within it. For every test run under the harness, that gate is shown the exploration, the step budget and the clean build passing every execution; for every kill that rests on a sampled exploration, also the mutant's first failing execution, by seed and index, replayed, and how many of the executions fail (card S-KAPPA). A kill that fails in fewer of them than the share card A-4c0 states with its floor is fragile across code changes, and its test gains a hook-steered form, in which hooks place the interleaving so that the mutant fails in every execution, as in `t_alloc_restart_progress`'s run (3) and `t_pin_load_race`'s case (k), which are not sampled kills. Card S-THETA's gate named six kills that rest on a sampled exploration: REVERSE-LATCH-LEFT, CKPT-WAIT-HOLDING-MAP, POOL-FLOOR-BLOCK, COUPLING-EARLY-RELEASE, PACK-RIGHT-FIRST, and POISON-LOST-WAKEUP's broadcast-before-mark form. **The step budget** is a count, never a time: under the harness, the number of scheduling steps within which every execution of the test must end; on SimEnv (A-1b), its count of yields; on OS threads, where hooks place the interleaving, a count of the operation's attempts, restarts or wake-ups that a hook keeps. The test states it, with slack over the longest run a correct build makes on its fixture, and a run past it fails, as a deadlock does, so a mutant that waits or retries without bound fails the test and never hangs it; a test whose mutants can block, rather than retry, therefore runs where a block is detected, under the harness or on SimEnv. A pass condition that an operation finishes, completes or returns "within the step budget" holds when the run, which ends only after that operation has, ends within it.

**Timed waits** (card S-XI). A wait that must also end when time passes, such as C-T0 v8 §6.3's park with a pending `lock_timeout` or deadlock search, uses the sync layer's **timed wait**: a condition-variable wait that returns when it is notified, spuriously, or once its deadline has passed, and says which. The caller passes the time left, `deadline − Env::now()`; card A-4c0-T names the primitive. Every build reads one clock for its timed waits and for `Env::now`:
- **the platform** (`StdPrimitives` with `StdEnv`): the wait times out on the platform's monotonic clock, which `StdEnv::now` reads, and the OS's wake latency is its only lateness;
- **the harness** (card A-4c0-T): a **model clock** that only a test's clock steps move, with a test `Env` whose `now` reads it. A clock step moves the model clock to the earliest deadline of a parked timed waiter and wakes that waiter timed-out. The scheduler chooses when a clock step runs, so a deadline may pass at any point of an execution, and a waiter that ignores its deadline stays parked, which the harness reports as a deadlock;
- **SimEnv**, once a card makes SimEnv host the sync layer (none does at this draft: the only backends are `StdPrimitives` and the harness's, and the store's SimEnv tests run one task each; A-8a's port is the first that runs the store on SimEnv's scheduler): when every task is parked and some parked task holds a timed wait, the scheduler moves SimEnv's virtual clock to the earliest such deadline and wakes that wait timed-out, instead of failing the world with DEADLOCK. With no timed waiter, every task parked is DEADLOCK, as before.

A timed wait never returns timed-out before its deadline. Its caller re-tests its predicate on every return, reading `Env::now`, as after any wake. No store or C-T0 v8 code meets a deadline by sleeping in slices or by re-testing in a loop: a deadline is met only by a timed wait. A build whose `Env` and sync-layer backend read different clocks (`MemEnv` with `StdPrimitives`) has no timed wait, and C-T0 v8 §6.3 runs there with its timed terms off, which only test configurations do. The poison mark and the broadcast treat a timed wait as any other wait (§9.2 items 3–4).

| ID | Statement | Pinned by | Named mutants |
|---|---|---|---|
| I-ENC-ORDER | §7.2: entry-key order = (user key asc, ts desc); `upper(k)` separates groups. Mutant ESCAPE-OFF: the entry key's prefix leaves a `0x00` byte of the user key unescaped, so that the entry keys of a user key and of its extension by `0x00` no longer order as the user keys do (card S-MU) | A-4a (port X-2's proptest + directed cases) | TS-ASC, ESCAPE-OFF |
| I-ENC-ROUNDTRIP | §7.2–7.3: decode∘encode = id; undecodable → XX001, never a panic. Mutant DECODE-PANIC: an entry-key decoder takes its 8-byte suffix without checking the key's length, so a key shorter than 8 bytes panics where the rule gives XX001 (card S-MU) | A-4a, fuzz | TOMBSTONE-AS-VALUE, DECODE-PANIC |
| I-TAG-RESERVED | §7.3: reserved tags (9–255), suffix 0, an ordinary tag with suffix MAX and a provisional tag without it → XX001 on every read path | A-4e | RESERVED-AS-VALUE |
| I-PROV-READ | §8: provisional head first, resolver called under the leaf latch, candidate rule; dead entries never served | B-1d (C-T0 v8 §11.2 owns the txn side) | ORDINARY-FIRST, DEAD-AS-LIVE |
| I-SEEK | §8.1: one descent; newest version ≤ S; prefix-checked | A-4e | TS-ASC, SEEK-NO-PREFIX-CHECK |
| I-NEWEST | §8.2: newest entry of the group, ts and liveness | B-1c | NEWEST-SKIPS-TOMB, UNIQUE-TOMB-LIVE |
| I-SCAN | §8.3: bounds table; one version per key; tombstones omitted; streaming | A-4e | SCAN-NO-SKIP, SCAN-HI-INCL, SCAN-TOMB-EMIT |
| I-CURSOR | §6.6: a cursor parks before each storage call returns, holding no latch and no pin, and resumes by re-descent through a validated root name (§6.8) at its parked position, never at a remembered LPID; a parked position is after a group, or inside one with the group's carried state. Test `t_cursor_park_equiv` (A-4e on stand-ins, with A-4b packs racing; full forms B-3, B-1d, B-2c, R-1; §15.1): a scan parks after every entry while writers add versions above `S`, a GC sweep packs and merges its leaves, and another tree is dropped and recreated on reused LPIDs; its output equals the oracle scan at `S`, and in the SER variant so do its recorded edges. A mid-group variant: a group spans several leaves, its head is a provisional entry whose owner is `Committed(c)` with `c ≤ S`, finalize is held back, and the per-call bound fires while the scan skips the versions above `S`; a point-read variant runs a SER point read over the same group, finalize still held back, with a bound smaller than the group. A scroll variant fetches forward, parks, fetches backward, parks and fetches forward again; each fetch equals the oracle's from the same boundary. A close variant closes a parked cursor through `ROLLBACK TO`; its batch buffer is freed and R-1's per-session account returns to its value before the open. At A-4e the forms run on stand-ins: in the main form coupled deletes pack and merge the scan's leaves (A-4b, §6.5) and a split then takes each freed LPID (§4.2's lowest-first), so a remembered LPID names another page, and the output equals the oracle scan at `S`; the mid-group form runs over a group of ordinary versions that spans several leaves, with the per-call bound firing inside it (i) after the newest version `≤ S` was found and (ii) while the scan still skips versions above `S`, and its output equals the oracle's, one version per key; the point-read form runs a SER-mode point read over the same group, in cases (i) and (ii), with a bound smaller than the group, and returns the oracle's version; the scroll form runs as written; the close form closes a parked cursor through the store's close call, which `ROLLBACK TO` will use (S3), and a test hook sees its batch buffer freed; each resume goes through the tree's root LPID, which never changes (I-ROOT-STABLE). At A-4e, PARK-DROPS-CANDIDATE is applied to the found flag that a park inside a group carries (§6.6), so A-4e carries that flag in the parked position and does not recompute it from the resume key; recomputed, the flag leaves the mutant nothing to drop at A-4e. A resume that keeps only the key takes the newest version `≤ S` either as found, which fails case (ii) because the key's version is missing, or as not found, which fails case (i) because a second, older version of the key is returned, where A-4e parks inside the group after a find; where it parks after the group instead (§6.6), that second reading is equivalent at A-4e. The mutant's other half, the head's candidate version, is judged at B-1d. CURSOR-STALE-LPID (resume by a remembered LPID) fails it; PARK-DROPS-CANDIDATE (a mid-group park keeps only the resume key) fails the mid-group and point-read variants, which return a version older than `c`; SCROLL-RESUME-SKIP (a direction change resumes past the boundary) fails the scroll variant; BATCH-KEPT-AFTER-CLOSE (a closed cursor's batch buffer is not freed) fails the close variant | A-4e (with A-4b packs racing; stand-ins), B-3, B-1d, B-2c, R-1 (full forms, §15.1) | CURSOR-STALE-LPID, PARK-DROPS-CANDIDATE, SCROLL-RESUME-SKIP, BATCH-KEPT-AFTER-CLOSE |
| I-TREE | §6.1: key order and subtree bounds after every SMO. Mutants SPLIT-OFF-BY-ONE: a leaf split's separator is the left page's last key, not the right page's first; SEP-NOT-UPDATED: a split child's non-root parent receives no separator for it (card S-MU) | A-4a/A-4b (proptest vs `BTreeMap`) | SPLIT-OFF-BY-ONE, SEP-NOT-UPDATED, PACK-SEP-STALE |
| I-LATCH | §5.3/§6.2: coupling; one order (directory pages only by a directory change, then one tree's pages by depth and left to right, then map pages by radix rank), in which a thread blocks only on a latch ranked after every latch it holds, a claim takes no map latch and a map page's write never holds the page while it latches the parent; one call and one tree per latch hold, with two stated exceptions (`try_latch` acquirers, a stale root name's first latch); task-fair latches whose bypass a count bounds, on the store's sync layer (§5.3); no recursive latch; no page read and no wait for a load in a reader's hold; no storage call yields holding a latch or a page being loaded (card S-KAPPA). §6.8 "Root names": only an operation that changes the directory latches a directory page, and a root name comes from the root table and is validated under the root's latch. Test `t_dir_root_no_stall` (A-7b, with A-4c's shuttle harness and the stand-in step S4; B-3 with its sweep; §15.1), a regression for the directory leaving the hot path: trees X, D and Y have their entries in one directory leaf. A chain operation holds X's leaf root; a sweep step of D that named D's root while D was `live` is paused under that root's latch, while DropTree(D)'s last step holds the directory leaf exclusively and is held up on D's root; a CreateTree queues on that leaf. A reader and a writer of Y finish within the step budget under every schedule, and a hook asserts that no operation that does not change the directory latches a directory page. DIR-LATCH-ON-READ (an operation that does not change the directory latches a directory page) fails it through the hook, deterministically. Test `t_freeset_lock_order` (A-3c, with A-4c's shuttle harness): allocations pin MapLeafs under the free-set lock while dirty evictions allocate slots and a pool at its minimum forces eviction; its fixture evicts the MapLeaf of the lowest free LPID, and a variant has no free LPID and `next_lpid` at a MapLeaf boundary. A hook asserts no page read, eviction, page creation or frame latch taken while the lock is held (the allocation already holds `P`'s), and no take of the free-set lock by a thread holding the page table, and every schedule finishes. FREESET-LOCK-IO (an allocation creates or reads a MapLeaf under the lock) and FREESET-LOCK-INVERT (a dirty eviction takes the free-set lock while holding the page table) fail it. Test `t_latch_scope` (A-4c; its cursor fixtures and reverse variant A-4e, with A-4c's tracker; §15.1): a per-thread latch tracker asserts, at every storage-call return and every yield point, that the thread holds no latch and no pin; at every blocking acquisition of a tree page, that the thread holds no latch of another tree; and at every blocking acquisition, that the thread holds no latch ranked at or after the one it requests (§5.3 "Order": map pages rank after every tree page, and pages of different trees are unranked). Fixtures: an index scan with table lookups, a self-join behind a queued writer, two cursors that each read the other's tree, and two threads that each hold a leaf exclusively and read the other's tree. A reverse variant runs a reverse scan across a left-to-right pack. A map variant runs a pool at its minimum through a checkpoint, so that dirty MapLeafs are evicted and step 2's map phase runs, while two writers that each hold a MapLeaf's latch in `write_guard` exhaust the reserve; a hook pauses one MapLeaf's eviction after it releases the MapLeaf's latch and before it sets the parent's ref, while another thread looks up an LPID whose entry in that MapLeaf changed since its old slot. In the same pause the evicted MapLeaf is a previous-epoch victim (§5.2) of checkpoint `k`, and step 2's map phase builds it from its frame; a hook asserts that the map phase sets no current ref. A checkpoint variant has step 2 wait on a frame whose holder records into a MapLeaf. A floor variant runs a pool at its minimum, a gated writer queued on a root with a flip pending, and a holder of that root that needs a frame. Every schedule finishes. CURSOR-LATCH-ACROSS-CALL and CURSOR-PIN-ACROSS-CALL (a cursor keeps a latch or a pin past the call's return) and LATCH-ACROSS-YIELD (a call yields holding a latch) fail it through the tracker; LOAD-ACROSS-YIELD (card S-KAPPA: a load yields, at an `await` of its read or at an N10 step boundary, while its page is being loaded) fails it through the tracker as well, at the yield inside the map variant's load of the evicted MapLeaf, where the load holds its frame's pin; NESTED-READ-UNDER-LATCH (a thread reads another tree, or re-latches its own, while holding latches) fails it through the tracker and deadlocks the two-leaf and self-join fixtures; REVERSE-LATCH-LEFT (a reverse cursor latches the previous leaf while holding its leaf) deadlocks the reverse variant; MAP-PARENT-UNDER-CHILD (a map page's write latches its parent while holding the page) fails the map variant through the tracker; MAP-DROP-BEFORE-REF (an evicted map page leaves the page table before its parent's ref is set) fails the map variant: the paused lookup reloads the old slot and returns a stale entry; MAP-REF-SET-STALE (the map phase makes the slot it writes a map page's current home) fails the map variant through the hook, and the paused eviction's ref then names a slot already reusable; CKPT-WAIT-HOLDING-MAP (step 2 waits on a frame while holding a map latch) deadlocks the checkpoint variant; POOL-FLOOR-BLOCK (a thread holding a latch waits for an unpin instead of releasing and restarting) deadlocks the floor variant. Test `t_root_latch_fair` (A-3b, with A-4c's shuttle harness; its cold variant A-4a, re-run with a cursor at A-4e, §15.1): a stream of overlapping readers holds tree X's root shared while a writer queues; the writer is granted within a bypass budget, a bound with slack on the later requests granted before it, not their arrival order, so a strict-FIFO latch passes too, and no shared request that arrives while the writer waits is granted before it (§5.3 "Fair latches"), which the test checks beside the budget (card S-MU). The harness runs the latch type's own code, so the budget counts grants in every schedule the test explores, and no time enters it (§5.3). A writer variant runs a stream of writers on X's root while a reader queues; the reader is granted within the same budget. A latch-level variant drives the latch type directly: with a writer queued on a frame that a reader holds shared, `try_latch` in either mode fails, including at the instant the reader releases. A cold variant has a reader under X's root meet a non-resident child while a gated writer queues on the root with a flip pending; in its second run (card S-KAPPA) the reader meets that child being loaded by another thread, which `t_pin_load_race`'s hooks hold at *published* until the reader reaches *waiting* (§15 I-PIN-CURRENT); a hook asserts that no reader reads a page, or reaches *waiting*, while it holds a latch, and the flip completes within the step budget. LATCH-READER-PREF (a waiting writer does not block new shared grants) fails the reader stream; LATCH-WRITER-PREF (writers always overtake waiting readers) fails the writer variant; TRYLATCH-BYPASS (`try_latch` succeeds over a queued exclusive waiter) fails the latch-level variant; READER-IO-UNDER-LATCH (a reader loads a page while holding its parent's latch) fails the cold variant through the hook; READER-WAIT-UNDER-LATCH (card S-KAPPA: a reader that finds a page being loaded waits for that load holding its parent's latch, instead of releasing it and restarting) fails its second run through the hook. §12.4: the append critical section is a leaf lock, with no lock, I/O or wait under it, and a reservation that hops does nothing about the next segment. Test `t_append_section_leaf` (A-6c, A-7c; §15.1): at A-6c, a tracker marks every entry to and exit from the append critical section, and a hook asserts that no thread inside it makes an `Env` call, takes a lock or waits, while reservations of every kind run, across hops into segments whose creation a hook holds until the hopping reservation has returned, and the test fails if it has not returned within its step budget, which here is a count the tracker keeps of the sync layer's locks, waits, atomics and yields made inside the section in one entry, stated with slack over a correct reservation's (§15 "The step budget"; card S-KAPPA), since every lock, wait, atomic and yield of store code goes through that layer; at A-7c, with A-4c's shuttle harness, a commit's reservation hops into a segment whose creation a hook holds, a flip starts once that reservation has reached the hop, and the hook releases the creation only after step 0 has released the gate and a gated operation started after the flip has completed: under every schedule the test finishes, the commit is acked and the checkpoint completes. APPEND-SECTION-IO fails A-6c's form, its I/O form through the hook and its wait form, however it waits, through the hook or the step budget's count while the creation is held, and deadlocks A-7c's: step 0 waits in the section on the held creation while it holds the gate | A-4c (loom/shuttle, `t_latch_scope`'s tracker, two-leaf, map, checkpoint and floor forms), A-4e (`t_latch_scope`'s cursor fixtures and reverse variant), A-3b (`t_root_latch_fair`), A-4a (its cold variant), A-4e (the cold variant with a cursor), A-3c (`t_freeset_lock_order`), A-7b (`t_dir_root_no_stall`), B-3 (`t_dir_root_no_stall` on its sweep), A-6c and A-7c (`t_append_section_leaf`) | COUPLING-EARLY-RELEASE, PACK-RIGHT-FIRST, DIR-LATCH-ON-READ, FREESET-LOCK-IO, FREESET-LOCK-INVERT, CURSOR-LATCH-ACROSS-CALL, CURSOR-PIN-ACROSS-CALL, NESTED-READ-UNDER-LATCH, LATCH-ACROSS-YIELD, REVERSE-LATCH-LEFT, MAP-PARENT-UNDER-CHILD, MAP-DROP-BEFORE-REF, MAP-REF-SET-STALE, CKPT-WAIT-HOLDING-MAP, POOL-FLOOR-BLOCK, LATCH-READER-PREF, LATCH-WRITER-PREF, TRYLATCH-BYPASS, READER-IO-UNDER-LATCH, APPEND-SECTION-IO, READER-WAIT-UNDER-LATCH, LOAD-ACROSS-YIELD |
| I-TIMED-WAIT | §15 "Timed waits": a timed wait returns timed-out only once its deadline has passed on the build's clock, and it does return once the deadline has passed: on the platform within the OS's wake latency, under the harness at the clock step that passes it, and on SimEnv when every task is parked; a notify ends it as it ends an untimed wait. Test `t_timed_wait` (A-4c0-T). Under the harness, a waiter with deadline `D` and a clock thread that steps the model clock: the waiter is not woken timed-out by a step below `D`, is woken timed-out by the step that reaches `D`, and a notify before `D` ends its wait not timed-out. On OS threads with `StdPrimitives` and `StdEnv`, a wait of `d` returns timed-out with `StdEnv::now` at least `d` later, and a notify made once the waiter is parked (a hook reports it) ends the wait not timed-out; a watchdog fails the form if a wait has not returned by `10 d`, a bound on failure only. TIMED-EARLY (a wait returns timed-out before its deadline), TIMED-NEVER (a wait never times out) and TIMED-NOTIFY-LOST (a notify during a timed wait is not seen until the deadline) fail it. Test `t_sim_timed_advance` (A-8a): on the first SimEnv backend of the sync layer, two tasks park, one in a timed wait: the world does not fail, the clock moves to that deadline and the waiter returns timed-out; with neither waiting timed, the world fails DEADLOCK. SIM-TIMED-DEADLOCK (a world whose parked tasks include a timed waiter fails DEADLOCK) fails it | A-4c0-T, A-8a | TIMED-EARLY, TIMED-NEVER, TIMED-NOTIFY-LOST, SIM-TIMED-DEADLOCK |
| I-ROOT-STABLE | §6.3: a tree's root LPID never changes. Mutant ROOT-MOVES: a root split keeps the left half in the root's page and writes the new root to a new page, so the tree's root LPID changes and nothing records it (card S-MU) | A-4a/A-4b (in process), A-7f (reopen; §15.1) | ROOT-MOVES |
| I-PACK | §6.5: entries preserved; merge iff it fits in `F_PACK·U`; never splits | A-4b | PACK-DROP-LAST, PACK-OVERFILL |
| I-ENTRY-UNIQUE / I-REPLAY-IDEM | §9.1, §14: identical re-insert (chains compared by content) is a no-op, a different one XX001; recovering twice gives the same state | B-1c, A-7d (incl. the double-crash test; an overflow value in a partially applied commit) | REPLAY-DUP-CONFLICT, DUP-DIFFERENT-ACCEPTED, CHAIN-LPID-COMPARED |
| I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE | §12.7, §14: within one chunked transaction the last op per key wins, `revert` deletes; replay twice gives the same state | A-7d, B-1d | SUPERSEDE-AS-CONFLICT |
| I-KEY-SIZE | §3.2: an entry key over `K_MAX` is refused with 54000 at write-set entry; apply never sees one | A-2 (formula and values), B-1a (the write path calls the check) | SIZE-CHECK-OFF |
| I-FAIL-STOP | §9.2: a 58030/53100/53200/XX001 inside a gated operation, a checkpoint step, a background store job or the WAL path (a WAL write or `sync_data`, a segment's creation, preallocation, recycling rename or `wal/` directory sync, recovery's step-5 truncation) poisons the store; a client read's XX001 never does; an engine invariant violation, XX000, poisons it wherever it is raised, a client read included, as a panic in store code does. The mark is made before the first release, a panicking thread's included, and every acquisition checks it; a mutex of the store's that unwinding releases is poisoned, and a thread that takes it returns the poison's error, XX000 if no mark was made; on wasm32 a trap stops the browser handle (card S-KAPPA). Every blocking wait, the lock-table waits and a gateless thread's wait for a pending flip included, has it in its predicate, under the wait's own mutex, and sleeps by releasing that mutex in one step, re-testing on every wake. The broadcast comes after every latch, pin, the gate, the settle stripe and every internal mutex are released, and reaches every wait queue, each live transaction's waiter list included. After poison, every call fails, R-1-memory §8's report and a test build's seams aside, every waiter returns, and no write starts: one already past its thread's check of the mark may complete (card S-MU). Test `t_gated_xx001_poisons`: a damaged referenced leaf on an apply's path; every commit waiter returns the error within the test's step budget, and no task is left parked. Its **lock-waiter variant**: a txn waits in the lock table on a key held by a commit that sits behind the poisoned apply, so the holder is never released; the waiter must return the error (POISON-LOST-WAKEUP and POISON-WAITER-PARKED leave it parked; pinned in B-1c and B-1b). Its **WAL variant**: the group-commit writer's `sync_data` fails (58030); the store is poisoned and every waiter on `durable_end` returns the error. WAL-ERR-NOT-POISON (an error on the WAL path does not poison the store) fails it: at A-7c, the first card whose Depends closure holds both the group-commit writer (A-6c) and the poison mark (A-3c), through a test `Env` whose `sync_data` fails under the writer, where every waiter on `written_end` or `durable_end`, every later append and a later call into the store that is not the writer's, a read made before any checkpoint or background job runs, return the error and a write hook sees nothing more written, so that a writer that keeps its own error but leaves the mark unset fails it through that read; and at B-1c with commit waiters (§15.1). An XX001 that the drop walk meets on an unmapped root under an unchanged entry poisons too: `t_root_name_reuse`'s walk-poison variant (A-7d; §15 I-LINK-ATOMIC), which WALK-XX001-NO-POISON fails. Test `t_client_read_xx001_no_poison` (B-1c): a client read of that leaf gets XX001, then an unrelated commit and a checkpoint both succeed. Test `t_poison_before_unlatch` (shuttle; A-3c with an evictor and a stand-in operation, A-4c with a reader and a split; §15.1): a reader that races the failing SMO gets the error, never the half-changed page; so does an evictor's `try_latch`. Its invariant and panic variants (A-3c) fail a dirty eviction, A-3c's own code, after its victim's write, on OS threads with hooks placing the race: once a reader waits on the victim's latch (a hook in the latch's wait reports it), a hook in the eviction raises an engine invariant violation, as the eviction's own checks would, or a panic, which the evicting thread's test code catches; the reader gets the error, XX000, and is never served the page, and every later store call returns XX000. Its read variant: a hook raises an engine invariant violation in a client read's pin, and every later store call returns XX000. In each variant A-3c's own code picks the code: the hook raises the violation as the eviction's or the pin's own checks would, or panics inside the eviction. INVARIANT-NOT-POISON fails the invariant and read variants, and PANIC-UNPOISONED the panic variant: the reader is served the page, or a later call succeeds. INVARIANT-AS-CORRUPT fails all three: the error is XX001. Its mutex variant (A-3c; card S-KAPPA): a hook pauses A-3c's own code inside the page table's lock, and once a second thread is blocked on that lock (a hook in the sync layer's lock reports it) the hook panics, and the panicking thread's test code catches the panic; the second thread returns XX000 and acts on nothing the lock guards (a hook at the start of its critical section never fires), and every later store call returns XX000. MUTEX-POISON-IGNORED fails it: that hook fires, or a later call succeeds. Test `t_poison_wakeup_race` (shuttle; A-3c for its flip-waiter variant, A-4c, B-1c): a waiter tests its predicate concurrently with the poisoner's mark and broadcast; under every schedule it returns the error. POISON-LOST-WAKEUP (a wait predicate omits the mark, so a woken waiter re-tests only its own condition and sleeps again) fails it, and so does a broadcast made before the mark. Its flip-waiter variant (A-3c, under the interleaving harness with `t_pin_load_race`'s hooks and S1 for the flip; card S-KAPPA): as `t_pin_load_race`'s case (k) until T1 is paused at *restarting*, which then releases it at once, while a hook holds T2 at *missed* when it looks again; once T1 sleeps in its wait for the flip (the sync layer's wait reports it), T2 fails with XX001 (S3); S1's exclusive acquisition returns the error without flipping, and T1 returns XX001, every schedule ending within the step budget. FLIP-WAITER-UNWOKEN fails it: T1 sleeps on, a deadlock the harness reports | B-1c (commit waiters, COMMIT's error, the WAL variant); A-7c (the WAL variant: the writer's waits and a later read outside the writer); B-1b, the lock-table card, with B-2b for `wait_for` (the lock-waiter variant); A-3c, A-4c (the latch and `try_latch` check, shuttle); A-3c (the mutex variant, and `t_poison_wakeup_race`'s flip-waiter variant under the harness); A-7d (the drop walk's XX001: `t_root_name_reuse`'s walk-poison variant) | POISON-IGNORED, XX001-NOT-POISON, CLIENT-READ-POISONS, POISON-WAITER-PARKED, POISON-AFTER-UNLATCH, POISON-LOST-WAKEUP, WAL-ERR-NOT-POISON, WALK-XX001-NO-POISON, INVARIANT-NOT-POISON, PANIC-UNPOISONED, INVARIANT-AS-CORRUPT, MUTEX-POISON-IGNORED, FLIP-WAITER-UNWOKEN |
| I-GC-READEQ | §10.2: every GC mutation is read-equivalent at every S ≥ the published W, including a sweep's own delete of each chain-bearing entry (§10.4, "Chains first"). Test `t_sweep_step_shape` (B-3): a sweep over a tree of height ≥ 2 with a toast-region leaf and a leaf of maximum-size values, each holding superseded chain-bearing versions and dead tag-8 entries next to non-chain garbage, with every chain evicted, reads at `S ≥ W` between its gated operations, and a hook that counts each gated operation's chain frees and, at each chain page read, checks the latches the thread holds. The fixture also holds (a) an elided-tombstone group that ends within the step's `L` and `R` and whose older versions hold chains, with no version above `w`, read at `S ≥ W` between each two of the gated operations that delete its entries; and (b) a writer, run by a hook between the step's end and the delete of a dead tag-8 entry on key `k`, that settles that entry (settler (c)) and writes its own current-boot tag-8 entry on `k`, then stays `Open`. An operation frees at most one chain, no chain page is read under an inner page's latch, the step that packs frees no chain, every read is unchanged, the writer's entry and its chain survive the sweep (its owner reads its own value, and amcheck finds the chain mapped and referenced), and every read of the tombstone's key at `S ≥ W` finds no version. SWEEP-CHAINS-UNDER-P (a step deletes its chain-bearing entries and frees their chains in its own mutation, under `P`'s latch) fails it through the hook. CHAIN-DELETE-BY-KEY (the delete of a marked chain-bearing entry matches its entry key alone, with no identity check) fails it through (b): it deletes the writer's entry and frees its chain. STEP-MUTATE-BEFORE-CHAINS (the step deletes its non-chain marks, the ending group's tombstone among them, in its mutation, and only then deletes the chain-bearing ones) fails it through (a): a read between the tombstone's delete and an older version's sees that version | B-3 (+ G0 re-point, B-7) | GC-DROPS-VISIBLE, G-WATERMARK+1, G-RESURRECT, SWEEP-CHAINS-UNDER-P, CHAIN-DELETE-BY-KEY |
| I-GC-ORDER | §10.2/§10.4: an elided tombstone is deleted after every older version of its key, including the chain-bearing ones a sweep deletes in their own gated operations (§10.4, "Chains first") | B-3 (a group spanning leaves, read between sweep steps or across a checkpoint and crash; `t_sweep_step_shape`'s fixture (a), I-GC-READEQ row) | G-TOMB-FIRST, STEP-MUTATE-BEFORE-CHAINS |
| I-GC-COMPLETE | §10.2: after a quiescent sweep, at most one version ≤ w per key, and it is live | B-3 (the §10.2 directed test, with a reader) | GC-KEEPS-TOMB |
| I-GC-LOCAL | §10.3: page-local prune acts only on what the leaf proves; it frees no chain and elides a tombstone only when no older version of its group holds one. Test `t_prune_split_shape` (B-3): a tree of height ≥ 2 whose target leaf is a full toast-region leaf. Its superseded chunk versions (tag 4) each have a newer `≤ w` version on the leaf, and the leaf also holds dead tag-8 entries, an elided-tombstone candidate group that ends on the leaf with a tag-4 older version, non-chain garbage, and every chain evicted. An insert into that leaf prunes and splits. A hook counts each gated operation's chain frees and LPID frees and, at each page read, checks the latches the thread holds. The splitting insert frees no chain and no LPID and reads no Overflow page. Every chain of a pruned-around version stays mapped and referenced (amcheck). A read of the tombstone's key at `S ≥ W` after the prune still finds the tombstone. A sweep afterwards deletes each chain-bearing entry in its own gated operation, and I-GC-COMPLETE holds after it. Its eviction variant runs the prune at eviction of the same leaf, nested inside another tree's gated insert, with the same checks. PRUNE-FREES-CHAINS (the prune deletes a chain-bearing version or a dead tag-8 entry and frees its chain in the splitting insert's gated operation) fails it through the hook: it counts a chain free, and an Overflow page read under `P`'s exclusive latch. PRUNE-ELIDES-OVER-CHAIN (the prune skips a group's chain-bearing older version and still elides the group's tombstone) fails it through the read of the tombstone's key at `S ≥ W`, which finds the older version (I-GC-READEQ, I-GC-ORDER). PRUNE-EVICT-FREES-CHAIN (only the split-time prune skips chains; the prune at eviction still frees them, under the outer operation's hold) fails its eviction variant: the hook counts a chain free inside the outer insert | B-3 | PRUNE-FIRST-ENTRY, PRUNE-FREES-CHAINS, PRUNE-ELIDES-OVER-CHAIN, PRUNE-EVICT-FREES-CHAIN |
| I-SPACE | §11: quiescent fill bound at every height, except rightmost children and the reported `skips`; B-3's W4-shape acceptance ≤ 1.5× in tree bytes (reachable pages × `page_size`) | A-4b (bound, incl. trees of height ≥ 4), B-3 (acceptance) | PACK-OFF, SKIP-UNCOUNTED, SWEEP-PACK-LEFT, CASCADE-ONE-LEVEL, JUNCTION-SKIP |
| I-GCW | §10.5: meta `gc_w` bounds the image's GC and every DropTree mark in it, and is `≤ T`; a violation at meta write is fail-stop, with XX000, or with XX001 at a boot checkpoint, where `gc_w_used` still holds what recovery read. A mark raises `gc_w_used` to its `w`: for a logged DropTree, the `w` its op carries, the published `W` that the drop-cleanup job read (§12.3, §6.8 step 1), in normal operation and when pass 2 replays it alike (§14 step 4); so after open `gc_w_used`, as recovery leaves it, is above the retiring DDL's commit ts of every retired tree whose mark, freed pages or removed entry the recovered state holds, and C-T0 v8's watermark starts there (§17). Test `t_drop_restart_floor` (§10.5 "Drops") | A-7c (GCW-NOT-RECORDED, and GCW-BOUND-SKIP by a forged `gc_w_used > T`; the drop's raise through the meta, and DROP-GCW-AT-TS), A-7b (the mark's raise, checked directly), A-7d (the replayed raise, checked exactly, the restart forms, and BOOT-GCW-AS-INVARIANT by a forged meta's `gc_w > T`), A-6a (the op's `w` in the codec), B-3 (restart floor; §15.1), B-6 (`t_drop_restart_floor` in full, with its AS OF checks) | GCW-NOT-RECORDED, GCW-BOUND-SKIP, DROP-NO-GCW, DROP-GCW-AT-WALK, DROP-GCW-AT-TS, REPLAY-DROP-NO-GCW, REPLAY-DROP-AT-TS, DROP-OP-W-ZERO, DROP-MARK-AT-PUBLISHED-W, BOOT-GCW-AS-INVARIANT |
| I-LPID-EXACT | §6.8: in every image, crash images included, mapped LPIDs = reachable pages; no leak on pack, drop or chain free, and no freed page written to map its LPID again (§4.2 "Freeing"). "Quiescent" describes amcheck's reading, not an exemption | A-8 amcheck, A-4b (in process; S2), B-6, A-7d (images of drops and chains; §15.1), A-7f (images of packs, one of them a checkpoint's whose flip follows a pack's free while the freed page's frame is still resident; card S-KAPPA) | LEAK-ON-PACK, DROP-ENTRY-SPLIT, FREED-FRAME-WRITTEN |
| I-LINK-ATOMIC | §5.6, §6.7, §6.8, §12.8, §14 step 4: every gated operation leaves the reference graph closed. An LPID is mapped in the gated operation that inserts its first reference and unmapped in the one that removes its last, after removing it, so every image satisfies I-LPID-EXACT with no special case. An operation restarts only before its first mutation. DropTree deletes each chain-bearing entry with its chain in its own gated operation; its last step removes the entry, then frees the root, in one gated operation; one walk runs per tree; a root LPID is named through the root table, validated under the root's latch and not kept (§6.8); pass 2 runs no walk. The walk's code is A-7b's; A-7d's tests call it directly, and B-6 runs it as the drop job and the boot resume. A split reserves its pages and its allocations' MapLeafs before its prune and decides from the prune's marks whether it splits (§6.4); an allocation pins a resident MapLeaf or restarts (§5.6); an operation pins the MapLeaf of each clean page it will dirty before its first mutation and keeps it across a restart (§5.6, card S-MU); a replace, a settle or a chain-freeing delete outside the sweep frees its old chain holding only the leaf (§6.7); a chain build holds only the leaf (§6.7). Test `t_drop_every_cut` (A-7d, B-6): a flip at every boundary between a DropTree's gated steps, with and without a concurrent split that takes the freed LPID. Its fixture holds chain-bearing entries in a non-root leaf and in a leaf root, an evicted directory page with a flip pending, an evicted root and an evicted MapLeaf of an LPID the walk frees, each with a flip pending, in the last step and in an unlink step, a running sweep of the tree paused between its steps until the walk ends, and a second DropTree during the walk. Every image passes amcheck and opens, no other tree loses a page, and one walk runs. DROP-ENTRY-SPLIT (directory root, unmap; E4's probe: 28 of 51 C-XX rounds), DROP-UNLINK-SPLIT (child pointer, unmap), DROP-RESTART-MID-OP (the flip pending lands in its restart), DROP-RESTART-AFTER-REMOVE and UNLINK-RESTART-AFTER-UNLINK, which are DROP-RESTART-MID-OP's realistic form (a forgotten pin: the flip lands in the restart, and the image fails I-LPID-EXACT, amcheck finding a mapped, unreachable page), ROOT-NAME-KEPT (the paused sweep then prunes another tree's page) and DROP-SECOND-WALK fail it. At A-7d the paused sweep is S4, the stand-in step A-7b writes, which follows §6.8's root names; `t_drop_step_shape` runs on the same fixture and shares it. ROOT-NAME-KEPT applied to the stand-in proves the fixture only. At B-6 both tests re-run with B-3's real sweep, and ROOT-NAME-KEPT is applied to B-3's sweep code and must fail there. A-7d's kill does not count toward the sweep. Test `t_drop_step_shape` (A-7d, B-6): on the same fixture, a hook counts each gated operation's frees and page reads, and at each LPID free reads the pages the operation holds. An operation frees at most one chain, an unlink step or a last step frees none and reads no page after its first mutation, and no held page or entry names the LPID being freed. DROP-LEAF-CHAINS and DROP-FREE-BEFORE-REMOVE fail it. Its fixture also holds a directory leaf that the removal leaves below `U/4`, and the hook asserts that the last step latches no directory page beside its own leaf; DROP-LAST-STEP-PACKS fails it. Test `t_split_reserve_order` (A-4a): a full leaf with prunable non-chain garbage, too little to fit the insert's cell, under a height-≥ 2 tree, the MapLeaf of the next free LPID evicted, and a flip pending; a variant whose split also splits `P` evicts the MapLeaf of its second LPID. A hook at each gate release and each page read asserts that no restart follows a mutation and no page is read under an inner exclusive latch. SPLIT-RESERVE-AFTER-PRUNE (the split prunes, then decides to split or reserves, meets the evicted MapLeaf and restarts, releasing the gate) and ALLOC-READ-UNDER-P (the allocation reads its evicted MapLeaf in place under `P`'s exclusive latch) fail it through the hook. Its second variant has a full leaf whose marks free just enough room for the insert's cell: the hook asserts that the insert allocates no LPID and pins no MapLeaf. SPLIT-RESERVE-ALWAYS fails it there: an insert into an unsafe leaf allocates and pins its split's reservation before it knows it splits. Its dirty variant (card S-MU): the full leaf and `P` are clean, their MapLeafs evicted and mapping no dirty page, and the MapLeaf of the next free LPID resident; the insert splits the leaf, dirtying both, and the hook asserts as above. DIRTY-MAPLEAF-UNDER-P (an operation that holds an inner page's exclusive latch loads the cold MapLeaf of a clean page at that page's first mutation) fails it through the hook: a MapLeaf is read under `P`'s exclusive latch. Test `t_alloc_restart_progress` (A-4a, with A-3c; the race variant's image check A-7f; its pool-floor variant §5.4 and I-POOL-BOUND; §15.1): a split whose allocation restarts; during each restart a hook frees a lower LPID under an evicted MapLeaf. The split finishes within the restart bound §5.6 states. A variant has no free LPID, `next_lpid` at a MapLeaf boundary and every MapLeaf evicted; the hook asserts no page is read or created under an inner exclusive latch. ALLOC-RETRY-LOWEST (the retry takes the lowest free LPIDs afresh) fails the first; ALLOC-NEW-MAPLEAF-IN-PLACE (the allocation creates the new MapLeaf under `P`'s exclusive latch) fails the variant. A dirty-MapLeaf variant (card S-MU): the split's leaf and `P` are clean, with their MapLeafs evicted, and the MapLeaf of its LPID resident; at each restart, once the restart has pinned what it needs and before its retry takes the gate, a hook evicts every MapLeaf no pin holds, at most three times; the split finishes after at most two restarts, one for each MapLeaf it found cold. DIRTY-MAPLEAF-PIN-DROPPED (a restart keeps no pin of the MapLeaf of a page it will dirty but the one it found cold, as draft 7.20's "keeping no pin but the page it found cold and its reservation's pages and MapLeafs" read) fails it: each retry finds the MapLeaf the hook evicted cold again and restarts, four restarts in all. A race variant restarts two allocations on the same missing MapLeaf, with a pool at its minimum so that obtaining a frame evicts a dirty page: the first creates the MapLeaf, and its retry maps an LPID there before the second latches the MapInner; the second finds that MapLeaf, pins it and maps the next LPID. It runs three ways (§5.6 "Restart"): (1) an eviction has written the first LPID's page, setting its entry in the MapLeaf, while the MapLeaf itself is unwritten, its ref null and its frame in the page table, and the second finds that frame; (2) the MapLeaf has been written and dropped, so the second finds the ref set and loads the MapLeaf through it; (3) as (2), under the interleaving harness, with a pin of the MapLeaf held by a hook after its frame is in the page table and before it reads the ref, so that the second finds the MapLeaf being loaded, and released once the second waits for that load. Every image passes amcheck, both LPIDs stay mapped, and the hook asserts that no page is evicted or read under the MapInner's exclusive latch. MAPLEAF-CREATE-UNCONDITIONAL (the second creates a MapLeaf anyway and replaces the ref) fails it through amcheck (I-LPID-EXACT); MAPLEAF-FRAME-UNDER-INNER (the frame is obtained after the MapInner is latched) fails it through the hook. MAPLEAF-EXISTS-BY-REF (the second decides by the ref alone) fails run (1) through amcheck: the first LPID's entry is lost with the first MapLeaf's frame, and a read of its page gets XX001. MAPLEAF-WAIT-UNDER-INNER (the second waits for the load holding the MapInner's latch) deadlocks run (3). Test `t_replace_free_shape` (A-5, with A-4a's split, on stand-ins; A-7f with images and replay; §15.1): under a height-≥ 2 tree, a full leaf whose owner rewrites its provisional tag-8 entry with a larger value (so the leaf is unsafe and splits), and a second unsafe leaf where the rewrite fits without a split; the old chain's pages and the MapLeafs of its LPIDs evicted, and a flip pending. Variants: a writer on `k` settles a dead tag-8 entry with a chain (settler (c)), and a superseding replay op replaces a chain-bearing entry. Delete variants, each on a full leaf under the same height-≥ 2 tree with the tag-8 entry's chain pages and their MapLeafs evicted and a flip pending: settler (a)'s abort-cleanup delete, `T`'s `ROLLBACK TO` delete, and a replayed `Delete{revert}` of a tag-8 entry.
