A build variant, under the same height-≥ 2 tree: the rewritten entry's old value is inline, so nothing is freed; its new value needs a chain, and the rewrite splits the leaf but not `P`. The split takes the last LPID its MapLeaf covers, every lower LPID mapped, so the next free LPIDs the new chain takes lie on later MapLeafs, each evicted, unpinned and mapping no dirty page (§4.1), with a flip pending. Those MapLeafs are the only pages the variant reads cold after the split. A hook at each page read and each gate release asserts that no page is read under an inner exclusive latch and no restart follows a mutation, and every image passes amcheck. REPLACE-FREE-UNDER-P (the insert frees the old chain while still holding `P`) fails it through the hook: a chain page or MapLeaf is read under `P`'s exclusive latch. DELETE-FREE-UNDER-P (a chain-freeing delete outside the sweep frees its chain while still holding `P`) fails a delete variant through the hook: a chain page or MapLeaf is read under `P`'s exclusive latch. CHAIN-BUILD-UNDER-P (a chain build after a split keeps `P` and reads its allocations' MapLeafs under it) fails the build variant through the hook: an evicted MapLeaf is read under `P`'s exclusive latch. Test `t_create_every_cut` (B-6): the leak direction; under CREATE-TREE-SPLIT (directory root, map), I-LPID-EXACT fails after recovery. Test `t_chain_every_cut` (A-7d): a flip at every boundary between the gated operations that insert, replace and delete chain-bearing entries, with an evicted leaf and a flip pending; every image passes amcheck and opens. CHAIN-BUILD-SPLIT (chain reference, map: a chain mapped one gated step before its entry, such as a build before the descent and then a restart), CHAIN-FREE-SPLIT (chain reference, unmap: a chain freed one gated step after its entry's delete), CHAIN-NEXT-MAP-SPLIT (next pointer, map: an Overflow page mapped one gated step before its predecessor's pointer) and CHAIN-NEXT-FREE-SPLIT (next pointer, unmap: an Overflow page freed one gated step before its predecessor) fail it. Test `t_load_every_cut` (B-4d): a flip at every boundary between a load's gated steps, then a crash; every image passes amcheck, and walking the discarded build leaks nothing. LOAD-PAGE-SPLIT (child pointer, map) fails it. Test `t_ro_open` with a replayed `DropTree` (A-7d): under REPLAY-DROP-WALK, a read-only open frees a page. Test `t_root_name_reuse` (A-7b, with A-4c's harness): tree X has root `r`, and no LPID below `r` is free, so §4.2's lowest-first order hands `r` to the next allocation; a fixture check asserts that the reusing tree's root or page is at `r`, and an oracle records each LPID's owning tree at every allocation and free. Case A: thread A reads X's entry and a hook stops it before it pins `r`; DropTree(X) runs to completion, CreateTree(Z) takes `r` as Z's root, and A resumes. Case B: A names X's root in a test-only mode that accepts `dropping`, a stand-in as A-7d's paused sweep is; the hook is in DropTree(X)'s last step, after it releases the root's latch and before the operation ends, and there a split of tree Y, in its own gated operation, takes `r` before A names X's root. Case C: as case A, but `r` is freed and not reused, and no frame holds `r` when A pins it (the free leaves `r`'s frame in the page table, freed (§4.2), and a test seam has the pool drop it as an eviction drops a clean victim, with no write), so A's pin finds `r` unmapped (§5.4 "What a pin finds"). Case D: as case A, but `r` is reused as a non-root page of tree Y, and in a variant as an Overflow page of Y. In a second variant a hook pauses Y's split after `r` is mapped, to a frame that last held another LPID's page, and before Y initialises that page; A latches the frame there if the allocation path allows it, and otherwise A's latch is granted only after Y initialises the page, so the variant is case D. The fixture records which branch occurred: A's latch landed on `r`'s frame before Y initialised it, or the allocation path held the frame's latch when the hook paused it. A churn variant has a hook run one CreateTree of another tree to completion between A's read and its re-read, on each of A's attempts; A validates on its first attempt and finishes within the step budget. An exploration runs A, in both modes, with DropTree(X) and a split of Y that can take `r`, under shuttle with no hooks. The property is checked only when A's validation succeeds: the oracle says `r` is mapped and is X's root and X's entry is present, and A never reads or mutates a page the oracle assigns to another tree. In every case A names the root outside any gated operation, as a client read in the normal mode and as the test-only stand-in in the other, a root-name read that accepts `dropping` and holds no gate (not S4's sweep step), so no XX001 of A's poisons the store (§9.2); the walk-poison variant (below) is the gated case. Between its latch and its re-read A reads nothing of the page but its header `id`, no case but case E raises XX001, and no case poisons the store. Case E: X's entry stays unchanged, in the normal mode with X `live` and in the test-only mode with X `dropping`; the fixture evicts `r`, so that no frame holds it, and then clears `r`'s map entry; A's pin finds `r` unmapped, its re-read finds the entry unchanged, and A raises XX001 within the step budget. Case F: X's entry stays unchanged and `r` is X's root as CreateTree(X) made it, resident and never written, so that its map entry still holds slot 0; no eviction and no checkpoint runs between CreateTree(X) and A's pin (the pool holds every page, no background evictor runs, and no flip is pending); A's pin finds `r`'s frame, A validates the name and reads X's root with no error. *Walk-poison variant* (A-7d; §15 I-FAIL-STOP): the real drop walk, A-7b's, called as A-7d's tests call it, names the root `r` of a `dropping` entry X that stays unchanged, after the fixture has evicted `r` and cleared its map entry, with no background store job and no checkpoint run between the eviction and the step, which the test runs itself; the walk step's pin finds `r` unmapped, its re-read finds the entry unchanged, and the step raises XX001 inside its gated operation, so the store is poisoned (§9.2): every later store call returns the error, every waiter returns, and a write hook sees nothing more written. WALK-XX001-NO-POISON (the walk step returns that XX001 to its job without poisoning the store) fails it: a later store call succeeds, or the job retries the step past the step budget. ROOT-NAME-UNVALIDATED (no re-read under the root's latch) fails case A; ROOT-UNPUBLISH-LATE (DropTree removes the root-table entry after releasing the root's latch) fails case B, where A validates `r` as X's root after Y took it; ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-FATAL (a pin of an unmapped LPID on the root-name path raises XX001 without re-reading the entry) fails case C; ROOT-DECODE-BEFORE-VALIDATE (the thread reads the latched page as X's root before the re-read) fails case D; ROOT-HEADER-MISMATCH-FATAL (a header `id` mismatch before the re-read raises XX001) fails case D's second variant where A latches the frame before Y initialises it, and is equivalent on a path whose fixture record shows the allocation path held the frame's latch at the pause, so an equivalent verdict at A-7b's gate rests on that record; ROOT-TABLE-GLOBAL-GEN (validation compares a table-wide counter that every directory change bumps) fails the churn variant, where A retries without bound; ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-SPIN (a pin that finds the LPID unmapped retries from (1) even when the re-read finds the entry unchanged) fails case E, where A retries without bound; ROOT-PIN-MAP-FIRST (a pin reads the map entry before the page table, §5.4) fails case F, where A raises XX001 on a healthy store | A-4a (`t_split_reserve_order`, `t_alloc_restart_progress`), A-4b, A-5 (`t_replace_free_shape`), A-7b, B-4d, B-6, A-7d, A-7f (the full forms of `t_alloc_restart_progress`'s race variant and of `t_replace_free_shape`), A-8, A-8a | DROP-ENTRY-SPLIT, DROP-UNLINK-SPLIT, CREATE-TREE-SPLIT, DROP-RESTART-MID-OP, DROP-FREE-BEFORE-REMOVE, DROP-LEAF-CHAINS, DROP-SECOND-WALK, ROOT-NAME-KEPT, CHAIN-BUILD-SPLIT, CHAIN-FREE-SPLIT, CHAIN-NEXT-MAP-SPLIT, CHAIN-NEXT-FREE-SPLIT, LOAD-PAGE-SPLIT, REPLAY-DROP-WALK, DROP-RESTART-AFTER-REMOVE, UNLINK-RESTART-AFTER-UNLINK, SPLIT-RESERVE-AFTER-PRUNE, ALLOC-READ-UNDER-P, DROP-LAST-STEP-PACKS, SPLIT-RESERVE-ALWAYS, REPLACE-FREE-UNDER-P, DELETE-FREE-UNDER-P, CHAIN-BUILD-UNDER-P, ALLOC-RETRY-LOWEST, ALLOC-NEW-MAPLEAF-IN-PLACE, MAPLEAF-CREATE-UNCONDITIONAL, MAPLEAF-FRAME-UNDER-INNER, MAPLEAF-EXISTS-BY-REF, MAPLEAF-WAIT-UNDER-INNER, ROOT-NAME-UNVALIDATED, ROOT-UNPUBLISH-LATE, ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-FATAL, ROOT-DECODE-BEFORE-VALIDATE, ROOT-TABLE-GLOBAL-GEN, ROOT-HEADER-MISMATCH-FATAL, ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-SPIN, ROOT-PIN-MAP-FIRST, DIRTY-MAPLEAF-UNDER-P, DIRTY-MAPLEAF-PIN-DROPPED |
| I-POOL-BOUND | §5.1/§5.5: the pool never grows; a dirty set of 4× the pool completes. §5.4: the minimum counts every pin a thread can hold at once, in three per-thread terms (the path, the reservation's pages, the reservation's MapLeafs), the MapLeaf pins a restarted allocation keeps while it holds no latch and waits on the gate included, so that a pool at the minimum completes every operation; `R_MAP` counts every MapLeaf that §4.1's pins hold and no frame acquisition can release by evicting, so that a thread below its count always has a frame it can obtain, however the dirty pages' LPIDs lie; no root split makes a tree taller than the open's height bound `H`, and no descent passes it. Test `t_pool_minimum` (A-3b: a pool one frame below the minimum is refused with 53200, one at it is accepted, with `R_EPOCH` and `R_MAP` at R-1-memory's values); `t_alloc_restart_progress`'s pool-floor variant (A-4a: two restarted cascading splits in a pool of exactly the minimum, with a pin tracker); `t_pool_floor_scattered` (A-3c on S3, A-4a in full: a pool of exactly the minimum, filled with dirty pages under MapLeafs of their own; no frame acquisition waits for an unpin); `t_height_bound` (A-4a, and A-7f in full after a reopen: a root split past `H`, and a descent past it, give 53200) | A-3b/A-3c (E4 shape), A-3b (`t_pool_minimum`), A-3c and A-4a (`t_pool_floor_scattered`), A-4a (the pool-floor variant, `t_height_bound`), A-7f (`t_height_bound` in full) | POOL-GROW, EVICT-SKIP-DIRTY, POOL-MIN-PATH-ONLY, MIN-RMAP-DENSE, MIN-RESERVE-NO-STEP2, RESTART-KEEPS-EMPTY-MAPLEAF, MAPPIN-KEPT, HEIGHT-SPLIT-UNCHECKED, HEIGHT-DESCENT-UNCHECKED |
| I-NO-INPLACE | §4.4: a live home slot is never overwritten | A-3c, A-7b (write hook), A-7f (SimEnv crash; §15.1) | INPLACE-EVICT |
| I-INTEGRITY | §3.3: CRC, id and epoch checked on every read, with XX001 | A-2, A-7d (SimEnv lost/misdirected/torn) | CRC-SKIP, EPOCH-SKIP, ID-SKIP |
| I-PIN-CURRENT | §5.1, §5.4: a pin is served its page's current content. The page table holds at most one frame per page, an LPID and a MapLeaf's position keyed apart, and a pin looks there first; a pin that finds no frame puts its own there as a page being loaded, looking again in the same step, before it reads the page's reference; a pin that finds the page being loaded waits for that load, holding no map latch, reads nothing itself and never takes a frame whose load failed, and a page being loaded is not resident; a load ends, its frame out of the page table, before its thread restarts or waits for the flip, and that thread loads the page again only once the flip has run; a creation waits for a load holding no map latch (card S-KAPPA); a page's current reference changes only while a frame holding the page is in the page table; a creation that finds a frame for its page makes no second one, and makes its page in a freed page's frame (card S-THETA). Test `t_pin_load_race` (A-3a on S3, A-3c, A-4a for case (l); §15.1). On OS threads, hooks pause a pin's load once its frame is in the page table (*published*), once it has read the reference (*resolved*), and where a pin is about to put its frame there (*missed*); they report where a pin or a creation is about to wait for a load (*waiting*) and pause a dirty eviction between its two steps after the victim's write, setting the map entry and taking its frame out of the page table (*evicting*), pause a thread where it restarts because it cannot take a frame (*restarting*), and pause a thread that holds no gate where it is about to wait for a pending flip to run (*flip-waiting*; card S-KAPPA). A hook fires holding no mutex. A paused loader holds its pin on its frame, or at *missed* the frame it obtained, not in the page table; a waiter holds its pin on the loader's frame, or a creation the frame it obtained; a paused evictor holds the victim's exclusive latch and the gate shared, and no map latch; a thread paused at *restarting* or at *flip-waiting* holds no latch, no gate and no page being loaded. The controller records what it sees, releases every paused thread and joins it before it asserts, and while a thread is paused calls the pool only in ways that cannot wait. (a) Page `L` at slot `s1` with marker `m1`: T1 pins `L` and is paused at *resolved*; T2 pins `L`, writes marker `m2`, and a dirty eviction writes `L` to a fresh slot and sets its entry, `s1` staying as it was; the controller records which comes first, T2 *waiting* or T2's end, then releases T1. T2 must wait first, and a later pin of `L` reads `m2`. (b) T1 pins `L` and is paused at *published*: a probe, a pin that may not wait, finds `L` not resident; the controller records which comes first, T2's pin *waiting* or T2 being served, then releases T1; T2 must wait first, and is served T1's frame with `m1`, with one slot read in all. (c) `L`'s slot holds bytes that fail §3.3: a pin gets XX001, and a second pin reads the slot again and gets XX001; T2, which waited on T1's failing load (T1 paused at *resolved* until T2 is *waiting*), gets XX001 by a load of its own; and no frame for `L` is left in the page table. (d) An unmapped LPID: a pin finds it unmapped, and so does T2, which waited on T1's load of it (T1 paused at *published* until T2 is *waiting*), with no slot read and nothing left in the page table. (e) T1 pins `L` and is paused at *missed*; T2 pins `L` and ends; T1, released, is served T2's frame: one frame and one slot read. (f) T1 pins an unmapped LPID `K` and is paused at *published*; T3 creates `K` as an allocation does; the controller records which comes first, T3 *waiting* or T3's end, then releases T1. T3 must wait first, T1 finds `K` unmapped, and afterwards one frame holds `K`, with T3's page. (g) `K` is created and then freed (§4.2), and a pin on its frame is kept, as a stale name's would be; a creation of `K` makes its page in that same frame. (h) A dirty eviction of `L`, marker `m2` over `m1` at its home, is paused at *evicting*; T2 pins `L`; after the release, T2 reads `m2`. (i) LPID 5 and the MapLeaf at index 5 are each pinned, and each is served its own page in its own frame. (j) A page that was created and never written, its creator's pin dropped, is found by a pin, not as unmapped. (k) At A-3c, under the interleaving harness with these hooks and S1 for the flip: `L`'s MapLeaf is not resident and every frame a thread could take is dirty; T1, holding no gate, pins `L` and is paused at *published*; T2, a gated operation holding the gate shared, pins `L` and is *waiting*; S1 requests the gate exclusively and waits (a hook in the gate reports it); T1, released, cannot take a frame for `L`'s MapLeaf, since its eviction's try of the gate fails, and is paused at *restarting*, where `L` is not in the page table, until T2 has ended and S1 has flipped; T2 loads `L` under its hold, T1's retry is served `L`, and every schedule finishes within the step budget. Its reload variant (card S-KAPPA): the *restarting* hook releases T1 at once, and a hook holds T2 at *missed* once it looks again, until T1 is paused at *flip-waiting* or at *published*; a hook at T1's next *published* asserts that S1 has flipped, and every schedule finishes within the step budget. (l) At A-4a, under the interleaving harness with these hooks (card S-KAPPA): (f) with T3 a split of A-4a's whose new page takes `K`, `K`'s MapLeaf resident and held by the split's reservation; T1 is released once T3 is *waiting*; T1's load finds `K` unmapped, T3 makes `K`'s page, and every schedule finishes within the step budget. LOAD-REF-BEFORE-PUBLISH fails (a): T2 ends first, and the later pin reads `m1`. LOADING-SERVED fails (b): T2 is served before T1's release, or the probe finds `L` resident. FAILED-LOAD-KEPT fails (c), MISS-TWO-FRAMES (e), CREATE-OVER-LOADING (f), CREATE-BESIDE-FREED (g), whose page is in another frame, PAGE-KEY-MERGED (i) and ROOT-PIN-MAP-FIRST (j); EVICT-UNLINK-BEFORE-ENTRY fails (h) at A-3c, whose eviction it is: T2 reads `m1`; LOAD-KEPT-ACROSS-RESTART fails (k) at A-3c: at *restarting* `L` is still in the page table as a page being loaded, and T2, T1 and the flip wait for one another; RELOAD-BEFORE-FLIP fails (k)'s reload variant at A-3c: T1 reaches *published* with S1 not flipped, or, never reaching *flip-waiting* or *published*, passes the step budget; CREATE-WAIT-UNDER-MAPLEAF fails (l) at A-4a: T1's load waits on `K`'s MapLeaf latch, which T3 holds while it waits for that load, a deadlock the harness reports | A-3a (S3), A-3c, A-4a (case (l)) | LOAD-REF-BEFORE-PUBLISH, LOADING-SERVED, FAILED-LOAD-KEPT, MISS-TWO-FRAMES, CREATE-OVER-LOADING, CREATE-BESIDE-FREED, PAGE-KEY-MERGED, EVICT-UNLINK-BEFORE-ENTRY, LOAD-KEPT-ACROSS-RESTART, ROOT-PIN-MAP-FIRST, RELOAD-BEFORE-FLIP, CREATE-WAIT-UNDER-MAPLEAF |
| I-META-SLOT | §2.2, §2.4: the opened meta is a valid copy in a place of its own parity (slot `1 + (ckpt_id mod 2)` or that parity's replica block); a misplaced valid copy, one in a place of the other parity, is never opened; one whose `ckpt_id` exceeds the newest valid meta's by two or more is XX001 and never fallen back past, and one exactly one above it fails no open, because image `k` and its WAL span stay intact until step 6 of checkpoint `k + 2` (I-TWO-GEN; card S-DELTA, S-ALPHA-F5) | A-2 (`t_meta_copies`' table form), A-7d (SimEnv misdirected meta copy, the meta's other copy lost; `t_meta_copies`' in-flight variant) | META-WRONG-SLOT, MISPLACED-NEXT-REFUSES, MISPLACED-IGNORED |
| I-FREE-SAFE | §4.4/§13 step 6: no reuse before the freeing meta is synced | A-3c, A-7c | FREE-EARLY |
| I-FREE-EXACT | §4.3: the persisted bitmaps are exact | A-7b, A-8 | BITMAP-OMITS-FREESET-PAGES |
| I-META-ATOMIC | §13: a meta never points at unwritten pages | A-7c (crash between every step) | META-BEFORE-SYNC |
| I-CKPT-AT-T | §13: the image is a consistent cut; (a)–(c), with `T` = the apply frontier `M`; (b) bounded by `wal_ckpt_end`/`ts_ckpt_hi`, bulk-load entries via their fence commit; only step 2 writes the image map, from the image-record list and the image refs (§4.1; card S-MU). Test `t_epoch_reserve_two_exhausters` (A-3c on stand-ins, with A-4c's shuttle harness; A-7a through `write_guard`; §5.6, §15.1): two first touches exhaust the reserve together; both copies reach the image. In its cross variant two writers each hold a MapLeaf's latch in `write_guard`, A and B, and exhaust the reserve together, every claimable copy being of a page that the other's MapLeaf maps; both finish, both copies reach the image, and a hook asserts that no claim takes a map latch. EXHAUST-DOUBLE-EVICT and EXHAUST-BLOCKING-CLAIM fail it; CLAIM-LATCHES-MAP (a claim records its copy by latching the MapLeaf that maps it) fails the cross variant through the hook, and deadlocks it. Its floor variant (A-3c on S3, A-7a through `write_guard`; §5.6): a reserve of exactly `threads + s + 1` frames, each a claimable copy, `threads − 1` writers held inside claims and step 2 inside a copy's write; one more first touch completes within the step budget, and no thread holds two reserve frames' latches at once. RESERVE-PER-THREAD-ONLY (the reserve sized as `threads` frames) fails it at A-3c and at A-7a: the last first touch finds every reserve frame latched. CLAIM-DEPTH-TWO (`write_guard` holds a second reserve frame's latch while it holds its first) fails it at A-7a through the reserve's latch hook. Test `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy` (A-3c on stand-ins S1–S3 with the read-back S2; A-7f in full; §13 step 2; §15.1): a hook pauses a writer that has claimed an epoch-`k` copy (§5.6 "Reserve") after the copy's write and before its image-record append, just as step 2's data phase reaches its end. Step 2 must wait, and image `k` must hold the copy's content (amcheck, and a read at `T` after a crash at post-5). A slot variant forces slot allocations from a pool at its minimum after the claim's append and before the map phase runs. A map-copy variant evicts in epoch `k+1` a page that MapLeaf M maps, so that M is copied, and has a claim evict M's epoch copy before step 2's data phase writes another page that M maps. A current-home variant has a §5.2 previous-epoch eviction write MapLeaf M, dirty at the flip with no dirty page it maps, to slot `s1`, which becomes M's current home; after the map phase has rewritten M, slot allocations from a pool at its minimum reach `s1`, M is dropped and reloaded through the current map, and amcheck checks image `k`'s slot bitmap. Then, in epoch `k+1`, M is mutated and evicted again: once that write has moved M's current home, `s1`, an eviction home of M that no image names, is free, and the next allocation, no lower slot being free, takes it (card S-MU). A victim variant pauses a §5.2 previous-epoch victim after its write and before its image-record append, so that step 2's data phase finds the victim latched by its evictor and leaves it to that evictor (§5.2) as the data phase ends. A source variant pauses the map phase after it finds no epoch copy of MapLeaf M and before it latches M's frame, while a writer first-touches M, which installs M's epoch copy, and a claim evicts that copy. A namespace variant lists, in one checkpoint, a record for LPID `n` and one for the MapLeaf at leaf-level position `n` (a claimed copy of it), and adds a radix level in epoch `k+1` before the map phase runs (I-MAP-GROW, §4.1 "Growth"). An adoption variant pauses step 2's data phase after it picks a dirty page P and before it latches P's frame, while a §5.2 previous-epoch eviction writes and lists P and P is reloaded; a hook asserts that the list holds at most one record per page and that no adoption replaces a home the list names. An allocation variant runs a checkpoint with a hook that fails any allocation the image-record list or a map-phase buffer makes after open; the checkpoint completes. A stale-claimant variant: a claim C chooses MapLeaf M's epoch copy, in frame `f`, as its candidate, and a hook pauses it before its `try_latch`; the map phase then takes and detaches the copy. In a detach form C resumes immediately after the map phase has released `f`'s latch, with the map phase held at that point and so before it returns `f` to the reserve; C wins `f`'s latch, its re-check finds `f` no longer M's epoch copy, and it moves on. In a draw form C resumes after `f` is back in the reserve, wins `f`'s latch and is paused before its re-check, with `f` the only free reserve frame, the epoch copy of a map page the map phase has not reached left to claim, and the map phase held by a hook after it returned `f`; a writer holding a page latch first-touches another map page the map phase has not reached, passes over `f`, claims that copy and finishes, and only then does C re-check and move on. A hook asserts that no draw waits on a frame latch, and no record for M is appended after the map phase took M's copy. A two-checkpoint variant (card S-MU): a test fanout; MapLeafs M and N under one level-1 MapInner P, the map's root, with page X under M and page Y under N, all written by a checkpoint `k−1`. In epoch `k`, M is evicted and reloaded, so that its current home holds X's home in image `k−1`, and X is then mutated and evicted, so that M is dirty at flip `k` with a current home whose X entry is older than its own; checkpoint `k` writes M and P. In epoch `k+1` M stays resident and nothing it maps changes, while Y is mutated and evicted, so that checkpoint `k+1` writes N and P and not M. Image `k+1` must hold X's epoch-`k` content (S2's read-back; a read at `T` after a crash at post-5 of checkpoint `k+1`). Its growth run (I-MAP-GROW): the same as far as flip `k`, Y left unchanged; before checkpoint `k`'s map phase runs, a new level is added over P and an LPID is mapped under the new root's second child (§4.1 "Growth"); in epoch `k+1` nothing under P changes; image `k+1`'s root names P's page of image `k`, and image `k+1` holds X's epoch-`k` content. Its reload run (card S-MU): in epoch `k`, X is mutated and evicted and M is then evicted to a slot `s2` and reloaded, so that M is clean at flip `k` with content epoch `k`; after the flip and before the map phase runs, another page M maps is mutated and evicted, which mutates M, and M is evicted again, so that its current home moves and `s2` is free (§4.4), and a slot allocation from a pool at its minimum takes `s2`. Image `k` must hold X's epoch-`k` content (S2's read-back; a read at `T` after a crash at post-5). STEP2-NO-WAIT (step 2's data phase ends with a claimed copy outstanding) and IMAGE-RECORD-AFTER-RELEASE (a claim appends its record after it releases the copy's latch) fail it: image `k` misses the page's epoch-`k` content. IMAGE-SLOT-REUSED (a slot that only the list names is reusable before the map phase applies it) fails the slot variant: a later write lands on the listed slot, and amcheck finds the page's CRC wrong. MAP-PHASE-DROPS-CLAIMED-COPY (the map phase builds M from its frame although the list records M's claimed copy) fails the map-copy variant: image `k`'s M maps the evicted page's epoch-`k+1` home, which a read at `T` after a crash at post-5 returns; MAP-HOME-SLOT-REUSED (a slot the list records for a map page becomes reusable once the map phase rewrites the page, although it is the page's current home) fails the current-home variant: the reload reads a reused slot and fails its CRC check with XX001; MAP-PHASE-SKIPS-VICTIM (the map phase starts while a §5.2 previous-epoch victim's write is in flight) fails the victim variant: image `k` maps the victim's earlier home, which a read at `T` after a crash at post-5 returns; MAP-SOURCE-UNCHECKED (the map phase builds a map page from its frame without checking the frame's content epoch under its latch) fails the source variant: image `k`'s M holds epoch-`k+1` entries, which a read at `T` after a crash at post-5 follows; MAP-POSITION-AS-LPID (a map page's record is keyed as an LPID) fails the namespace variant: one record replaces the other, and amcheck finds image `k`'s map wrong; ADOPT-LISTED-HOME (the data phase decides a page's write and its adoption before it latches the frame) fails the adoption variant through the hook; IMAGE-LIST-GROWS (the image-record list or a map-phase buffer allocates during a checkpoint) fails the allocation variant: the failed allocation is fail-stop with 53200; MAP-COPY-NOT-DETACHED (the map phase releases a map page's epoch copy's latch before it detaches the copy, so the frame is still claimable as that copy) fails the detach form: C's re-check passes and it appends a record for M after the map phase took M's copy, which the hook and §13's engine invariant violation catch; RESERVE-DRAW-BLOCKS (`write_guard` draws a reserve frame with a blocking latch acquire) fails the draw form through the hook: the writer waits on C's latch while it holds its page latch; MAP-INNER-FROM-CURRENT (the map phase builds a level-1 MapInner's image page from its current refs, as draft 7.20's text read) fails the two-checkpoint variant: image `k+1`'s P names M by its current home, whose X entry is X's home in image `k−1`, so the read at `T` returns X's content from before its epoch-`k` mutation, and every check passes; IMAGE-REF-NOT-SET (the map phase sets no image ref when it writes a page) fails it too: image `k+1`'s P names M by its ref from before checkpoint `k`; MAPLEAF-HOME-LEAKED (a MapLeaf's eviction home is reusable at once only when its ref carries the current epoch, as a page's is) fails the current-home variant's second eviction: `s1`, whose ref carries epoch `k`, stays used, and the next allocation takes another slot; MAPLEAF-CLEAN-NOT-COPIED (`write_guard` makes no epoch copy of a MapLeaf of content epoch `k` whose frame is clean, as if its current home were a slot the image map references) fails the reload run: image `k`'s M is built from its image ref's page, so the read at `T` returns X's content from before its epoch-`k` mutation, and every check passes | A-3c (the reserve, the image-record list and the map phase; `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy` (stand-ins; full form A-7f)), A-7c (splits and packs during a checkpoint; async mix; S2, S3), A-7f (the crash forms, FLIP-NO-GATE's splits and packs across a flip among them), B-1c (CKPT-T-VISIBLE with the real frontier), B-1d (a checkpoint during a finalize) | FLIP-NO-GATE, WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY, CKPT-T-VISIBLE, EXHAUST-DOUBLE-EVICT, EXHAUST-BLOCKING-CLAIM, STEP2-NO-WAIT, CLAIM-LATCHES-MAP, IMAGE-RECORD-AFTER-RELEASE, IMAGE-SLOT-REUSED, MAP-PHASE-DROPS-CLAIMED-COPY, MAP-HOME-SLOT-REUSED, MAP-PHASE-SKIPS-VICTIM, MAP-SOURCE-UNCHECKED, MAP-POSITION-AS-LPID, ADOPT-LISTED-HOME, IMAGE-LIST-GROWS, MAP-COPY-NOT-DETACHED, RESERVE-DRAW-BLOCKS, RESERVE-PER-THREAD-ONLY, CLAIM-DEPTH-TWO, MAP-INNER-FROM-CURRENT, IMAGE-REF-NOT-SET, MAPLEAF-HOME-LEAKED, MAPLEAF-CLEAN-NOT-COPIED |
| I-REDO-COMPLETE | §14: every acked sync commit is in the image or replayed (given I-ACK-PREFIX; §14 Post-sync damage; also from an older meta, `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot`). `wal_start_lsn` is at or below every `Chunk` of every transaction whose marker is not below `T`: step 0 takes §12.6's terms at its one entry to the append critical section, which reads the open-chunk set with the append position, and a first `Chunk` enters the set in the entry that reserves it (§12.4, §12.6, §13 step 0). Test `t_flip_chunk_race` (a flip that races a new chunked transaction's first `Chunk`; §14); a transaction that ends with no marker leaves the set as it ends and stops holding `wal_start_lsn` back (its variant *ended*) | A-6d, A-7d | START-LSN-PAST-CHUNK, START-LSN-MARKED-CHUNK, START-LSN-SPLIT-READ, CHUNK-SET-KEEPS-ENDED |
| I-BOOT-DURABLE | §14 step 5, §12.4 "After recovery", §13 step 1: a read-write open truncates the WAL at the valid end durably before step 6 (the rest of the valid end's segment zeroed, every later segment zeroed or removed, each segment written synced, and `wal/` after a removal), and its writer starts with `durable_end` at the walk's start, so its boot checkpoint's step 1 makes every byte the recovery walk accepted durable before the boot meta is written. Test `t_wal_trunc_durable` (class MP with power cuts, under `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot`'s fixture constraints, §12.5): boot `b` recovers to the valid end `V` and writes its boot meta; its first record at `V` is still unwritten when a later batch, which runs past a hop into the next segment `F`, is written and synced (`t_ack_prefix`'s interleaving); a power cut follows, and both copies of `b`'s boot meta are lost after their sync, as in `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot`. Boot `b′` opens the older meta, stops at `V` with `b`'s boot id (§12.5, Residual), truncates, writes its boot meta, and acks `synchronous_commit=on` commits, whose records differ from `b`'s, up to a durable `SegmentEnd` at the end of `V`'s segment, and a power cut that loses every unsynced write follows: in a first run before `b′` syncs `wal/` for any other reason (it creates, recycles or writes no segment past `V`'s), and in a second run after `b′` has written records into `F` without a sync. On a writer that makes the next segment durable, syncing `wal/`, before the `SegmentEnd`'s batch is durable, the first run has no cut point and is skipped: there the removal form is equivalent, since that sync persists step 5's removal before any walk can cross that `SegmentEnd`. The open must succeed with no splice error and replay every commit `b′` acked and no record of `b`; under TRUNC-NOT-DURABLE the first run's cut reverts `b′`'s truncation of `F`, whether step 5 zeroed `F` or removed it, `b`'s record returns there under the same id, and the open fails with a splice XX001. Test `t_boot_durable_start` (a process crash, then class MP with a power cut, under the same constraints): a life writes meta `k`, then appends records past its `wal_ckpt_end` that fill segment `E` and cross a hop into segment `F`, without a sync, and its process crashes, so the page cache keeps them. Boot `b` recovers from `k`, its walk accepts them and stops in `F`, it writes its boot meta `k+1`, and it acks `synchronous_commit=on` commits; a power cut that loses every unsynced write follows, and both copies of meta `k+1` are lost after their sync, as in `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot`. The open selects meta `k` and must replay every commit `b` acked; under BOOT-DURABLE-AT-VALID-END the records in `E` stay unsynced, the cut loses them, the walk stops there above `wal_ckpt_end`, I-WAL-COVER passes, and the open returns `Ok` without them. Variant *fall-back*: no meta is lost; after the same power cut, a post-sync LOST on a page of image `k+1` that the open reads at step 2: the open falls back to meta `k` with proof (§14 step 4a) and must replay every commit `b` acked; under BOOT-DURABLE-AT-VALID-END the walk from meta `k` stops in `E`, below meta `k+1`'s `wal_ckpt_end`, the proof fails, and the read-write open gives XX001 | A-7d (step 5 and the writer's start state; both tests), A-7c (step 1 at a boot checkpoint), A-6c (a sync through `L` covers every segment holding a byte in `[durable_end, L)`, written by this boot or not) | TRUNC-NOT-DURABLE, BOOT-DURABLE-AT-VALID-END |
| I-WAL-COVER | §13 step 0, §14 step 3a: meta `k` records `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`. An open fails with XX001 before pass 2 and writes nothing if its valid end (the walk's stop position, §12.5) is below `wal_ckpt_end`, or if its `Commit`/`CommitMarker` records do not reach `ts_ckpt_hi`. No open holds an entry above its recovered frontier. Test `t_wal_loss_below_ckpt_end`: a SimEnv fixture with post-sync WAL faults (LOST, TORN or MISDIRECTED on a synced WAL write) holds a commit `c > T` partly applied before the flip, whose record step 1 synced; then meta `k` is durable, then a fault on `c`'s record. The open gives XX001 and every file is byte-identical afterwards. Variants: the fault on a record that was appended but unapplied at the flip; on a `SegmentEnd` before `wal_ckpt_end`; on a chunk of a marked transaction; on a chunk of a transaction that never committed, appended after the last `Commit` or `CommitMarker` before the flip (no committed data lost; still XX001; card S-LAMBDA); on a bulk load's fence commit `f > T`, with loaded entries in image `k` (a stand-in load at A-7d; B-4d's load at B-4d, §15.1); **short segment**: a synced record below `wal_ckpt_end` lost after its sync, so that the walk stops on its zeros, in a segment whose file the test then cuts short past that record, at or above `wal_ckpt_end` (forged through `Env`); the lost record is a `Chunk` of a transaction that never commits, appended after the last `Commit` or `CommitMarker` before the flip, so that the walk reads every commit up to `ts_ckpt_hi`, the second condition holds, and only the first decides. Under COVER-CHECK-SKIP, a new commit and then a read at `S ≥ c` see part of `c`; under COVER-END-AT-T, the never-committed chunk's variant opens (in the main fixture the second condition refuses it as it does the build, since the fault on `c`'s record leaves the walk short of `ts_ckpt_hi`; card S-LAMBDA); under COVER-AFTER-TRUNCATE, the files differ; under COVER-AT-EOF, the short-segment variant opens. Test `t_cover_ts_cross_check`: a meta whose `ts_ckpt_hi` exceeds every replayable commit (forged through `Env`) gives XX001; for §14 step 1's open-time check, a forged meta with `T > ts_ckpt_hi`, and one with `wal_start_lsn > wal_ckpt_end`, each with a valid CRC, give XX001 (META-RANGE-SKIP opens them) | A-7c (the fields), A-7d (SimEnv post-sync WAL damage), B-4d (the bulk-load variant's real load; §15.1) | COVER-CHECK-SKIP, COVER-END-AT-T, COVER-AFTER-TRUNCATE, TS-HI-CHECK-SKIP, COVER-AT-EOF, META-RANGE-SKIP |
| I-WAL-CHAIN | §2.2, §12.2, §12.4, §12.5, §13 step 0, §14 step 3a, §14.1: every record carries `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, the start and the `crc` of the record appended before it (`SegmentEnd` included); a boot's first record names its recovery's last accepted record, or the opened meta's `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc`. The chain is checked last, after every stop check, and both fields are compared. A record that passes every stop check but names another predecessor (either field differs) is a splice, never an end: a read-write open fails with XX001 before pass 2 and writes nothing, and a read-only open serves salvage cut before it (§14.1). The stop checks keep their meaning. Undetected only when the spliced-in record's predecessor is byte-identical, at the same position, to the walk's (or their CRCs collide). Every reservation moves the append position and the chain head together, step 0 reads the start pair with the append position in one entry and takes its `crc` once final, after recovery the chain head is the recovery's, a batch's first record is sealed with the previous batch's last record's final `crc` (§12.2), and in a read-only open the splice cut also cuts back pass 1's marker set, largest `commit_ts`, coverage result and dependency check. Tests `t_wal_splice` (both placements, the read-only variant, crossed with the same-start variant and with a marker past the cut, the same-id residual variant, the same-start variant that a position-only chain misses), `t_wal_chain_crash` (no splice under ordinary crashes, including a flip that races a `Chunk` reservation and two batches in flight) | A-6a (the fields), A-6b (the check), A-6c (sealing in order: SEAL-STALE-HEAD), A-7c (`wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc`, read with the append position, each boot's first `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, and the boot checkpoint's pair from the recovery's chain head; its mutants judged by A-7f's `t_wal_chain_crash`), A-7d (the outcomes and the salvage cut-back) | PREV-CHECK-SKIP, PREV-CRC-UNCHECKED, PREV-MISMATCH-AS-END, PREV-FIRST-WRONG, PREV-HOP-WRONG, WAL-START-PREV-WRONG, WAL-START-PREV-CRC-WRONG, SPLICE-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT, WAL-START-PAIR-SPLIT-READ, BOOT-PAIR-UNSET, SEAL-STALE-HEAD, SPLICE-MARKER-PAST-CUT, SPLICE-CUT-SAME-START-KEEP |
| I-RO-OPEN | §14.1: a read-only open writes nothing through `Env`, and its reads at every registered `S` equal those of a read-write open of the same files after step 4, except under WAL loss, a splice, a malformed record or a short segment (§14 step 3a), a read error in pass 1's walk (step 3), a missing start segment (step 3), an unmet checkpoint dependency (step 3b), an unproven fall-back (step 4a) or a newest image that a read error (58030) stops at open when there is a previous image (step 4a), where the read-write open fails and the read-only open serves the salvage state at the recovered frontier (§14.1). Every build offers it (§14.1). Its evictable part meets §5.4's minimum at every instant of pass 2 and after it, with `H` and `R_EPOCH` sized from that part as R-1-memory §5.3 says, or the open fails with 53200 at once, writing nothing and waiting for no unpin (card S-LAMBDA). Test `t_ro_open`, with a SimEnv that fails every write, sync, rename and truncate. Its fixture is a crashed file with a torn WAL tail, small commits above `T`, a marked chunked txn whose marker lies after a commit `≤ T`, unmarked chunks, a `dropping` entry, a replayed `DropTree`, provisional entries in the image, and replay splits. The test compares the reads with a read-write open of a copy, checks the files are byte-identical after close, checks that a pool too small for the replay gives 53200 with nothing written, and checks that the replayed `DropTree`'s tree keeps every page mapped (REPLAY-DROP-WALK fails it). Its budget forms (card S-LAMBDA), each a read-only open with the pool's hook at a frame acquisition about to wait for an unpin (`t_pool_floor_scattered`'s), which must never fire: (i) a replay that dirties pages whose LPIDs lie under MapLeafs of their own opens with a pool of `F_min` plus the dirty frames plus those MapLeafs (R-1-memory §5.3), and with one frame fewer fails with 53200 and writes nothing; (ii) with a pool `F` large enough that a reserve sized from it, `R_EPOCH_min + ⌊(F − F_min) / 32⌋` (R-1-memory §4.3), would leave fewer frames outside it at `F_e = F_min` than a write that splits every level of a tree as tall as the `H` that `F_min` gives takes, a replay that drives `F_e` down to `F_min` and then replays such a write completes, and a second run that dirties one page more fails with 53200, writing nothing; (iii) a replay whose last op is an insert that builds an overflow chain at `ROW_LIMIT` (§6.7), begun with `F_e` just above `F_min`, so that the chain takes `F_e` below `F_min` before the op ends: the open fails with 53200 before that op ends (a hook at the end of each replayed op never fires for it) and writes nothing; (iv) a tree of exactly the open's first `H` levels, written under a larger pool, and a replay that first dirties enough pages to lower `H`, then replays a write into that tree: the open fails with 53200 and writes nothing. RO-BUDGET-DIRTY-ONLY (the evictable part counts the dirty frames but not their MapLeafs) fails (i): the open goes on one frame below, or the hook fires; RO-SIZED-FROM-F (`R_EPOCH` and `H` sized from `F`) fails (ii): the hook fires; RO-FLOOR-PER-OP (the floor checked only between replayed ops) fails (iii): the op ends, or the hook fires; RO-H-SET-ONCE (`H` set once, at open) fails (iv): the write's descent proceeds. Test `t_ro_open_disk_full` (the stopped store, C4 decision 5; A-7d with the walk scan S6, B-6 with A-4e's cursor; §15.1): a SimEnv stops a writer with ENOSPC (53100) at each write point in turn, namely a WAL append, a segment's creation, its preallocation (a short segment, §12.5), a file extension (§4.5), a checkpoint page write and the meta write. A read-only open then runs on a SimEnv that reports zero free bytes and refuses every write, including a lock file's creation. It must succeed, its reads must equal those of a read-write open of a copy on a roomy Env, a full scan of every published tree must succeed, and every file must be byte-identical after close. With a pool below the replay's evictable minimum it must give 53200 with nothing written, and a read-only open of the same files with a larger pool budget must then succeed at zero free bytes (§14.1's remedy). SHORT-SEG-ERROR (a segment entered by a hop that is missing, or too short for a record header at its first LSN, returns 58030 instead of ending the walk there, §12.5) fails the open. Test `t_seg_ack_durable` (A-6b on the walk; A-7f with acks and replay; §12.5's writer rule; §15.1), with a SimEnv whose power cut also loses unsynced creates, renames and `set_len`: a segment is newly created (the lost-create case) or recycled by rename (the lost-rename case, the rename made by a process that crashes before its directory sync, so that the next boot's writer finds the segment present), records in it are acked, and power is cut at every step from the create or rename to the ack. Recovery must replay every acked record, and the walk must end in a stop. SEG-ACK-BEFORE-DURABLE (ack before the segment's `wal/` directory sync, or, for a segment present at open, before this boot's own) loses an acked commit as a clean stop and fails it; SEG-LEN-NOT-DURABLE (the directory entry synced but not the length) leaves the segment short behind its last synced record, so that the walk reports damage and a healthy store's read-write open fails with XX001, and fails it. Test `t_wal_loss_salvage` (Q1 = A, PLAN-v3 §10 item 8; A-7d with the directory as the catalog's tree list (S3) and the walk scan S6; B-6 with the catalog and A-4e's cursor; §15.1): the `t_wal_loss_below_ckpt_end` fixture, plus a `dropping` mark from a drop-cleanup commit above `T` whose record is lost, opened read-only. At every admitted `S` it reads the model's state at `S ≤` the frontier, never an entry above it, and a full scan of every tree the catalog at `S` names succeeds. SALVAGE-ABOVE-FRONTIER fails it. Test `t_wal_splice`, read-only variant: the open serves the state at the frontier of the records below the splice cut, with pass 1's marker set, largest `commit_ts`, coverage result and dependency check cut back to it; SPLICE-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT fails it, and, crossed with the same-start variant and with a marker past the cut, so do SPLICE-CUT-SAME-START-KEEP and SPLICE-MARKER-PAST-CUT. Test `t_wal_read_error`, its read-only opens (card S-IOTA; §12.5): the open serves salvage at the read cut and reports the read error and the cut; WAL-READ-ERROR-RO-REFUSES and WAL-READ-ERROR-RO-AS-END fail it; in (e), below `wal_ckpt_end`, it admits no snapshot above its frontier, and READ-SALVAGE-ABOVE-FRONTIER fails it (card S-LAMBDA) | A-7d, A-7f (`t_seg_ack_durable` in full), A-6b, B-6 (`t_wal_loss_salvage` and `t_ro_open_disk_full` with A-4e's cursor; §15.1) | RO-NO-REPLAY, RO-WRITES, RO-LOCK-FILE, SHORT-SEG-ERROR, SEG-ACK-BEFORE-DURABLE, SEG-LEN-NOT-DURABLE, WAL-READ-ERROR-RO-REFUSES, WAL-READ-ERROR-RO-AS-END, SALVAGE-ABOVE-FRONTIER, SPLICE-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT, SPLICE-CUT-SAME-START-KEEP, SPLICE-MARKER-PAST-CUT, REPLAY-DROP-WALK, READ-SALVAGE-ABOVE-FRONTIER, RO-BUDGET-DIRTY-ONLY, RO-SIZED-FROM-F, RO-FLOOR-PER-OP, RO-H-SET-ONCE |
| I-WAL-STALE | §2.3, §12.1, §12.5: the reader stops at the first bad magic/CRC, wrong `lsn` or over-long `len`, or a boot id older, in serial-number order, than the last accepted record's (the opened meta's for the first record); a walk may cross into newer boots; recovery truncates there. It hops to the next segment after `SegmentEnd` or after a record that ends exactly at its segment's end, and a writer never leaves a shorter tail (§12.2). Nothing else ends the walk cleanly but a segment entered by a hop that is missing or too short for a record header at its first LSN: a read error in pass 1's walk fails a read-write open with 58030, writing nothing more than the open had written when the read failed (nothing in pass 1 from meta `k`), while a read-only open serves salvage cut there, reported with the read error and never as a complete open; one that only pass 2's walk meets fails both opens with 58030; and a record that passes every check but does not decode, or a read past a segment file's end-of-file anywhere else, is detected damage (XX001 before pass 2, writing nothing; a read-only open's salvage cut there); so no correct build shortens a segment file, step 5's truncation included (card S-LAMBDA). `wal_seg_size` is at least 212, and at creation at least 8 MiB outside the store's tests. A boot's id is fresh after a meta rollback (§14 step 8). Tests `t_stale_boot_record`, `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot`, `t_wal_exact_segment_end`, `t_boot_id_after_rollback` (both variants), `t_wal_read_error`, `t_wal_malformed`, `t_wal_short_read`, `t_wal_seg_size` | A-6b, A-7c, A-7d | LSN-CHECK-SKIP, BOOT-ID-STOP-SKIP, BOOT-ID-EQ-STOP, SEG-EXACT-NO-HOP, SEG-TAIL-SHORT, BOOT-ID-CKPT-ONLY, BOOT-ID-NO-META-FLOOR, WAL-READ-ERROR-AS-END, WAL-MALFORMED-AS-END, SHORT-READ-AS-END, SHORT-HOP-AS-DAMAGE, SEG-SIZE-UNCHECKED, PASS2-READ-ERROR-AS-END, TRUNC-SHORTENS-SEGMENT |

CKPT-PAIR-SPLIT-READ (the two values are read in two entries) fails it | A-6c, B-1c, A-7c | TS-AFTER-APPEND, CKPT-PAIR-SPLIT-READ |

| I-META-COPIES | §2.2, §2.4, §13 step 4: every meta is written twice before step 5, to slot `1 + (k mod 2)` and to its parity's replica block, the copies differing in their `copy` byte and each well-formed and carrying the file's static fields; the open selects by §2.2's table, with the tie rule, the misplaced-copy rule and foreign copies ignored, fails with 55000, in every open, on a record of the file of a later format version, whose `format_version` is above 1 (§2.2), and otherwise with XX001 on an intact page 0 that is not well-formed and on an intact copy with the file's `creation_uuid` that is not well-formed or has another static field, in any place, and needs no page 0; creation and step 4 refuse to write a record that is not well-formed, with XX000; one fault on one copy of a meta whose two copies were both written never changes which meta opens or what it recovers; a read-write open zeroes every meta place whose read failed, and syncs, before its first write (the fence, §14 step 1), so that a meta it could not read never opens later over slots it reused; no page has a reserved slot as its home. Tests `t_alloc_restart_progress` (its MapInner and level race variants), `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy` (its namespace variant's new level, and its two-checkpoint variant's growth run). LEVEL-IMAGE-REF-STALE (a level added in epoch `k+1` keeps, for its first child, the image ref the old root had when the level was made, so that checkpoint `k+1` builds it with the old root's page from before checkpoint `k`) fails the growth run: image `k+1` names P's page of image `k−1`, and the read at `T` returns X's content from before its epoch-`k` mutation | A-3c (the growth, the map phase), A-4a (the race variants), A-7d (the full forms) | MAPINNER-CREATE-UNCONDITIONAL, LEVEL-GROW-UNCONDITIONAL, MAP-ROOT-CURRENT, MAP-POSITION-FROM-ROOT, LEVEL-IMAGE-REF-STALE |

amcheck (A-8) checks I-TREE, I-ENC-ROUNDTRIP, I-TAG-RESERVED, I-LPID-EXACT, I-FREE-EXACT and I-INTEGRITY on a quiescent file. At open (§14 step 7) it runs before the boot job's walks start, or holds the gate exclusively, because a running walk is not quiescent: either way it reads a closed graph. A crash image's `dropping` tree gets no exemption (I-LINK-ATOMIC).

### 15.1 Test ownership

One row per card that runs a test or a form of it: *Needs* names the cards whose code the form uses besides its own, each, for a Phase A card, in that card's Depends closure (PLAN-v3 §9 A); *Form* is full, or the stand-ins it uses (S1–S6) and the variants it runs; *Mutants* are those judged on that card, by name. The Phase B cards in its Card column are B-1a, B-1b, B-1c, B-1d, B-2c, B-3, B-4d, B-6, B-7 and R-1; its Needs column also names B-2a, B-2b, B-2e and B-4a. A mutant that no Phase A row judges is pinned only in Phase B (PLAN-v3 §8). Every mutant that draft 7.8 judged on a Phase A card is judged on a Phase A row here, and a change to this table keeps it so. Every mutant added since draft 7.10 is judged on a Phase A row too. A-7f, which the plan split from A-7d (card PL-11), adds no code: it runs the full forms of tests whose earlier forms ran on stand-ins, and the half of `t_wal_chain_crash` that judges A-7c's and A-6c's mutants through recovery, five of them there first, so each of its rows needs A-7d, whose recovery those forms run.

- **Commits measured at:** W1 (b)/(c), W2, W3 and W4 at d28582e8; W1 (a), W5 and W6 at c39656fd.

- **Decision:** PLAN-v3 §4, "E1′ decision".

| One-seek point read (§8.1) | W2 reads at 0 writers: (b) 766,882 ops/s, p50 9,058 ns | README §W2 |

| Version skipping in scans (§8.3) | W3 100-row scans at 10 versions/key: latest (b) 88,019 ns vs (c) 10,715 ns (8.2×); old snapshot (b) 87,627 ns vs (c) 146,156 ns | README §W3; decision block "Known costs" |
| Merge and free set (§6.5, §11) | W4 (b) tree/logical 20.11 → 20.11 after GC, although versions fell 1.1M → 100k (1.13 MB freed): E0 never merges or frees | README §W4; `e0-btree/src/tree.rs` T-NOMERGE; decision block |

## 17. Boundaries

**C-T0 v8 (card S-2) owns, and this file does not specify:**
- **Watermark.** The computation, publication and monotonicity of `W`, and its floor after a restart (`≥` `gc_w_used` as recovery leaves it: meta `gc_w`, raised by every DropTree mark that pass 2 replays, §10.5 "Drops"). The published `W` is also the `w` that a drop-cleanup commit's `DropTree` op carries and its mark raises `gc_w_used` to (§6.8 step 1, §12.3). That `W ≤` every registered snapshot and `W ≤ M`, the apply frontier (hence `W ≤ visible_ts`), is the precondition this file relies on (§10.1, §10.5).
- **The write set and lock table:** own-write merge (I-HALLOWEEN), escalation, NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED.
- **The write-side rules:** the RR check after the lock wait, unique checks and ON CONFLICT, using §8.2.
- **Commit-visibility semantics.** The commit ack waits for `visible_ts ≥ c` (decision block, "Commit visibility"). There is no session floor: C-T0 v8 §3.4 rejects it and says why.
- **Pre-applying large transactions** as transaction-tagged provisional entries (decision block, "Large-txn head-of-line"). Specified in C-T0 v8 §2.4 and §3.2. This file holds the encoding (§7.1, §7.3 tags 6–8), the read rule (§8), chunk supersede (§12.7, §14) and the image rule (I-CKPT-AT-T (c)), from the S-2 co-edit.
- **Lock release at commit cheaper than O(keys)** (325k–437k probes slipped through the release tail in W6): C-T0 v8 §3.3.
- **Unrelated commits stalled** behind a big apply by the ts-ordered `visible_ts` advance: C-T0 v8 §3.1–§3.2.
- **SSI** without intents.
- **GC policy:** when sweeps run, their budgets, and DDL retirement timing (C-T0 v7.8 §9.2–9.3).
- **Bounds on disk growth under long readers.** Whether on-disk version growth under long readers needs a bound is S-2/B-3's call (decision block, ruling R2 "Not R-1's call"). This file only guarantees that the growth costs disk, not RAM (§5.5).

**S-3 owns:** the row format, TOAST semantics over §6.7's chains, and the online index build's steps under the §12.8 rule. That includes registering a snapshot at `R` **before** picking the load's `S > R` and keeping it registered for the whole load (§10.1), and how an unfinished build is restarted. S-3 should also record §10.1's reasoning for why this condition has no mutant.

**R-1 owns** (`docs/R-1-memory.md`): the numeric budgets per mode, `F`, `R_EPOCH`, `R_MAP`'s value, the height bound `H` with its floor `H_min`, and the per-consumer accounting of §5.7. This file keeps the rules those numbers enter: §5.3's fairness bound, §5.4's minimum, its count and the height rule, and §5.6's Bound.

**D-6 and D-8 own:** backup, PITR and replica handling of the §12.8 boundary; format versioning and upgrades.

## 18. Refinements of PLAN-v3 §3.3

This file wins over the plan (precedence above). The plan should be updated through an Opus card to match these. R10 refines the §4 decision block rather than §3.3. R11 comes from the S-2 co-edit. R12–R15 come from card S-1-F3, R16 from card S-1-F5, R17 from card S-1-F7, R18–R22 from card S-ALPHA, R23 from card S-BETA, and R24 from card S-GAMMA; R25 comes from card S-DELTA and R26 from card R-1-SPEC; R15 refines §3.4, as R9 does:

| # | Plan text | Here | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Meta fields: "checkpoint id, T, the page-map root, the free-set root, the catalog root, the WAL start LSN and a CRC" | Adds `gc_w`, `epoch`, `next_lpid`, `slot_count`; the root fields are refs with epoch and CRC; "catalog root" is the tree directory's root (§2.2) | `gc_w` replaces v7.8's synced `/sys/gc_w` (§10.5). The others are needed by I-FREE-EXACT and I-INTEGRITY on physically addressed pages |
| R2 | Step 1: "WAL `sync_data` through the last record with commit_ts ≤ T" | Through the end of every record appended before the flip (§13 step 1) | Applies finish out of ts order (`commit.rs`), so the image can hold part of a commit `> T` |
| R3 | Step 0: "Pause commit-apply briefly" | The epoch gate, held by every gated operation (§5.6) | A split, pack, GC or eviction that straddled the flip would tear the image (I-CKPT-AT-T) |
| R4 | Bulk loads: "S-1 picks one" | Force a checkpoint before publication (§12.8) | Versions at a registered ts `≤ T` cannot be placed by a replay filtered on `commit_ts > T`; page images carry LPIDs |
| R5 | B+tree (no root rule) | Stable root LPIDs (§6.3) | The directory and images never change on root splits |
| R6 | Free set (no representation) | Two bitmaps, slots and LPIDs (§4.3) | Memory is a fixed fraction of the file; no fixed-point problem at serialisation |
| R7 | WAL order (unstated) | I-WAL-TS-ORDER (§12.4) | Required for the ts-prefix that E4's oracle checks |
| R8 | "split and merge" (no merge rule) | The pack step with `F_PACK = 0.85`, driven by the GC sweep (§6.5, §10.4); I-SPACE (§11) | The decision block requires GC to return space; B-3's 1.5× acceptance |
| R9 | §3.4 GC "prunes versions below the watermark during page writes and splits" | Page-local prune (required at splits, optional at eviction) **plus** a whole-tree sweep (§10.3–10.4) | Page-local pruning cannot see groups that span leaves, so alone it cannot meet I-GC-COMPLETE or B-3's bound |
| R10 | §4 decision block, "Space amplification": "after-GC tree bytes" (undefined) | Tree bytes = reachable pages × `page_size` (§11) | The file never shrinks (§4.5), so a file-length or allocated-page reading stays at the post-update peak and B-3 could never pass |
| R11 | Step 0: "T = the apply frontier (every commit ≤ T is fully applied to the trees; under N2, trees hold only committed data)" | `T` = C-T0 v8's `M`; trees may also hold provisional entries, which are never data after recovery (§1, §13 (c)) | X-4 fired the pre-apply trigger; C-T0 v8 §3.2 |
| R12 | Meta fields (R1's list) | Adds `wal_ckpt_end`, `ts_ckpt_hi`, `boot_id` (§2.2) | I-WAL-COVER and the ordered boot-id stop |
| R13 | Recovery: "walk … to the valid end", with no rule for an early end | The coverage check against the walk's stop position: fail closed with XX001, write nothing, and a read-only salvage that every build offers (§14 step 3a, §14.1; PLAN-v3 §10 item 8) | E4 class MW (X-5c-F3) |
| R14 | Group commit: "one writer … then acks the group" | I-ACK-PREFIX (§12.4) | X-5c-F3 seed 21 |
| R15 | §3.4's fail-stop (I/O error, disk full) | I-FAIL-STOP includes XX001 inside gated operations, checkpoints and store jobs, and 58030/53100/53200/XX001 on the WAL path; client reads never poison on XX001, and an engine invariant violation poisons wherever it is raised, as a panic in store code does (card S-THETA), with XX000 (card S-IOTA); every wait, the lock-table waits included, observes the poison (§9.2) | G8, MP-LIVELOCK 41 → 0 |
| R16 | WAL record framing (unstated) and meta fields (R12) | Records carry `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, the meta carries `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc`, and the walk checks the chain (I-WAL-CHAIN, §12.5, §14 step 3a) | Owner decision, PLAN-v3 §10 item 9 (2026-11-03): back-pointers in the 2.0 format, because adding them later needs a format migration. Its addendum (owner, 2026-11-03): the chain also carries the predecessor's CRC, as SQLite's does, because a position alone misses two boots appending equal-length records from the same point |
| R17 | "DropTree frees a tree's pages bottom-up, and an unfinished drop resumes at open" | The entry removal and the root free are one gated operation, entry first; a chain-bearing entry goes with its chain in its own gated operation; an operation restarts only before its first mutation; an LPID is mapped and unmapped in the same gated operation as its reference (I-LINK-ATOMIC, §5.6, §6.8); and pass 2 runs no walk (§14 step 4) | E4's second design finding: a flip between the root free and the entry removal gave a refused open, a replay that freed another tree's live page, or an image whose dropping entry named one (28 of 51 C-XX rounds). Page headers carry no tree id (§3.1), so a recovery arm cannot tell a reused root from the tree's own |
| R18 | "Slots 1–2 = two meta slots: meta `k` goes to slot `1 + (k mod 2)`, so a torn meta write can destroy only the older one"; "At open, the meta with the highest checkpoint id whose magic and CRC are valid and which sits in its own slot wins. There is no fallback to the older meta" | Every meta is written twice, to its slot and to a replica block at a fixed byte offset, each copy with a `copy` byte and the file's static fields; the open selects among four places by §2.2's table, with a tie rule and the misplaced rule; an intact page 0 that is not well-formed, or an intact copy with the file's `creation_uuid` that is not well-formed or has another static field, fails the open rather than pass for a torn or a foreign one, since magic and CRC decide only that a record exists (card S-ZETA), with 55000 in every open when a later format version wrote it and XX001 otherwise (card S-IOTA); a newest image that fails at open, an integrity check (§3.3) failing or a decoder refusing a page it references (card S-LAMBDA), falls back one generation with proof that the log is complete, and otherwise the read-write open is refused and an explicit rollback can open it, as it can a newest image that a read error (58030) stops at open, which a read-only open serves as salvage (§2.2, §2.4, §14 step 4a, §14.1; I-META-COPIES, I-FALLBACK) | Owner decision, PLAN-v3 §10 item 16 (2026-11-10): "two places (copies differ in bytes, tie rule), two generations; automatic fall-back needs proof the log is complete, else refuse + explicit rollback" |
| R19 | Step 6: "Only now free the previous image's superseded slots, and recycle the WAL segments wholly below the start LSN"; "slots freed by image k may be reused only after meta k is synced (I-FREE-SAFE)"; "Segments wholly below the durable meta's start LSN may be recycled" | One checkpoint later: step 6 of checkpoint `k` frees image `k−2`'s superseded slots and recycles below meta `k−1`'s start, lowest first, each rename durable before the next; an open protects the previous image (§4.4, §12.1, §12.6, §13 step 6; I-TWO-GEN, I-WAL-START) | The same decision's "two generations": a fall-back of one generation is sound only if that image and its WAL span are intact |
| R20 | Bulk loads [R4]: "The publishing commit may be issued only after the forced checkpoint's meta is durable" | The publishing commit also names that checkpoint (`RequireCkpt`), and recovery refuses to replay it over an older image (§12.3, §12.8, §14 step 3b; I-CKPT-DEP) | E4's first silent-loss path (`header-design-verify.md` §1.1–§1.3), fixed in the prototype by card E4-SILENT-1; PLAN-v3 §10 item 16 (3): "dependency names the checkpoint id, not a log position" |
| R21 | File creation, and recovery's walk: "A segment cut short or missing also ends it" | Segment 0 exists from creation; every meta's start segment exists, with its preallocated length, before the meta is written; a missing start segment is XX001, never an empty WAL; a segment cut short ends the walk only where it holds less than a record header at the first LSN of a segment the walk enters by a hop, and is detected damage anywhere else, a record's body past its end-of-file and the start segment included (§2.3, §12.5, §13 step 1, §14 steps 3 and 3a; I-WAL-START, I-WAL-STALE) | E4's second silent-loss path (`header-design-verify.md` §1.4), fixed in the prototype by card E4-SILENT-1 |
| R22 | Page map: "a radix tree of physically addressed pages whose inner pages stay resident" (no growth rule) | The root is a MapInner whose level the meta records; a new level or MapInner is made in an allocation restart's own gated operation, under the current root's or the parent's exclusive latch, only if still missing (§4.1 "Growth"; I-MAP-GROW) | Verdict S-1-F7-F2-F2 at d909ebba, anomaly (a) and follow-up S-1-F7-F2-F2-F1: map growth was unspecified, and two restarts making one MapInner or level could lose a mapping |
| R23 | Recovery step 5: "Truncate at the valid end"; step 0's "Record `gc_w`, the WAL start LSN and `wal_start_prev`, `wal_start_prev_crc`"; the splice salvage "with the valid end cut back to the splice cut"; meta `gc_w` (R1) | The truncation is durable before the boot checkpoint, and the recovered writer's `durable_end` starts at the walk's start, so the boot checkpoint syncs every byte the walk accepted (I-BOOT-DURABLE; §14 step 5, §12.4, §13 step 1); step 0 reads the start pair with the append position in one entry (§13 step 0); splice salvage cuts pass 1's results and the dependency check back to the cut too (§14.1); `gc_w` also bounds every DropTree mark, and the restart floor is `gc_w_used` as recovery leaves it (§10.5, I-GCW; C-T0 §3.7 step 3) | P0 review 2 (2026-11-15): S-1-F6 (card S-1-F5's D5 and its gate, (7)), S-1-F5-F1 (`verdicts/S-1-F5.md`) and S-1-F4 (S-1-F3's draft gate, O-1) |
| R24 | Replay start and recycling: "the minimum of the first `Commit`/`CommitMarker` with commit_ts > T and the first chunk of every chunked txn whose marker is not yet below T"; step 0's "Record … the WAL start LSN" | Both terms are taken at step 0's one entry to the append critical section, which keeps the open-chunk set: a first `Chunk` enters it in the entry that reserves it, and step 0 reads it with the append position and the chain head (§12.4, §12.6, §13 step 0; I-REDO-COMPLETE). The section is a leaf lock, and a hop does nothing about the next segment (§12.4) | `verdicts/S-BETA.md` at 5cb70935, implementer item 4 and follow-up S-BETA-F1: a first `Chunk` reserved between step 0's read of the set and its read of the position lay below `wal_start_lsn` uncounted, and replay lost part of an acked commit silently |
| R25 | Buffer manager: "Minimum pool size: threads × (max tree height + 2) + the epoch-copy reserve `R_EPOCH` + the map-pin reserve `R_MAP`" | `threads × 3 × (max tree height + 2) + R_EPOCH + R_MAP`: per thread, the path, the pages a gated operation reserves before its first mutation, and the MapLeafs it pins for the LPIDs it allocates or frees, which a restarted allocation keeps while it holds no latch; `threads` counts the checkpointer and background jobs, and `R_MAP` covers dirty pages' MapLeafs only (§5.4, §5.6; I-POOL-BOUND) | Verdict S-1-F7-F2-F2 at d909ebba, follow-up S-1-F7-F2-F2-F2, and the lock-order review's C6.3: pins held by threads that wait on the gate close a cycle at the pool floor unless the minimum counts them; a split's reserved pages were never counted either (E0, card X-0-F2: `2·h + 1` frames for a cascading insert) |
| R26 | Buffer manager: the minimum pool size's `max tree height` and "the map-pin reserve `R_MAP`" (R25) | `max tree height` is the open's height bound `H`, which the open sets from its pool (R-1-memory §4.5) and the store enforces: no descent passes it and no root split exceeds it, each with 53200. `R_MAP = threads × (max tree height + 2) + R_EPOCH` counts the MapLeafs that no frame acquisition can release by evicting, and §5.3's fairness premise counts the frames a thread can obtain (§5.3, §5.4; I-POOL-BOUND) | PLAN-v3 §9 row R-1 (verdict S-1-F7's N6; verdict S-1-F7-F2-F2-F4b's fairness premise); card S-DELTA's owner point P-1 ("a smaller tree-height bound, which R-1 sets"); card R-1-SPEC's derivation (R-1-memory §4.4, §4.5) |

## 19. Open questions, known costs, revisit triggers

**Open:**
1. **File shrinking.** GC returns space to the free set, not to the filesystem (§4.5). Trigger for a tail-truncation step (at step 6, of free tail slots only) or an offline compaction: a browser quota (E6/D-5) or a user report of a file that does not shrink after a mass delete.
2. **Leaf prefix compression within groups.** Every version repeats its full escaped key, so a 10-version group of W1-sized keys spends about 117 B on repeated key bytes. A-4d covers inner nodes only. Trigger: NBench space rows, or I-SPACE met but B-3's bytes measured close to 1.5×.
3. **Optimistic latches and pointer swizzling** (N8/N9): only on the E0/E7 triggers (PLAN-v3 §3.12).
4. ~~**Provisional entries.**~~ Closed by the S-2 co-edit: §7.1, §7.3 tags 6–8, §8, §12.7, I-CKPT-AT-T (c), §14 (C-T0 v8 §2.4).
5. **PITR and replicas across a §12.8 bulk load** (D-6).
6. **Resumable overflow-chain free or build** if A-5 measures the flip hold of a gated operation as too long (§6.7): a chain's build or free, a replace, a GC sweep's or DropTree walk's chain delete over cold chains (§6.8, §10.4). A resumable chain free or build must keep I-LINK-ATOMIC (§5.6; mutants CHAIN-NEXT-MAP-SPLIT, CHAIN-NEXT-FREE-SPLIT).

**Known costs of (b), accepted, with revisit triggers (from the decision block, restated so the storage cards carry them):**
- **T1: latest-snapshot scans.** 8.2× slower than (c) at 10 versions/key (W3). Mitigations: GC at the watermark (B-3) and version skipping (§8.3, A-4e/B-3). **Trigger:** after both mitigations, the NBench OLTP range rows or E9 miss target, and a profile attributes more than 20% of scan CPU to stepping over invisible versions. Then an Opus card re-opens N3.
- **T2: space amplification after GC.** 20.11× in E0. **Trigger:** B-3's W3/W4-shape acceptance (after-GC tree bytes `≤ 1.5×` pre-update, both as reachable pages × `page_size`, §11) fails on the real tree. Then N3 is re-opened.
- **T3: contended writes.** Both arms collapse past 8 threads. This is a shared commit-path or latching bottleneck owned by S-2 and E7, not by N3 or this file.

**Known cost of a single-copy WAL (cards S-1-F3, S-1-F5):**
- **T4: undetectable post-sync WAL loss** (§14, "Post-sync damage").
  - **The cost:** acked commits since the last checkpoint can be lost silently on a device that drops synced writes: a lost tail, or damage the walk stops at, above `wal_ckpt_end`.
  - **2.0 has WAL back-pointers** (I-WAL-CHAIN: `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`; owner decision, PLAN-v3 §10 item 9, and its addendum adding `prev_crc`, both 2026-11-03). They catch a mid-log splice unless the spliced-in record's predecessor is byte-identical at the same position, and they fail it closed, but they catch neither a lost tail nor damage the walk stops at, and they add no recovery (§12.5).
  - **Hardware guidance (for D-9's release notes and the operator docs).** Durability of acked commits since the last checkpoint assumes a device that honours flush/FUA:
    - enterprise SSDs with **power-loss protection**, or consumer drives whose volatile write cache honours flush;
    - not virtual disks or network block devices configured to ignore flushes (for example, a hypervisor `cache=unsafe`).

    On browser OPFS, it is whatever the browser's flush guarantees (E6).
  - **Roadmap: opt-in synchronous replication for server mode** (PLAN-v3 §10 item 9). An `on` commit is acked only once a replica on another machine also holds its record. This is the real durability guarantee against a device that lies, because it gives both detection and repair. It builds on D-6's WAL-streaming replicas, which carry the chain (`prev_lsn`, `prev_crc`) through. A second WAL copy on the same device barely helps, because the same device can lie for both.
  - **Other mitigations, not in 2.0:** per-batch `durable_end` witnesses with a forward scan past the stop (detection only); more frequent checkpoints (a narrower window).
  - **Two generations change one case** (card S-ALPHA). When a device drops the sync that covered both copies of the newest meta, the open selects the previous meta, whose image and WAL span are intact as I-TWO-GEN states, and serves the state its WAL reaches: acked commits lost with the dropped WAL bytes stay lost without detection, as above, where draft 7.10 often refused instead, because the older image's slots had been reused. The owner adopted this with the header design (PLAN-v3 §10 item 16; `header-durability-design-eval.md` §7, "Outcome under F2").
  - **Trigger:** a field report on a target that drops synced writes, a splice XX001 reported from the field, or server mode's synchronous-replication card.

**Known cost of root names (cards S-1-F7, S-1-F7-F2):**
- **T5: no root name kept across operations.** §6.8's root names let no thread keep a tree's root LPID across operations: every operation names its root through the root table and validates it under the root's latch.
  - **The cost.** One root-table read and one re-read per operation, in place of draft 7.5's two-latch directory descent (about +240 ns per point get, and 1.46× at 4 threads to 2.08× at 16, on E0's tree, verdict S-1-F7). No ordinary operation latches the directory, so that global cache line leaves the hot path, provided a root-name read stores to no memory common to all trees (§6.8 "The root table"). Each operation still takes its tree's root latch, a read-modify-write on a cache line per tree, so a workload on one hot table keeps E0's scaling cliff; the remedy is the E0/E7 optimistic-latch trigger (PLAN-v3 §3.12), not this rule.
  - **Measurement.** A-5, with E0, measures the table path: the point-op CPU spent naming roots, and multi-tree scaling at 4 to 16 threads. ROOT-TABLE-SHARED-WRITE (a root-name read takes a read guard or a reference count on memory common to all trees) shows there, not in a functional test.
  - **Later.** C-T6 §7.5 keeps a tree from being dropped while a registered snapshot can see it, so a foreground operation could keep a root name for its registered snapshot. That is a later spec edit, opened if A-5's measurement puts the table path above 5% of point-op CPU; background jobs keep the current rule.

**Known cost of the unpacked directory (card S-1-F7-F2-F3):**
- **T6: directory pages are never reclaimed.** No directory page is packed or freed in 2.0 (§6.8 step 3), so the directory's size follows the trees ever created, not the trees that exist.
  - **The cost.** On the order of one leaf page per hundred trees ever created, at 8 KiB pages, kept for the store's life. DROP, TRUNCATE and a table rewrite each retire tree ids (C-T0 v8 §9.3), so a workload that repeats them grows the directory with no growth in live trees. The open's root-table build (§14 step 2) reads every directory leaf, the empty ones included.
  - **Trigger.** A directory that takes more than 1% of the file under a TRUNCATE- or rewrite-heavy workload, or a root-table build that dominates open time. No card measures either, so the trigger fires on a report, as Open 1's does. Then a card defines how directory pages are reclaimed, which must keep I-LATCH and §5.4's per-thread count, the constraints that stop DropTree's last step from packing (§6.8 step 3).

**Known cost of two generations (card S-ALPHA):**
- **T7: one more checkpoint interval of freed pages and WAL.** Image `k−1`'s superseded slots are reused one checkpoint later (I-FREE-SAFE, I-TWO-GEN), and the WAL from meta `k−1`'s start is kept, so the file's high-water mark and the WAL's size grow by about one checkpoint interval's rewritten pages and log. The file never shrinks (Open 1).
  - **Measured only on E4's prototype:** 173.6 slots per checkpoint on average against 198.3 (+14%), 590 at most against 706 (+20%), and 2.92 WAL segments on average against 3.00 (`header-design-verify.md` §2.4), on a tiny store whose numbers do not carry over.
  - **Measurement:** a space run with two generations on and off, on the E1 workloads and a browser-sized configuration, on the real store (PLAN-v3 §9 A-7e).
  - **Trigger:** a measured cost that a browser quota (E6, D-5) cannot carry. Then the owner decides whether browser mode keeps two generations; the fall-back (I-FALLBACK) needs them wherever it is on.

**Known cost of the height bound (card R-1-SPEC):**
- **T8: a tree taller than its pool serves.** An open serves trees of up to `H` levels, which it sets from its pool (R-1-memory §4.5; a read-only open from its evictable part, R-1-memory §5.3). A root split past `H` raises 53200, which poisons the store, since a root split lies inside a gated operation (§5.4, §9.2); a read of a taller tree, written under a larger pool, gets 53200 and poisons nothing, while a gated operation that descends it (a write, recovery's replay of one, a GC sweep step or a drop-walk step) poisons the store (§5.4). At the default budgets `H` is far above any real tree; at the minimum it is `H_min` (R-1-memory §4.6).
  - **The remedy** is a larger budget, which raises `H`; recovery then replays the commit that failed (§14).
  - **Trigger:** a 53200 from the height bound at a budget at least twice its minimum. Then a card considers admission control: fewer threads let into the store as its trees grow, at the cost of a count shared by every storage call (T5's concern).
