## Changelog

- **Draft 7.25** (2026-11-28, card S-NU; one sentence added to §15's stand-in S1, the A-3c gate's final wording (A-3c-F1) on the implementation's ESCALATE 3 ruling, reading (a), carried verbatim by this card's N1; card A-3c's Amendment 1 recorded it as proposed text that lands only through a spec card, and this is that card). No rule of draft 7.24 is weakened: a test-only stand-in gains a sentence, and no invariant, mutant, test or row changes.
  - **S1's clause for A-3c** (§15 "Stand-ins"; §5.6 "When it copies"; §4.4). At A-3c only, while step 2 does not exist (until A-3c-C), the stand-in flip also counts as step 2's map phase having taken, for image `k`, the content of every MapLeaf that is not dirty in the pool at the flip, whether its frame is clean or it has no frame, since that content is then at its current home, so that such a MapLeaf is mutated in epoch `k+1` without an epoch copy, as it would be once the map phase had taken it; a MapLeaf dirty in the pool at the flip still needs the copy at its first mutation in epoch `k+1`, which A-7a builds, and A-3c refuses that mutation; before A-3c-C the stand-in flip opens no image-record list, none existing and nothing appending to one, since every write that appends to it (§13 step 2), a previous-epoch victim's (§5.2), a claim's (§5.6) and step 2's data phase, comes with A-3c-C; and nothing the clause leaves out is read, since no image is written or read before A-3c-C, and until then no MapLeaf home whose epoch is below `e` is freed before step 6 (§4.4). Why it must land: without it draft 7.24 contradicts itself at A-3c, for §15.1 judges I-FREE-SAFE ("full (pending frees)") and I-TWO-GEN's `t_two_gen` row ("S3: step 6's release called as A-7c's checkpoint will call it, with an allocation hook") there, and each needs evictions after a flip of pages whose entries are in a pre-flip MapLeaf, while §5.6 copies a MapLeaf whose content epoch is `e − 1` at its first mutation in epoch `e` unless the map phase has taken that content, and no map phase runs before A-3c-C, and a MapLeaf reaches content epoch `e` only by a copy or by being made in epoch `e`, so no test at A-3c could judge either row. The clause scopes itself by step 2's existence, which its own "(A-3c-C)" states: where step 2 runs, the map phase takes content itself, and MAPLEAF-CLEAN-NOT-COPIED's reload run is judged with the map phase, not under this clause.
  - **Counts.** §15: 49 rows, 300 mutants, 58 tests. §15.1: 180 rows. Nothing is added, removed, renamed or moved: no mutant, test, invariant or §15.1 row. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.18 to 7.24. No invariant is weakened, no mutant or test is removed or renamed, and every mutant of draft 7.24 is judged on the cards it was.
- **Draft 7.24** (2026-11-27, card S-XI; drafted beside card S-2a-1's rework, from its gate's R1 and R2 (`verdicts/S-2a-1.md`, d28161e7) and follow-up S-2a-1-F3). No rule of draft 7.23 is weakened: the sync layer gains a primitive, and a read that §13 step 0 already required gains a mutant.
  - **Timed waits** (§15). The sync layer gains a timed wait, which returns when notified or once its deadline has passed and never times out early. Each build reads one clock for it and for `Env::now`: the platform's monotonic clock with `StdEnv`; the harness's model clock, which only test clock steps move; SimEnv's virtual clock, which, once a card makes SimEnv host the sync layer, moves to the earliest timed waiter when every task is parked, instead of failing the world. No store or C-T0 v8 code meets a deadline by sleeping in slices. New invariant I-TIMED-WAIT: `t_timed_wait` (A-4c0-T; TIMED-EARLY, TIMED-NEVER, TIMED-NOTIFY-LOST) and `t_sim_timed_advance` (A-8a; SIM-TIMED-DEADLOCK).
  - **The checkpoint pair** (§13 step 0, §15 I-WAL-TS-ORDER). Step 0's one-entry read of `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi` gains mutant CKPT-PAIR-SPLIT-READ and test `t_ckpt_pair_one_point`, at A-7c on a stand-in commit path (S3) and at B-1c with B-2a's sequencer; §15.1's preamble names B-2a in the Needs column.

No rule of draft 7.20 is weakened: one recovery path is added, a read-only open's 53200 comes sooner, and the rest are tests and wording.

The gate of a card that runs a test under the harness is shown the exploration, the step budget and the clean build passing every execution, and, for a kill that rests on a sampled exploration, the first failing execution's seed and index, replayed, and the failing share; a kill below the share card A-4c0 states gets a hook-steered form. The cap within which a fixture's search must end to be explored exhaustively is the harness card's. `t_append_section_leaf`'s A-6c bound is its step budget, a count of the sync layer's calls inside the append critical section.
  - **The panic clause on its targets** (§9.2; I-FAIL-STOP; S-THETA-F4). A mutex of the store's that a panicking thread holds is poisoned as unwinding releases it, in every backend of the sync layer, and a thread that then takes it returns the poison's error, making the mark with XX000 first if none is made; a thread that holds no gate and waits for a pending flip is a gate waiter, which §9.2 item 3's broadcast wakes; on wasm32 a trap is item 6's stop for the browser handle, which card D-5a's binding enforces and tests. No invariant is weakened, no mutant or test is removed or renamed, and every other mutant of draft 7.21 is judged on the cards it was.
- **Draft 7.21** (2026-11-21, card S-MU; spec finding SF-C1 of card A-3c-C, R-1-memory §14's Q-MAPLEAF-DIRTY and Q-COPY-PIN, and the spec findings of cards A-3b-0, A-3b, A-3c, A-3c-B, A-3c-C and A-4a that are small and certain). Draft 7.20's map phase could write a stale MapLeaf home into an image; this draft builds image `k`'s MapInners from image refs, answers the two R-1-memory questions, and carries the other findings. No invariant is weakened.
  - **The image map is built from image refs** (§4.1, §5.6 "When it copies", §5.7, §13 step 2; I-CKPT-AT-T, I-MAP-GROW; SF-C1). Every map page has an image ref, the ref with which the newest image names it, kept in memory beside the current map (16 bytes a MapLeaf, R-1-memory §5.2) and set only by the map phase, for a page it writes, and by the open. Step 2 builds each MapInner of image `k` from its children's image refs, never from a level-1 MapInner's current refs. Draft 7.20 built a MapInner "from the page's epoch-`k` content", which at level 1 is its MapLeafs' current refs, while "the map phase never makes a slot a map page's current home": so a MapLeaf that checkpoint `k` rewrote while it was dirty kept a current home holding its older entries, and if it stayed resident and unchanged while checkpoint `k+1` rewrote its MapInner, image `k+1` named that home, and after a crash its LPIDs mapped to their homes from before epoch `k`, with every check passing. A level added after flip `k` could likewise name its old root's page from before checkpoint `k`. A MapInner needs no epoch copy, and a MapLeaf's eviction home, which no image now names, is reusable once a later write moves it, whatever epoch its ref carries (§4.4). Mutants MAP-INNER-FROM-CURRENT and IMAGE-REF-NOT-SET (A-3c, A-7f: `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy`'s new two-checkpoint variant), LEVEL-IMAGE-REF-STALE (A-3c, A-7d: its growth run) and MAPLEAF-HOME-LEAKED (A-3c: the current-home variant's second eviction).
  - **A page's MapLeaf, and an epoch copy's** (§4.1 "Residency", §5.4, §5.6 "Restart"; I-LINK-ATOMIC; R-1-memory §14's Q-MAPLEAF-DIRTY and Q-COPY-PIN). An operation pins the MapLeaf of each clean page it will dirty before its first mutation, reading a cold one in place only with no inner page's exclusive latch held and otherwise restarting, and a restart keeps those pins, which `R_MAP` counts (class (Mp)). An epoch copy holds no MapLeaf pin, so `R_MAP`'s copy term is slack; it is kept. Mutants DIRTY-MAPLEAF-UNDER-P (A-4a: `t_split_reserve_order`'s new dirty variant) and DIRTY-MAPLEAF-PIN-DROPPED (A-4a: `t_alloc_restart_progress`'s new dirty-MapLeaf variant).
  - **Step 2 and the reserve** (§5.6 "Reserve", §13 steps 0 and 2, §15 S1; A-3c-C's SF-C4 to SF-C6). A draw and a claim that find no frame in one pass start again, and neither blocks; the flip, and S1, open the image-record list; step 2's two debug assertions are engine invariant violations (XX000).
  - **Fail-stop** (§9.2, I-FAIL-STOP; A-3c-B's SF-B1 to SF-B3 and SF-B7). A poisoned store still answers R-1-memory §8's report and a test build's seams; a panicking thread broadcasts after its last release; an error of another class raised in a poisoning context is XX000; and a write already past its thread's check of the mark may complete, while no write starts after it.
  - **Definitions and wording** (§3.1, §4.4, §4.5, §5.2, §5.4, §15, §15.1; A-3b's SF-1 to SF-3, A-3c's SF-3 to SF-6, A-4a's SF-2 to SF-4). Mutants POOL-GROW, EVICT-SKIP-DIRTY, INPLACE-EVICT, ESCAPE-OFF, DECODE-PANIC, SPLIT-OFF-BY-ONE, SEP-NOT-UPDATED and ROOT-MOVES are defined; a page's writer sets its header's `epoch`; a growth is a whole number of slots; `t_root_latch_fair`'s reader stream checks that no shared request overtakes the waiting writer; a reference count's atomics need not go through the sync layer; two §15.1 Form cells name S3; FREE-EARLY's form at A-3c is stated.
  - **Counts.** §15: 48 rows, 281 mutants, 55 tests. §15.1: 174 rows. Seven mutants are added, each judged on a Phase A row, and no test, row or invariant. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.18 to 7.20. No invariant is weakened, no mutant or test is removed or renamed, and every mutant of draft 7.20 is judged on the cards it was.
- **Draft 7.20** (2026-11-21, card S-IOTA; the owner's own words of 2026-11-20, QUEUE head and PLAN-v3 §10 item 22: "Yes to: XX000 for engine bugs; 55000 and full refusal for newer-version files; read-only salvage when part of the log is unreadable; …"; S-THETA's owner point OP-1, S-ZETA's OP-1 and OP-2 for newer-version files, and S-ETA's OP-1 with its riders O1 to O12, re-anchored on draft 7.19; S-ETA-F6). The three answers of that sentence that no gated text carried. Its other four, stopping the store on an engine bug found during a read, the count-bounded latch, shuttle as a test-only dependency and the tree-height and file-growth stops, were already the text of cards S-THETA and R-1-SPEC and of the plan.
  - **XX000 for engine bugs** (the error table, §2.2, §9.2, §10.5, §13 step 4; I-FAIL-STOP, I-GCW, I-META-COPIES; R15). A check that only an engine bug can fail is XX000, internal error, where draft 7.19 gave XX001, and it still poisons the store wherever it is raised, a client read included; a panic in store code marks the store with XX000 too. A check of what the device returned stays XX001, the open's refusal of a header record included, though step 4 checks the same domains with XX000; so does a check that the device's data can make a correct engine fail, though a bug can fail it too, such as a boot checkpoint's `gc_w ≤ T`, since `gc_w_used` there still holds what recovery read. Mutants INVARIANT-AS-CORRUPT (A-3c), WRITE-CHECK-AS-CORRUPT (A-7c), REFUSAL-AS-INVARIANT (A-2) and BOOT-GCW-AS-INVARIANT (A-7d, on a new §15.1 row).
  - **55000 and full refusal for newer-version files** (the error table, §2.2, §14 step 1, §14.1, §14.2; I-META-COPIES; R18). An intact page 0, or an intact copy with the file's `creation_uuid`, whose `format_version` is above 1 makes the file one that a later format version has written to, and every open of it fails with 55000, read-only and an explicit rollback included, writing nothing, with no fall-back, salvage or override, and ahead of every XX001 that another copy would give (page 0 is still judged first); draft 7.19 gave XX001. A record of the file whose `format_version` is 1 and that is otherwise not well-formed, or whose `format_version` is 0, is a faulty writer's or a deliberate one, which the owner's words do not cover, and still fails with XX001. `t_meta_copies`' table form gains a page 0 with `format_version` 0. Mutants LATER-FORMAT-AS-CORRUPT (A-2, A-7d), REFUSAL-AS-LATER (A-2), LATER-BY-INEQUALITY (A-2) and LATER-FORMAT-SALVAGED (A-7d).
  - **Read-only salvage when part of the log is unreadable** (§12.5, §14 steps 3 and 4, §14.1, §14.2; I-RO-OPEN, I-WAL-STALE). A read error in pass 1's walk, from meta `k`, or from meta `k−1` in a fall-back, lets a read-only open serve salvage at the read cut, the start of the first record the walk could not read, writing nothing, reporting the read error and the cut, and never reported as a complete open; draft 7.19 refused it with 58030. A read-write open and an explicit rollback still fail with 58030, and a read error that only pass 2 meets fails both opens with 58030, since the next open's pass 1 meets it if it persists (S-ETA-F6). `t_wal_read_error` gains the read-only salvage in its cases (b) and (c), and a case (d), pass 2 alone, with a new §15.1 row. Mutants WAL-READ-ERROR-RO-REFUSES, WAL-READ-ERROR-RO-AS-END and PASS2-READ-ERROR-AS-END (A-7d).
  - **Counts.** §15: 48 rows, 274 mutants, 55 tests. §15.1: 174 rows. Eleven mutants and two §15.1 rows are added, each mutant judged on a Phase A row, and no test or invariant. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.18 and 7.19. No invariant is weakened, no mutant or test is removed or renamed, and every mutant of draft 7.19 is judged on the cards it was.
- **Draft 7.19** (2026-11-20, card R-1-SPEC, with the new `docs/R-1-memory.md` draft 1 and C-T0 draft 8.8; PLAN-v3 §9 row R-1, verdict S-1-F7's N6, verdict S-1-F7-F2-F2-F4b's follow-up F2 (a), card S-DELTA's owner point P-1). The numbers this file left to R-1 are set in R-1-memory and pointed to, and the rules they enter are stated.
  - **`R_MAP`** (§5.4, §5.3; I-POOL-BOUND strengthened). `R_MAP = threads × (max tree height + 2) + R_EPOCH`: one MapLeaf for each page a thread's path term can hold dirty, and one per epoch-copy frame. A MapLeaf whose dirty pages no thread pins is not counted, since a frame acquisition releases it by evicting them, but a read-only open, which evicts no dirty page, counts such MapLeafs with its dirty frames and sets its height bound and `R_EPOCH` from the evictable part that is left (§14.1); §4.1's pins themselves have no constant bound, and `⌈dirty frames / 510⌉` was their least, not their most. §5.3's fairness premise now counts the frames a thread can obtain; draft 7.15's wording, a frame no pin holds for every pin, would have needed `R_MAP = 3 × threads × (max tree height + 2) + R_EPOCH`. Test `t_pool_floor_scattered` (A-3c, and A-4a in full), mutant MAPPIN-KEPT; `t_pool_minimum` computes `R_EPOCH` and `R_MAP` from R-1-memory's values, mutants MIN-RMAP-DENSE and MIN-RESERVE-NO-STEP2.
  - **The height bound** (§5.4; I-POOL-BOUND strengthened). `max tree height` is the open's bound `H`, set from its pool; a descent past it and a root split past it give 53200. Test `t_height_bound` (A-4a, and A-7f in full after a reopen), mutants HEIGHT-SPLIT-UNCHECKED and HEIGHT-DESCENT-UNCHECKED; S3 gains a case; §19 T8.
  - **`R_EPOCH`** (§5.6; I-CKPT-AT-T). R-1-memory §4.3 confirms the Bound, derives one claim per thread, and sets `R_EPOCH ≥ threads + s + 1`. `t_epoch_reserve_two_exhausters` gains a floor variant (A-3c, A-7a), mutants RESERVE-PER-THREAD-ONLY and CLAIM-DEPTH-TWO.
  - **Pointers.** §5.1, §5.7's cursor and pending-free rows, §17 and R26.
  - **Counts.** §15: 48 rows, 263 mutants, 55 tests. §15.1: 172 rows. Two tests and seven mutants are added, each judged on a Phase A row, and no invariant. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.17 and 7.18. No invariant is weakened, no mutant or test is removed or renamed, and every mutant of draft 7.18 is judged on the cards it was.
- **Draft 7.18** (2026-11-20, card S-ZETA, follow-up A-2-0-F1 of verdict A-2-0 (branch `card/v3-A-2-0`: gate 1 at c4b62093, its ruling on the implementer's ESCALATE and on the card's open point 3; gate 2 at b887a6c1, its addition IDENTITY-NO-FV-ALG), the rule re-derived against §2.2, §14 steps 1–4a, §14.1, §14.2, §15 and §15.1):
  - **A header record that its magic and CRC validate but that the open cannot use** (§2.2, §14 step 1; I-META-COPIES). At draft 7.15 an intact copy with the file's `creation_uuid` and another `format_version`, page size or other static field passed as foreign and was ignored, and card A-2-0 built the rule to count only a record that fully decodes, so that a page 0 or a copy with a field out of its domain passed as torn. Either way an open could select an older meta past a later format version's checkpoint, or past a faulty writer's record, and a read-write open would then write over it. Now magic and CRC decide only whether a record exists (it is **intact**), and §2.2 states the domains that decide whether the open may use it (it is **well-formed**): card A-2-0's list, with §14 step 1's `T ≤ ts_ckpt_hi` and `wal_start_lsn ≤ wal_ckpt_end`. An intact page 0 that is not well-formed, and an intact copy with the file's `creation_uuid` that is not well-formed or that differs from the file's identity in another static field, fail the open with XX001 in any place and in both modes, naming the place and the field, before any write and with no fall-back. A foreign copy is still ignored whatever its other fields hold, and a misplaced copy needs the file's identity.
  - **Page 0 and both replica blocks unreadable** (§2.2, §14.2). Slots 1 and 2 cannot then be located, and the open has seen no damage: it fails with 58030, not XX001 (card A-2-0's open point 3, the sub-case its gate left open). Page 0 torn with both replica reads failing stays XX001. An open that the open rule refuses writes nothing, the fence included.
  - **Writers and later versions** (§2.2, §13 step 4). Creation and step 4 check that each record they write is well-formed, so no correct build leaves one. Every later format version keeps each record's first bytes and its CRC where any build can check them, and any change of a layout or a domain raises `format_version`.
  - **Tests and mutants.** `t_meta_copies`' table form (A-2) gains the records the rule refuses, a foreign record of another version, page 0 with a later `format_version` and the unlocated 58030, and judges META-RANGE-SKIP too; its write form (A-7c) gains a forged meta and page 0; its fault form (A-7d) gains a later-format variant, an unlocated variant and a foreign copy of another version. New mutants: MALFORMED-AS-TORN, IDENTITY-NO-FV-ALG, STATICS-AS-FOREIGN, IDENTITY-DOMAIN-SKIP, FOREIGN-MALFORMED-REFUSES and UNLOCATED-AS-CORRUPT (A-2; all but the second and third also A-7d), FENCE-BEFORE-REFUSAL (A-7d) and WRITE-MALFORMED (A-7c). §14.2 gains a row and changes two; §18's R18 records the refinement of PLAN-v3 §3.3's "whose magic and CRC are valid".
  - **Counts.** §15: 48 rows, 256 mutants, 53 tests. §15.1: 168 rows. Eight mutants are added, each judged on a Phase A row, and META-RANGE-SKIP is judged on A-2 as well as A-7d. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.16 and 7.17. No invariant is weakened, and no mutant or test is removed or renamed.
- **Draft 7.17** (2026-11-20, card S-ETA, from QUEUE row A-6b-SF: the spec findings F-1 to F-5 and open points 4 and 5 of card A-6b, re-derived against §2, §12, §13, §14, §14.1, §14.2, §15 and §15.1):
  - **A read error during the walk** (F-1; I-WAL-STALE). Neither §14 step 3 nor §9.2 said what a failing read of a WAL segment does. It is neither a stop nor damage: the open fails with 58030, read-write and read-only alike, an explicit rollback and a fall-back's pass 1 included, and writes nothing more than the open had written when the read failed (nothing, in pass 1 from meta `k`), pass 2's own walk included (§12.5 "Read errors", §14 step 3). Test `t_wal_read_error`; mutant WAL-READ-ERROR-AS-END (A-6b, A-7d).
  - **A record that does not decode** (F-2; I-WAL-STALE). A record that passes every stop check and the chain check but whose body does not decode is detected damage, as a splice is: XX001 before pass 2, writing nothing, and a read-only open's salvage cut at it (§12.5 "Malformed records", §14 step 3a). Pass 1 decodes every record it accepts. Test `t_wal_malformed`; mutant WAL-MALFORMED-AS-END (A-6b, A-7d).
  - **Short segments** (F-3; I-WAL-START, I-WAL-STALE). Draft 7.15's short-segment sentence made any read past a segment file's end-of-file a clean stop, the start segment's included, so a start segment whose length a device lost after its sync ended the walk where its file ended: at a quiescent flip the coverage check passed and the open recovered none of the commits past that point, while step 5 zeroed or removed the intact segments after it. A correct build leaves a short segment only where the walk enters it by a hop, too short for a record header at its first LSN: the writer rule makes a segment's length durable before its first record, and the start segment's before its meta, and nothing shortens a segment file. That is now the only short read that ends the walk cleanly; every other is detected damage (§12.5 "Short segments", §13 "The start segment", §14 steps 3 and 3a). Test `t_wal_short_read`; mutants START-SEG-SHORT-AS-END (on `t_wal_start`), SHORT-READ-AS-END and SHORT-HOP-AS-DAMAGE (A-6b, A-7d).
  - **The segment size** (F-4; I-WAL-STALE). `wal_seg_size` is at least 212, so that a segment holds a record header; creation refuses less than 8 MiB outside the store's tests, C-T6-rehost §4.3's floor for its row-layer ops (its §13.1 N3), with 22023 and before any write; an open of a file whose identity carries less than 212 fails with XX001 (§2.1, §2.3, §12.1). Test `t_wal_seg_size`; mutant SEG-SIZE-UNCHECKED (A-7c, A-7d).
  - **The writer rule** (F-5, and card A-6b's open point 5). SEG-ACK-BEFORE-DURABLE no longer claims that a lost preallocated length loses a synced record: a `sync_data` makes durable the length its data needs. A writer that makes a segment's directory entry durable but not its length now has its store's read-write open refused after a power cut, where it lost nothing before (SEG-LEN-NOT-DURABLE, judged by `t_seg_ack_durable` at A-6b and A-7f). "Durable" in the writer rule means made durable by the writer's own boot, or shown so by a record that its recovery walk accepted in the segment, so a boot's writer makes a segment present at open durable itself (§12.5).
  - **Recycling targets** (card A-6b's open point 4; I-WAL-START). A recycled segment's new first LSN lies above the append position's segment and is held by no file, so no name below the append position is taken again and a recycled start segment stays missing (§12.1). Draft 7.15 did not say where: a build that took the lowest free name would, after both copies of the two newest metas were lost, open a meta whose start segment held another segment's records as a clean stop at a quiescent flip. Mutant RECYCLE-TARGET-LOW, on `t_wal_start` (A-6b, A-7d).
  - **COVER-AT-EOF's test** (found while deriving F-3). `t_wal_loss_below_ckpt_end`'s short-segment variant could not fail under COVER-AT-EOF: a record that runs past an end-of-file lies wholly below or wholly at or above `wal_ckpt_end`, a record boundary, so the end-of-file and the record's start were always on the same side of it. The variant now loses a synced record below `wal_ckpt_end`, a `Chunk` appended after the last commit before the flip so that the check's second condition holds, in a segment that the test then cuts short at or above it (§15 I-WAL-COVER).
  - **Counts.** §15: 48 rows, 248 mutants, 53 tests. §15.1: 168 rows. Four tests and eight mutants are added, each mutant judged on a Phase A row, and no invariant. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at draft 7.16. No invariant is weakened, and no mutant or test is removed or renamed.
- **Draft 7.16** (2026-11-17, card S-THETA, spec findings A-3a-SF (card A-3a's SF-1 to SF-5) and A-4c0-SF (card A-4c0's SF-1 to SF-3), each rule re-derived against §4, §5, §6.8, §9.2, §13, §14 and §15):
  - **The load** (§5.1, §5.4; I-PIN-CURRENT, new). Draft 7.15 did not say what a pin does when another thread is loading the page, or how a load is ordered against a write and an eviction of that page. A build that read the reference and the slot into a frame of its own, and put that frame in the page table afterwards if the page was still absent, could install bytes that a write had superseded meanwhile, and so lose the write. Now the page table holds at most one frame per page, an LPID and a MapLeaf's position keyed apart; a pin that finds no frame puts its own there, as a page being loaded, before it reads the reference; a pin that finds the page being loaded waits for that load, holding no map latch, and never takes a frame whose load failed, and a load ends before its thread restarts or waits for the flip; and a page's current reference changes only while a frame holding the page is in the page table, which every mechanism of draft 7.15 already does.
  - **A freed page's frame** (§4.2). Draft 7.15 left it dirty, so an eviction or step 2 could write it and map its LPID again. Now the free leaves it freed and not dirty: an eviction drops it with no write, or the next allocation of its LPID makes its page in it.
  - **A new MapLeaf** (§4.1, §5.6). Its ref is null until its first write, so a restart that needs it looks for its frame first, as a pin does, and waits for a load of it only after it has released the MapInner's latch.
  - **Engine invariant violations** (§9.2). A failed check that a correct engine never fails is XX001, and poisons the store wherever it is raised, a client read included; a panic in store code poisons it before any latch, pin or the gate is released, or stops the process.
  - **Fair latches and the harness** (§5.3, §15). A frame latch bounds its bypass by a count, never a time, and runs on the store's sync layer, so the interleaving harness runs it; a latch that parks on primitives of its own, `parking_lot`'s among them, does not qualify. §15 defines the harness, a test's exploration, "under every schedule" and the step budget: the five tests that say "under every schedule" or "every schedule finishes" mean every schedule of their stated exploration, exhaustive wherever the fixture's search ends within its cap, and the step budget is a count stated by each test, never a time.
  - **Tests and mutants.** New test `t_pin_load_race` (A-3a on S3, A-3c), failed by LOAD-REF-BEFORE-PUBLISH, LOADING-SERVED, FAILED-LOAD-KEPT, MISS-TWO-FRAMES, CREATE-OVER-LOADING, CREATE-BESIDE-FREED and PAGE-KEY-MERGED (A-3a), EVICT-UNLINK-BEFORE-ENTRY and LOAD-KEPT-ACROSS-RESTART (A-3c), and ROOT-PIN-MAP-FIRST, now judged at A-3a too. FREED-FRAME-WRITTEN (A-4b, I-LPID-EXACT's row test). MAPLEAF-EXISTS-BY-REF (A-4a, A-7f) and MAPLEAF-WAIT-UNDER-INNER (A-4a), on `t_alloc_restart_progress`'s race variant, which now runs three ways. INVARIANT-NOT-POISON and PANIC-UNPOISONED (A-3c), on three new variants of `t_poison_before_unlatch`. `t_root_name_reuse`'s case C names the freed frame.
  - **Counts.** §15: 48 rows, 240 mutants, 49 tests. §15.1: 160 rows. One invariant, one test and fourteen mutants are added, each mutant judged on a Phase A row. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.14 and 7.15. No invariant is weakened, and no mutant or test is removed or renamed.
- **Draft 7.15** (2026-11-16, card S-EPSILON, S-ALPHA-F4 (`verdicts/S-ALPHA.md` at 90264bc5, its follow-up S-ALPHA-F4 and "The owner's points, for the record"), adopting the blocks that card S-DELTA proposed for it, which S-DELTA's first gate verified (`verdicts/S-DELTA.md` at 4d0eb2c0, P-8) and its second confirmed (84954d1f), on the owner's conditional reply of 2026-11-16 (PLAN-v3 §10 item 22)):
  - **A read error under the newest image** (S-ALPHA-F4; I-FALLBACK). At draft 7.14 a persistent read error (58030) on a page that the open reads through image `k`'s references (its map root, a MapLeaf, its free set or its directory in step 2, or a leaf in pass 2) failed every open: the read-write open and a read-only open with 58030, and an explicit rollback with 55000, since its precondition was an integrity failure, although image `k−1` and its WAL span could be intact. Now an explicit rollback naming meta `k` proceeds on such a read error, and a read-only open serves salvage from meta `k−1`, when there is a previous image (§14 step 4a, §14.1, §14.2). The automatic fall-back stays integrity-only: a read error is not evidence of damage, and a transient one must not cost the newest image, so a plain read-write open still fails with 58030 and writes nothing more than the fence and the evictions before it. Test `t_fallback` gains its read-error case; mutant ROLLBACK-REFUSES-READ-ERROR (A-7d).
  - **What the case also needs** (this card's first gate, `verdicts/S-EPSILON.md`). An explicit rollback that proceeds on a read error and finds no previous image fails with 58030, not XX001, and a read error in image `k−1` is 58030 in every open, with no salvage from an older meta (§14 step 4a items 1 and 2). A salvage from meta `k−1` reports the valid end and largest `commit_ts` against meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`, and the read error that stopped image `k`, so that what it lacks against meta `k` under a device that drops syncs is reported (§14.1). §14.1's opening list and I-RO-OPEN name the case among those a read-only open salvages; §2.2's summary and I-FALLBACK's statement say that only the automatic fall-back is integrity-only, and §4.4 that a missing previous image is an error after a read error too; §14.2 gains the case's refusal rows. `t_fallback`'s read-error case faults a slot that only image `k` references, until that slot is rewritten, in step 2 and in pass 2, and once with no previous meta; ROLLBACK-REFUSES-READ-ERROR gains its step-2-only form.
  - **Counts.** §15: 47 rows, 226 mutants, 48 tests. §15.1: 158 rows. One mutant is added, judged on a Phase A row (A-7d). 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at draft 7.14. No invariant is weakened: I-FALLBACK's automatic fall-back is unchanged, and the two opens that now proceed, an explicit rollback and a read-only salvage, each report what they serve. No mutant or test is removed or renamed.
- **Draft 7.14** (2026-11-16, card S-DELTA, the remaining S-STORE follow-ups in one change: S-1-F7-F2-F2-F2 (`verdicts/S-1-F7-F2-F2.md` at d909ebba, and the lock-order review's C6.3), S-1-F7-F2-F3-F2 (`verdicts/S-1-F7-F2-F3.md` at 71ed0ddd), S-ALPHA-F1, -F2 and -F5 (`verdicts/S-ALPHA.md` at 90264bc5, with its two earlier verdicts), S-15-STANDINS-2-F1 (`verdicts/S-15-STANDINS-2.md`), S-15-RELABEL and PL-11-F2 (`verdicts/PL-11-gate.md`), and S-GAMMA-F1 to F7 (`verdicts/S-GAMMA.md` at 9abe5527, c5414fa6 and 60e71ed0); with C-T0 draft 8.7):
  - **The minimum pool size counts every pin** (S-1-F7-F2-F2-F2; I-POOL-BOUND strengthened). §5.4's minimum is `threads × 3 × (max tree height + 2) + R_EPOCH + R_MAP`: per thread the path, the pages a gated operation reserves before its first mutation, and the MapLeafs it pins for the LPIDs it allocates or frees, which a restarted allocation keeps while it holds no latch and waits on the gate. Draft 7.12 counted the path only and put the restart's MapLeaf pins in `R_MAP`, which dirty pages size, so §5.3's deadlock argument rested on a count that did not hold at the pool floor; a split's reserved pages were never counted (E0 measured `2·h + 1` frames for a cascading insert, card X-0-F2). `threads` counts every thread that pins frames, the store's own (the checkpointer, evictors and background jobs) included, and under N10 every task that can hold a pin while it waits. A restart keeps no pin but its cold page and its reservation's, and drops an emptied MapLeaf's pin before it pins another; a retry that holds such a pin restarts rather than pin a MapLeaf it does not hold (§5.6); a chain build or free holds at most two chain pages and their MapLeafs at once (§5.4). Test `t_pool_minimum` (A-3b) and `t_alloc_restart_progress`'s pool-floor variant (A-4a); mutants POOL-MIN-PATH-ONLY and RESTART-KEEPS-EMPTY-MAPLEAF; R25.
  - **What a pin finds** (S-1-F7-F2-F3-F2). A pin looks in the page table first and finds an LPID unmapped only when no frame holds it and its current map entry has slot 0; a never-written page has slot 0 while a frame holds it, and an eviction sets the entry before the frame leaves (§4.1, §5.2, §5.4). Mutant ROOT-PIN-MAP-FIRST, through `t_root_name_reuse`'s new case F. Cases C and E make `r` non-resident, so that their pins find it unmapped, and A names the root outside any gated operation; a walk-poison variant (A-7d) has the real drop walk meet an unmapped root under an unchanged `dropping` entry and poison the store (mutant WALK-XX001-NO-POISON, I-FAIL-STOP). §9.2's XX001 covers an unmapped reference. §19 T6's trigger fires on a report.
  - **Meta places an open could not read** (S-ALPHA-F1; I-META-COPIES strengthened). A read-write open zeroes every meta place whose read failed, and syncs, before its first write (§14 step 1, the fence). Without it a briefly unreadable meta could later open over slots the open had reused, and a boot that appended no records could leave a stale copy beside its rewritten `ckpt_id` with an equal `boot_id`: three faults gave an XX001 that stranded the store, read-only included. `t_meta_copies` gains two fence variants, and its tie variant now loses the fence's writes too. Mutant FENCE-SKIP. A fenced copy of the meta the open selected leaves that meta one copy until its parity's next checkpoint (§14 step 1, Residual).
  - **The previous image when its free set cannot be read** (S-ALPHA-F2; I-TWO-GEN strengthened). An open whose read of meta `k−1`'s free set fails protects every slot below meta `k−1`'s `slot_count` that image `k` does not reference, where draft 7.12 protected nothing, so a later open that falls back finds image `k−1` as it was; I-FREE-SAFE's exception shrinks to an open with no previous meta. A read error (58030) on that read is handled as an integrity failure is, except that where image `k` fails at open the open fails with 58030 rather than XX001, since a later open may read the free set and fall back (§14 step 2, step 4a item 1). `t_two_gen` gains (c), whose crash, like (b)'s now, comes before the boot checkpoint's step 4, so that the next open selects meta `k` again; `t_fallback`'s unread variant gains its 58030 form; and §4.4's list of slots that may not be written names the protection (item 4). Mutant UNREAD-PROTECTS-NOTHING.
  - **A misplaced copy one `ckpt_id` above the newest valid meta** (S-ALPHA-F5; I-META-SLOT). It fails no open: it shows only that the next checkpoint reached its step 4, and image `k` and its WAL span stay intact until step 6 of checkpoint `k + 2` (I-TWO-GEN), so the open selects meta `k`, as when both copies of the newer meta are lost. One two or more above still fails the open with XX001. A misdirected in-flight copy and a crash no longer refuse an intact store. Mutants MISPLACED-NEXT-REFUSES and MISPLACED-IGNORED; `t_meta_copies` gains its in-flight variant.
  - **Stand-ins tidies** (S-15-STANDINS-2-F1): S1's rule covers a stale value that a read in the running store returns and the oracle catches, so `t_latch_scope`'s map variant is judged in full on S1; S2's second judgement applies where §15.1 names a full form; I-GCW's B-3 row names the check its parenthesis meant; `t_wal_loss_salvage`'s B-6 row needs B-4a and A-4e; the A-7d LSN-CHECK-SKIP row needs A-7c; `t_wal_chain_crash`'s A-7d form names the step that crosses a hop, which PREV-HOP-WRONG fails.
  - **WAL-ERR-NOT-POISON at A-7c** (PL-11-F2 (a), S-15-STANDINS-F3 (f)): §15.1 judges it at A-7c, the first card whose Depends closure holds both the group-commit writer (A-6c) and the poison mark (A-3c), through the writer's waits and a later read outside the writer, so it leaves the Phase-B-only list. At A-6c, which the plan's A-6c row names, a writer that keeps its own error would pass the mutant.
  - **A-7f** (S-15-RELABEL, PL-11-F2 (b)): the ten rows the plan moved from A-7d are relabelled, the two it split are split, each moved row needs A-7d, and the owner parentheses that named A-7d for those forms name A-7f.
  - **S-GAMMA's follow-ups**, its gates' blocks byte for byte (S-GAMMA-F1 to F7): `t_flip_chunk_race`'s variant *ended* and mutant CHUNK-SET-KEEPS-ENDED (A-7d), so that a transaction that ends with no marker leaves the open-chunk set, and S3 names that end as a test-only caller's (S-GAMMA-F1); the leaf rule names what a thread may hold when it enters the append critical section (§12.4; S-GAMMA-F2); B-6's second AS OF in (d) and (e) is taken at the op's `w` (S-GAMMA-F3); (a) checks the normal-operation floor exactly at A-7c, with mutant DROP-MARK-AT-PUBLISHED-W (S-GAMMA-F4); §12.6 names the two transactions that do not count, an aborted one and one that committed with no ts (S-GAMMA-F5); C-T0 draft 8.7's §3.2 says that a materialisation's `Chunk` reservation is not a gated operation and points to §12.4 (S-GAMMA-F6); and §12.4 now also says that the store's append takes the I-WAL-TS-ORDER critical section for no `SegmentEnd` of a `Chunk`'s hop, and only for a `Commit` or `CommitMarker` and its hop (S-GAMMA-F7).
  - **Counts.** §15: 47 rows, 225 mutants, 48 tests. §15.1: 158 rows. Ten mutants are added, two of them S-GAMMA's follow-ups', each judged on a Phase A row. 24 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards: draft 7.13's 25, less WAL-ERR-NOT-POISON. No mutant or test is removed or renamed, and no invariant is weakened: I-META-SLOT refuses wherever image `k` may have been freed, as before, and no longer where it is intact.
- **Draft 7.13** (2026-11-15, card S-GAMMA, from S-BETA's gates: S-BETA-F1 and S-BETA-F2 (`verdicts/S-BETA.md` at 5cb70935), S-BETA-F3 (at 5f92447f), and the orchestrator's call S-BETA-O1 of 2026-11-15 (QUEUE)):
  - **A chunked transaction's first `Chunk` raced the flip** (S-BETA-F1 (a); I-REDO-COMPLETE strengthened). Nothing said when step 0 reads §12.6's set of first chunks, and nothing kept a `Chunk`'s reservation out of step 0, so on a build that reserved a first `Chunk` with no gate held, between that read and the read of the append position, the `Chunk` could lie below `wal_start_lsn` uncounted, and a crash after its transaction committed and was acked replayed the transaction without that chunk, silently: a loss of an acknowledged commit present since draft 7.8. A build that reserved every `Chunk`, and recorded a transaction's first one, inside a gated operation could not lose it, since step 0 holds the gate exclusively; §12.4 now states that a reservation is not a gated operation, and that the store's append takes the I-WAL-TS-ORDER critical section for no `Chunk`. The append critical section now keeps the open-chunk set, a first `Chunk` enters it in the entry that reserves it, and step 0 reads it with the append position and the chain head in one entry and takes both of §12.6's terms there (§12.4, §12.6, §13 step 0). Mutant START-LSN-SPLIT-READ; test `t_flip_chunk_race` (A-7d). WAL-START-PAIR-SPLIT-READ and `t_wal_chain_crash`'s racing step now say how each order of the split fails once the set is read with the position: the chain head read after them pairs a racing first `Chunk` with itself, and read before them it goes stale across a first `Chunk`'s hop whose `SegmentEnd` is an entry of its own. The racing hooks of both tests reserve with no gate held. A transaction leaves the set as it ends with no marker, or at the first flip whose `T` is at or above its marker, and the set is empty at open (§12.6).
  - **The append critical section is a leaf lock** (S-BETA-F1 (b); I-LATCH): no lock, I/O or wait under it, and a reservation that hops does nothing about the next segment, which the writer makes durable outside the section before its first write there (§12.4). Mutant APPEND-SECTION-IO; test `t_append_section_leaf` (A-6c, A-7c).
  - **The drop's watermark in its WAL record** (S-BETA-O1; I-GCW). The `DropTree` op carries `w`, the watermark the drop-cleanup job read when it found the drop allowed, 8 bytes (§12.3), and a logged DropTree's mark raises `gc_w_used` to it in normal operation and in replay alike (§6.8 step 1, §10.5, §14 step 4), in place of draft 7.12's commit ts minus one in replay; so a crash that replays a drop no longer refuses AS OF snapshots in `[w, c − 1)`. `t_drop_restart_floor` checks the replayed value exactly, with `w < c − 1`, and gains a codec form at A-6a. Mutants REPLAY-DROP-AT-TS and DROP-OP-W-ZERO. S3, §15, §15.1 and §17 follow, and C-T0 draft 8.6 says the same in §9.1.
  - **S-BETA-F3**, the second gate's riders R1–R7, byte for byte: `t_drop_restart_floor`'s fixture keeps the page-local prune off, its variants (b) and (c) name their checkpoint, and its AS OF snapshots are taken before `W` is first published above the floor or with a non-zero retention window (§10.5, B-6's row); `t_wal_trunc_durable`'s first run is skipped on a writer that leaves it no cut point (I-BOOT-DURABLE); I-WAL-CHAIN's row names the predecessor's final `crc`; I-GCW's B-3 row says `gc_w_used`; C-T0 gains W-FLOOR-META-ONLY.
  - **S-BETA-F2**, wording: §2.2's pair for the append position is the chain head; §14 step 3a no longer says that the splice cut keeps neither history, since an overwrite of two or more records that chains to the walk's earlier records leaves overwriting records below the cut, which salvage serves as the walk read them (§14.1); `t_wal_splice`'s same-start crossing overwrites one record; START-SEG-AS-END names both copies of every newer meta. The cut does not change.
  - **§18:** R24.
  - **Counts.** §15: 47 rows, 215 mutants, 47 tests. §15.1: 152 rows. Four mutants are added, each judged on a Phase A row. 25 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.10 to 7.12. No invariant is weakened, and no mutant or test is removed or renamed.
- **Draft 7.12** (2026-11-15, card S-BETA, P0 review 2's confirmed defects S-1-F6 (card S-1-F5 D5; `verdicts/S-1-F5.md` (7)), S-1-F5-F1 (`verdicts/S-1-F5.md`, Follow-ups) and S-1-F4 (card S-1-F3's Notes; `verdicts/S-1-F3-draft-gate.md` O-1)):
  - **Durable truncation and the recovered `durable_end`** (S-1-F6; new invariant I-BOOT-DURABLE):
    - §14 step 5 truncates durably, before step 6: the rest of the valid end's segment is zeroed, and every later segment is zeroed or removed, then synced (with `wal/` after a removal). Without it, a meta rollback (both copies of a meta lost, or a fall-back from it) with ordinary power cuts brings a same-id boot's records back past the truncation (§12.5, Residual, which now names both rollbacks), whose premise this was. I-WAL-START (§12.5) admits the removals: they never take the start segment of the meta an open recovers from.
    - A recovered boot's `durable_end` starts at the walk's start (§12.4 "After recovery"), so the boot checkpoint's step 1 syncs every byte the walk accepted (§13 step 1). Without it, a process crash, then both copies of the boot meta lost and a power cut, lose acked commits silently; with a fall-back in place of the lost meta, the proof fails and a recoverable store is refused. Class C alone cannot reach this: no walk reads below the boot meta's `wal_start_lsn` without a rollback. E4 does not certify it: its SimEnv resolves every unsynced write at each crash.
    - Tests `t_wal_trunc_durable` and `t_boot_durable_start`, with its fall-back variant (A-7d). Mutants TRUNC-NOT-DURABLE and BOOT-DURABLE-AT-VALID-END. I-REDO-COMPLETE (§14) and the Residual (§12.5) say what they now rest on.
  - **The start pair, the chain head, sealing and splice salvage** (S-1-F5-F1):
    - Every reservation is one entry to the append critical section, which moves the append position and the chain head together (§12.4). Step 0 reads the position, the chain head and `wal_ckpt_end` in one entry, fixes which record the pair names, and takes its `crc` once final (§13 step 0). Mutant WAL-START-PAIR-SPLIT-READ.
    - After recovery the chain head is the recovery's, so a boot checkpoint records it (§12.2, rather than §2.2). Mutant BOOT-PAIR-UNSET.
    - A batch is sealed with the chain head the previous batch left, with a non-normative note on folding at reservation (§12.2). Mutant SEAL-STALE-HEAD.
    - `t_wal_chain_crash` gains a flip that races a `Chunk` reservation and two batches in flight, and a form at A-6c.
    - Splice salvage cuts pass 1's marker set, largest `commit_ts`, coverage and step 3b's dependency check back to the splice cut (§14.1, §14 step 3a). Mutants SPLICE-MARKER-PAST-CUT and SPLICE-CUT-SAME-START-KEEP. `t_wal_splice`'s read-only variant runs crossed with the same-start variant and with a marker past the cut.
    - Post-sync damage's "at or above" row makes step 5 plainly the non-splice case. §12.5 states the chain's transitivity.
  - **The restart floor across drops** (S-1-F4; I-GCW strengthened):
    - A DropTree's mark raises `gc_w_used` to the published `W` (§6.8 step 1), and a replayed mark raises it to its commit ts minus one (§14 step 4). After open the floor is `gc_w_used` as recovery leaves it (§10.5, §14, §14.1, §17). Without this, an AS OF snapshot below a retiring DDL is admitted after a restart and names a tree that is `dropping` or gone.
    - Test `t_drop_restart_floor` (A-7b, A-7c, A-7d; full form B-6). Mutants DROP-NO-GCW, DROP-GCW-AT-WALK, DROP-GCW-AT-TS and REPLAY-DROP-NO-GCW; GCW-NOT-RECORDED is judged again at A-7d. S3 gains the published watermark.
    - C-T0 draft 8.5, in the same card, states the floor in §3.7 step 3, §9.1 and I-W-FLOOR's row.
  - **S-ALPHA-F6** (`verdicts/S-ALPHA.md`, 90264bc5), wording only: I-FALLBACK's "never two generations from the meta the open selects" (§15); `t_meta_copies`' tie variant (§2.2); step 2 reads meta `k−1`'s free set even after a read of image `k` failed in it (§14 step 2); `t_fallback`'s unread variant corrupts the bytes of its read and sits in the proven case (§14 step 4a); four citations of the previous image read "as I-TWO-GEN states" (§1, §14, §19 T4); a fault on the one surviving copy of the newest meta (§2.2, §14); the two states outside I-TWO-GEN's condition (§4.4); the unproven fixture's range (§14 step 4a). No behaviour, name, mutant or §15.1 row changes.
  - **§18:** R23.
  - **Counts.** §15: 47 rows, 211 mutants, 45 tests. §15.1: 148 rows. Eleven mutants are added, each judged on a Phase A row. 25 mutants are judged only on Phase B cards, as at drafts 7.10 and 7.11. No invariant is weakened, and no mutant or test is removed or renamed.
- **Draft 7.11** (2026-11-15, card S-ALPHA, from the owner's header decision of 2026-11-10 (PLAN-v3 §10 item 16; QUEUE S-HDR-1), with the spec side of card E4-SILENT-1, and from verdict S-1-F7-F2-F2's follow-up S-1-F7-F2-F2-F1 (d909ebba)):
  - **Two places** (§2.1, §2.2, new §2.4, §13 step 4; I-META-COPIES, I-META-SLOT).
    - Every meta is written twice: its primary copy to slot `1 + (k mod 2)`, and its replica to a 4096-byte block at a fixed offset, 1 MiB for even `ckpt_id`s and 1 MiB + 4 KiB for odd ones, both before step 5's sync.
    - The copies differ in a `copy` byte, and each carries page 0's static fields and `creation_uuid`, so an open needs no page 0 and ignores a foreign copy.
    - The open selects among four places by one table. Each parity's valid copy with the higher `ckpt_id` is its candidate; a tie of one `ckpt_id` with other bytes goes to the newer `boot_id`, and equal ones are XX001; a misplaced copy, one in a place of the other parity, fails the open only if no valid copy has an equal or higher `ckpt_id`.
    - The slots under the replica region are reserved, and a file is created at least as long as the region.
  - **Two generations** (§4.4, §12.6, §13 step 6, §14 step 2; I-TWO-GEN, I-FREE-SAFE). Step 6 releases image `k−2`'s superseded slots and recycles below meta `k−1`'s start, one checkpoint later than at draft 7.10, so image `k−1` and its WAL span survive until meta `k+1` is durable. An open protects the previous image's slots from its free set. I-FREE-SAFE now says meta `k+1`.
  - **Fall-back** (§14 step 4a, new §14.2, §14.1; I-FALLBACK). §2.2's "no fallback" is replaced. A newest image that fails an integrity check at open falls back to the previous meta, with every check against it, and automatically only with proof that the walk reaches the failed meta's `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`; a read-write fall-back first zeroes both copies of the abandoned meta. Unproven, the read-write open fails with XX001, a read-only open serves salvage, and an explicit rollback naming the abandoned meta opens it read-write (55000 when its precondition does not hold). §14 step 1's range check keeps its "no fallback".
  - **The two silent-loss fixes** (`header-design-verify.md` §1.1–§1.5; card E4-SILENT-1).
    - A publishing commit carries `RequireCkpt` with the forced checkpoint's `ckpt_id`, and recovery refuses to replay it over an older image (§12.3, §12.8, §14 step 3b; I-CKPT-DEP).
    - Segment 0 is made at file creation, step 1 makes every meta's start segment exist, recycling runs lowest first with each rename durable, and a missing start segment is XX001, never an empty WAL (§2.3, §12.1, §12.5, §13 steps 1 and 6, §14 step 3; I-WAL-START).
    - I-REDO-COMPLETE's "I-WAL-COVER after recycling" did not cover an older meta at a quiescent flip, and an intact image and WAL span did not cover a load: both are corrected. §14's post-sync damage list gains the meta and image cases.
  - **Map growth** (§3.1, §4.1, §5.6, §5.7, §13 steps 0 and 2; I-MAP-GROW). The root is always a MapInner, and the meta records its level. A new level is made under the current root's exclusive latch, and a MapInner under its parent's, each only if still missing, in the allocation restart's own gated operation. Positions are counted from the leaf level, and image `k` is built up to the root step 0 recorded. A test build may lower the fanout. S1 records the map's root and level too.
  - **§15 and §15.1.** Six invariants; six tests (`t_meta_copies`, `t_replica_region`, `t_two_gen`, `t_fallback`, `t_ckpt_dep`, `t_wal_start`) and new variants of `t_alloc_restart_progress` and `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy`; thirty-one mutants, each judged on a Phase A row; nineteen §15.1 rows. S3 gains the publishing commit, the growth call and step 6's release. META-WRONG-SLOT is judged at A-2 as well, and RO-WRITES covers a retire write. §15.1's intro adds that every mutant added since draft 7.10 is judged on a Phase A row.
  - §18 gains R18–R22. §19 gains T7 (two generations' space) and a T4 note on the dropped-sync case. The error table gains 55000.
  - **The byte layout changes:** the meta record (`copy`, page 0's static fields, `map_level`), the replica blocks, the `RequireCkpt` op, and map pages' `id`. No invariant is weakened: I-FREE-SAFE and I-REDO-COMPLETE are stated more strictly, I-META-SLOT and I-RO-OPEN cover the new places and refusals, and no existing mutant or test is removed or renamed.
  - **Gate corrections** (`verdicts/S-ALPHA.md`, REWORK): pass-2 evictions made before image `k` fails, and the retire write after them; I-FREE-SAFE's exception for an open with no previous image; no fall-back unless step 2 read the previous free set; §11's reuse one checkpoint later; the fixtures that roll a meta back now lose both copies; the chained fall-back after two lost metas, stated; the retire write zeroes a misplaced copy first; the identity rule's order; and the test texts that a correct build could not pass, or whose mutant could not fail. A second gate (REWORK on 41795fba): I-META-COPIES and I-TWO-GEN stated with their conditions, the explicit rollback's 55000 after a pass 2 that evicted, and two more test texts that a correct build could not pass (`t_fallback`'s unread variant and the placement of its unproven case's WAL loss).
